📚 本期文献进展 / Literature Brief

  • 本期SARS-CoV-2研究集中呈现以下进展。
  • 40例中29例胎盘中检测到严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 RNA表达,主要定位于合体滋养层,从感染到分娩最长间隔为221天;在两个 COVID (+) 新生儿胎盘的绒毛核心血管内皮中也检测到病毒 RNA 表达。
  • 轻、中、重度病例中rs11568821等位基因频率和rs2227982基因型及等位基因频率存在显着差异;然而,这些关联都不是独立的,也没有发现单倍型与疾病严重程度或结果之间存在关联。 [10.1002/jmv.71090]。
  • Omicron 变体受到已知可激活 TLR9 的小 DNA 寡核苷酸配体的独特限制,并且不同序列的短 DNA 寡核苷酸可在 TMPRSS2 介导的激活下游的进入步骤中阻断 Omicron 感染,其活性已被证实与 TLR9 无关。
  • 我们发现 NP 对 SEBFF 变化的敏感性因区域而异,突变 对结构化 RNA 结合域 (RBD) 和二聚化域 (DD) 更有可能导致 SEBFF 减少。我们表明,连接区中的 R203K 突变 增加了 NP 的折叠适合度,这表明热稳定性可能是推动 α、γ 和 omicron 谱系中出现的 R203K/G204R 突变 组采用的因素之一。
  • This reporting window highlights the following research developments.
  • The COVID-19 pandemic was associated with an unprecedented global reduction in influenza incidence, with a decline of −92% (95% CI: −94 to −90). [10.1111/irv.70301].
  • No association was found between anti‐RBD/neutralizing antibodies and ABO or Rh(D) type 4 weeks post‐booster; AB blood group had significantly lower breakthrough infection rates (HR 0.67 [0.50–0.90]; p = 0.008) compared to O, with a trend for group B (HR 0.82 [0.66–1.01]; p = 0.06); no associations were observed with Rh(D) or COVID‐19 severity.
  • SARS-CoV-2 RNA expression was detected in placental disk, predominantly localized in syncytiotrophoblasts in 29 out of the 40 cases, with the longest interval from infection to delivery being 221 days; viral RNA expression was also detected in villous core vessel endothelium in two COVID (+) newborn placentas.
  • Significant differences were observed in allele frequencies of rs11568821 and genotypes and allele frequencies of rs2227982 among mild, moderate, and severe cases; however, none of these associations remained independent, and no association was found between haplotypes and disease severity or outcome.

📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Influenza and Other Respiratory Viruses  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-01  |  DOI: 10.1111/irv.70300  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 20, 8, e70300
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

Impact of ABO Blood System on Immunogenicity and Vaccine Efficacy of COVID-19 Booster Vaccination-A Population-Based Study of 3066 Individuals.

Impact of ABO Blood System on Immunogenicity and Vaccine Efficacy of COVID-19 Booster Vaccination-A Population-Based Study of 3066 Individuals.
作者： Daniela Sieghart, Galateja Jordakieva, Caroline Dürrschmid, Claudia A Hana, Lan Vi Tran, Leonhard X. Heinz, Helmuth Haslacher, Markus Zlesak, Valentin Marzoner, Philip Nasztl
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
ABO 血型与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 易感性相关,但关于其对 疫苗接种 加强剂后突破性感染和抗体反应的影响的数据很少。
设计与对象
这项基于人群的多中心 队列研究 研究包括接受 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 加强剂 队列研究 的 18 岁以上个体,在多变量回归模型中对 3066 名参与者(平均年龄 49 [35-59],62% 女性)进行了分析。
核心方法
在基线、4周后和6个月后评估了针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体结合域(RBD)的抗体水平以及针对野生型和Omicron变体的中和抗体水平;使用时间依赖性 Cox 回归分析来评估突破性感染率。
主要结果
No association was found between anti‐RBD/neutralizing antibodies and ABO or Rh(D) type 4 weeks post‐booster; AB blood group had significantly lower breakthrough infection rates (HR 0.67 [0.50–0.90]; p = 0.008) compared to O, with a trend for group B (HR 0.82 [0.66–1.01]; p = 0.06); no associations were observed with Rh(D) or COVID‐19 severity.
解释与创新
The authors conclude that ABO or Rh(D) blood group was not associated with COVID‐19 severity or antibody responses, but differences in ABO group appear relevant for Omicron transmission, despite not supporting previously discussed protective effects of anti‐A/B/Rh(D) agglutinins.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,ABO 血型可能会影响 Omicron 传播 风险,可能为针对性的 疫苗接种 策略或 监测 方法提供信息,但没有观察到对 疫苗 诱导的免疫力或疾病严重程度的影响。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于人群的设计和大样本量提供了中等的证据强度,但其局限性包括依赖自我报告的血型以及模型中未完全调整的潜在残留混杂因素。

Impact of ABO Blood System on Immunogenicity and Vaccine Efficacy of COVID-19 Booster Vaccination-A Population-Based Study of 3066 Individuals.

Authors: Daniela Sieghart, Galateja Jordakieva, Caroline Dürrschmid, Claudia A Hana, Lan Vi Tran, Leonhard X. Heinz, Helmuth Haslacher, Markus Zlesak, Valentin Marzoner, Philip Nasztl
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background ABO blood group has been associated with SARS‐CoV‐2 susceptibility. Little data exist regarding the impact of ABO and Rhesus (Rh[D]) type on breakthrough infections and antibody responses following SARS‐CoV‐2 booster vaccination. Methods This multicenter, population‐based cohort study includes individuals ≥ 18 years who received a booster vaccination against SARS‐CoV‐2. Antibody levels against SARS‐CoV‐2 receptor binding domain (RBD) and neutralizing antibodies against wild‐type (WT) virus and Omicron variant were assessed at baseline, after 4 weeks and after 6 months. At 6 months follow‐up, self‐reported ABO and Rh(D) type, time to, and severity of breakthrough infections were collected. Results In total, 3066 participants (mean age 49 [35–59], 62% female) were included in multivariable regression models, showing no association of anti‐RBD and neutralizing antibodies against wild‐type and Omicron variant with ABO or Rh(D) 4 weeks after booster vaccination. Time‐dependent Cox regression analysis showed significantly lower rates of breakthrough infections in patients with AB (hazard ratio [HR] 0.67 [0.50–0.90]; p = 0.008) compared to O, even after adjustment for relevant covariates and a trend for blood group B (HR 0.82 [0.66–1.01]; p = 0.06) compared to O; there were no associations between Rh(D) and breakthrough infection rates or between observed (asymptomatic to moderate) COVID‐19 symptom severity and ABO or Rh(D) blood group. Conclusions ABO or Rh(D) blood group was not associated with COVID‐19 severity or antibody responses following SARS‐CoV‐2 vaccination. Breakthrough infections were least common in blood groups AB and B. Although our data do not support previously discussed protective effects of anti‐A/B/Rh(D) agglutinins, differences in ABO group appear relevant for Omicron transmission.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
ABO blood group has been associated with SARS‐CoV‐2 susceptibility, but little data exist regarding its impact on breakthrough infections and antibody responses following booster vaccination.
Design and population
This multicenter, population‐based cohort study included individuals ≥ 18 years who received a SARS‐CoV‐2 booster vaccination, with 3066 participants (mean age 49 [35–59], 62% female) analyzed in multivariable regression models.
Core methods
Antibody levels against SARS‐CoV‐2 receptor binding domain (RBD) and neutralizing antibodies against wild‐type and Omicron variant were assessed at baseline, after 4 weeks, and after 6 months; time‐dependent Cox regression analysis was used to evaluate breakthrough infection rates.
Main results
No association was found between anti‐RBD/neutralizing antibodies and ABO or Rh(D) type 4 weeks post‐booster; AB blood group had significantly lower breakthrough infection rates (HR 0.67 [0.50–0.90]; p = 0.008) compared to O, with a trend for group B (HR 0.82 [0.66–1.01]; p = 0.06); no associations were observed with Rh(D) or COVID‐19 severity.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors conclude that ABO or Rh(D) blood group was not associated with COVID‐19 severity or antibody responses, but differences in ABO group appear relevant for Omicron transmission, despite not supporting previously discussed protective effects of anti‐A/B/Rh(D) agglutinins.
Scientific and public-health significance
Findings suggest ABO blood group may influence Omicron transmission risk, potentially informing targeted vaccination strategies or surveillance approaches, though no impact on vaccine-induced immunity or disease severity was observed.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's population-based design and large sample size provide moderate evidence strength, though limitations include reliance on self-reported blood types and potential residual confounding factors not fully adjusted in models.

Effect of probiotics on humoral responses to COVID-19 vaccination in older adults: a randomized, placebo-controlled trial (PIRATES-COV study).

Effect of probiotics on humoral responses to COVID-19 vaccination in older adults: a randomized, placebo-controlled trial (PIRATES-COV study).
作者： Jean‐Charles Pasquier, Nils Chaillet, Mélanie Plourde, Guy Boivin, Marie-Ève Hamelin, Hugues Allard-Chamard, Alain Piché, Geneviève Baron, Isabelle Laforest-Lapointe, Mélissa Généreux
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Although COVID-19 vaccination has reduced morbidity and mortality, adults aged 65 y old and older are at high risk of complications from COVID-19. Few studies have evaluated the potential benefit of probiotic supplementation in this context in this high-risk population.
设计与对象
这项研究是一项随机对照试验,招募了居住在加拿大魁北克省的 65 岁至 89 岁成年人,他们接受了 mRNA 增强剂(辉瑞 BioNTech 或 Moderna)。参与者在基线、疫苗接种 后 3 个月和 6 个月时提供干血斑样本。
核心方法
疫苗接种 治疗前 15 天至治疗后 15 天期间,与安慰剂相比,补充益生菌(鼠李糖乳杆菌 Rosell®-11 和副干酪乳杆菌 Rosell®-215,每粒胶囊 1x6x10^9 CFU)。主要结果是在 疫苗接种 后 6 个月时未检测到抗 S1 受体结合域(抗 S1-RBD)抗体的参与者比例。
主要结果
592 adults were enrolled. At 6 months post-vaccination, anti-S1-RBD antibodies were comparable between groups, and the percentage of participants with undetectable antibody response in the placebo and probiotic groups (1.2% vs 1.6%) was comparable. Anti-N seropositivity was lower in the probiotic group at 6 months (OR 0.6, 95% CI 0.4-0.9; p = 0.02) and marginally lower at 3 months after adjustment.
解释与创新
Probiotic supplementation administered around COVID-19 vaccination did not change the proportion of seronegative participants for anti-S1-RBD antibodies at 6 months. However, in the probiotic group, anti-N antibody levels were lower, while self-reported COVID-19 infections also tended to be reduced, although this difference was not statistically significant.
科研与公卫意义
The findings suggest that probiotic supplementation may not enhance humoral responses to COVID-19 vaccination in older adults, but may reduce anti-N antibody levels and potentially lower COVID-19 infection incidence, warranting further investigation.
局限与证据强度
由于随机对照设计和大样本量,证据强度中等,但该研究受到抗 N 抗体水平降低的推测性解释和未经病毒学确认的自我报告感染数据的限制。

Effect of probiotics on humoral responses to COVID-19 vaccination in older adults: a randomized, placebo-controlled trial (PIRATES-COV study).

Authors: Jean‐Charles Pasquier, Nils Chaillet, Mélanie Plourde, Guy Boivin, Marie-Ève Hamelin, Hugues Allard-Chamard, Alain Piché, Geneviève Baron, Isabelle Laforest-Lapointe, Mélissa Généreux
Original abstract (English)
Background Although COVID-19 vaccination has reduced morbidity and mortality, adults aged 65 y old and older are at high risk of complications from COVID-19. The evidence suggests that probiotics may enhance immune responses when co-administered with influenza vaccination. However, few studies have evaluated the potential benefit of probiotic supplementation in this context in this high-risk population. Material This study was a randomized controlled trial recruiting adults between 65 and 89 y of age living in Quebec, Canada, who received an mRNA booster (Pfizer-BioNTech or Moderna). Probiotic supplementation ( Lacticaseibacillus rhamnosus Rosell ® -11 and Lacticaseibacillus paracasei Rosell ® -215, 1x 6 x 10 9 CFU per capsule) versus placebo was administered from 15 d pre- to 15 d post-vaccination. Participants provided dried blood spot samples at baseline, at 3- and 6-month post-vaccination. The primary outcome was the proportion of participants without detectable anti-S1-receptor binding domain (anti-S1-RBD) antibodies at 6 months post-vaccination. Secondary outcomes included longitudinal anti-S1-RBD and anti-nucleocapsid (anti- N ) antibody responses across the three timepoints. Results 592 adults were enrolled. At 6 months post-vaccination, anti-S1-RBD antibodies were comparable between groups, and the percentage of participants with undetectable antibody response in the placebo and probiotic groups (1.2% vs 1.6%) was comparable. Anti- N seropositivity was lower in the probiotic group at 6 months (OR 0.6, 95% CI 0.4-0.9; p = 0.02) and marginally lower at 3 months after adjustment. Self-reported COVID-19 infection incidence was 16.5% overall (19.1% placebo vs 13.9% probiotic; p = 0.134). Conclusion Probiotic supplementation administered around COVID-19 vaccination did not change the proportion of seronegative participants for anti-S1-RBD antibodies at 6 months. However, in the probiotic group, anti- N antibody levels were lower, while self-reported COVID-19 infections also tended to be reduced, although this difference was not statistically significant. They may be consistent with earlier control of infection and reduced exposure to the nucleocapsid antigen, but this interpretation remains speculative. Trial registration number #NCT05195151.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Although COVID-19 vaccination has reduced morbidity and mortality, adults aged 65 y old and older are at high risk of complications from COVID-19. Few studies have evaluated the potential benefit of probiotic supplementation in this context in this high-risk population.
Design and population
This study was a randomized controlled trial recruiting adults between 65 and 89 y of age living in Quebec, Canada, who received an mRNA booster (Pfizer-BioNTech or Moderna). Participants provided dried blood spot samples at baseline, at 3- and 6-month post-vaccination.
Core methods
Probiotic supplementation (Lacticaseibacillus rhamnosus Rosell®-11 and Lacticaseibacillus paracasei Rosell®-215, 1x6x10^9 CFU per capsule) versus placebo was administered from 15 d pre- to 15 d post-vaccination. The primary outcome was the proportion of participants without detectable anti-S1-receptor binding domain (anti-S1-RBD) antibodies at 6 months post-vaccination.
Main results
592 adults were enrolled. At 6 months post-vaccination, anti-S1-RBD antibodies were comparable between groups, and the percentage of participants with undetectable antibody response in the placebo and probiotic groups (1.2% vs 1.6%) was comparable. Anti-N seropositivity was lower in the probiotic group at 6 months (OR 0.6, 95% CI 0.4-0.9; p = 0.02) and marginally lower at 3 months after adjustment.
Interpretation and novelty
Probiotic supplementation administered around COVID-19 vaccination did not change the proportion of seronegative participants for anti-S1-RBD antibodies at 6 months. However, in the probiotic group, anti-N antibody levels were lower, while self-reported COVID-19 infections also tended to be reduced, although this difference was not statistically significant.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that probiotic supplementation may not enhance humoral responses to COVID-19 vaccination in older adults, but may reduce anti-N antibody levels and potentially lower COVID-19 infection incidence, warranting further investigation.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the randomized controlled design and large sample size, but the study is limited by the speculative interpretation of reduced anti-N antibody levels and self-reported infection data without virological confirmation.

Journal: PLOS Pathogens  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-07-30  |  DOI: 10.1371/journal.ppat.1014461  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 22, 7, e1014461

DNA 寡核苷酸可阻止 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 变体的病毒进入

DNA oligonucleotides block viral entry of SARS-CoV-2 Omicron variants
作者： Jorge A. Acuña, Benjamin Gabriel, Kasirajan Ayyanathan, Jesse Miller, Yue Li, Kanupriya Whig, Yongqing Zhu, Marisa McGrath, Peter Hewins, Kellie Ann Jurado
摘要中文翻译包括 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 在内的成功病毒可以拮抗并逃避免疫检测以建立感染。随着人群对祖先菌株的免疫力增强,新的变种(尤其是 Omicron)已经进化,以避免被循环抗体和先天免疫系统检测到。通过筛选先天免疫激动剂,我们之前发现 STING 激动剂是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 祖先变体的有效抑制剂。当我们探索 Omicron BA.1 对多种先天刺激的敏感性时,我们发现 Omicron 对干扰素和 STING 激动剂也敏感;然而,Omicron 变体受到已知可激活 TLR9 的小 DNA 寡核苷酸配体的独特限制。机制研究表明,不同序列的短而非长 DNA 寡核苷酸(包括那些不参与 TLR9 的寡核苷酸)可在 TMPRSS2 介导的激活下游的进入步骤中阻断 Omicron 感染。通过遗传和药理学测定,我们证实活性与 TLR9 无关。此外,这些寡脱氧核苷酸 (ODN) 在体内具有活性,可降低 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 滴度。总之,这些发现确定了短 DNA 寡核苷酸先前未被识别的 抗病毒药物 活性,并将其确立为 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 进入的选择性抑制剂。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
包括 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 在内的成功病毒可以拮抗并逃避免疫检测来建立感染,并且随着人群对祖先毒株的免疫力增强,新的变种(尤其是 Omicron)已经进化,可以避免被循环抗体和先天免疫系统检测到。
设计与对象
该研究探讨了 Omicron BA.1 对多种先天刺激的敏感性,并研究了 DNA 寡核苷酸针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 变体的 抗病毒药物 活性。
核心方法
使用遗传和药理学测定进行机制研究,以确定短和长 DNA 寡核苷酸对 Omicron 感染的影响,并进行体内实验以评估 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 滴度的降低。
主要结果
Omicron 变体受到已知可激活 TLR9 的小 DNA 寡核苷酸配体的独特限制,并且不同序列的短 DNA 寡核苷酸可在 TMPRSS2 介导的激活下游的进入步骤中阻断 Omicron 感染,其活性已被证实与 TLR9 无关。
解释与创新
这些发现鉴定了短 DNA 寡核苷酸先前未被识别的 抗病毒药物 活性,并将其确立为 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 进入的选择性抑制剂。
科研与公卫意义
这些寡脱氧核苷酸 (ODN) 在体内具有活性,可降低 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Omicron 滴度,这表明针对 Omicron 变体的潜在治疗应用。
局限与证据强度
该研究通过机械和体内验证证明了高证据强度,但对所有 Omicron 亚变体的普遍性和长期影响需要进一步研究。

DNA oligonucleotides block viral entry of SARS-CoV-2 Omicron variants

Authors: Jorge A. Acuña, Benjamin Gabriel, Kasirajan Ayyanathan, Jesse Miller, Yue Li, Kanupriya Whig, Yongqing Zhu, Marisa McGrath, Peter Hewins, Kellie Ann Jurado
Original abstract (English)
Successful viruses including SARS-CoV-2 antagonize and evade immune detection to establish infection. As population immunity to the ancestral strain has risen, new variants—particularly Omicron—have evolved to avoid detection by both circulating antibodies and the innate immune system. Screening innate immune agonists, we previously identified STING agonists as potent inhibitors of the ancestral variant of SARS-CoV-2. When we explored the sensitivity of Omicron BA.1 to diverse innate stimuli, we found that Omicron is also sensitive to interferons and STING agonists; however, Omicron variants are uniquely restricted by small DNA oligonucleotide ligands known to activate TLR9. Mechanistic studies showed that short, but not long, DNA oligonucleotides of diverse sequences, including those that do not engage TLR9, block Omicron infection at the entry step downstream of TMPRSS2-mediated activation. Using genetic and pharmacological assays we confirmed that activity was independent of TLR9. Furthermore, these oligodeoxynucleotides (ODNs) are active in vivo , reducing SARS-CoV-2 Omicron titers. Together, these findings identify a previously unrecognized antiviral activity of short DNA oligonucleotides and establish them as selective inhibitors of SARS-CoV-2 Omicron entry.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Successful viruses including SARS-CoV-2 antagonize and evade immune detection to establish infection, and as population immunity to the ancestral strain has risen, new variants—particularly Omicron—have evolved to avoid detection by both circulating antibodies and the innate immune system.
Design and population
The study explored the sensitivity of Omicron BA.1 to diverse innate stimuli and investigated the antiviral activity of DNA oligonucleotides against SARS-CoV-2 Omicron variants.
Core methods
Mechanistic studies were conducted using genetic and pharmacological assays to determine the effect of short and long DNA oligonucleotides on Omicron infection, and in vivo experiments were performed to assess the reduction of SARS-CoV-2 Omicron titers.
Main results
Omicron variants are uniquely restricted by small DNA oligonucleotide ligands known to activate TLR9, and short DNA oligonucleotides of diverse sequences block Omicron infection at the entry step downstream of TMPRSS2-mediated activation, with activity confirmed to be independent of TLR9.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings identify a previously unrecognized antiviral activity of short DNA oligonucleotides and establish them as selective inhibitors of SARS-CoV-2 Omicron entry.
Scientific and public-health significance
These oligodeoxynucleotides (ODNs) are active in vivo, reducing SARS-CoV-2 Omicron titers, which suggests potential therapeutic applications against Omicron variants.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study demonstrates high evidence strength with mechanistic and in vivo validation, though the generalizability to all Omicron subvariants and long-term effects requires further investigation.

Effectiveness of BNT162B2 and CoronaVac vaccines in reducing COVID-19 severity among children aged 3-4 years in Brazil.

Effectiveness of BNT162B2 and CoronaVac vaccines in reducing COVID-19 severity among children aged 3-4 years in Brazil.
作者： Marina Coelho de Padua, Marcos Otávio Brum Antunes, Renato T. Stein, Leonardo A. Pinto, Marcus H. Jones, Frederico Friedrich, Scotta MC, Marcelo Comerlato Scottá
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
National population-based databases from large countries can provide real-world estimates of COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness. This analysis examined the effectiveness of BNT162b2 and CoronaVac against severe COVID-19-related outcomes in children aged 3 to 4 years during the Omicron phase of the pandemic in Brazil.
设计与对象
This nationwide population-based cohort study assessed incidence rate ratios for hospitalization due to COVID-19-associated severe acute respiratory syndrome, invasive ventilatory support, and death among children aged 3 to 4 years, according to vaccination status (unvaccinated, 1 dose, or 2 doses), during 2022-2023.
核心方法
Data on laboratory-confirmed COVID-19-related hospitalizations, clinical outcomes, and vaccination status were obtained from OpenDATASUS, and vaccination coverage was estimated using data from the Brazilian Ministry of Health COVID-19 vaccination dashboard.
主要结果
The study period spanned epidemiological weeks 31-52 of 2022 and weeks 1-13 of 2023. Among children aged 3 to 4 years, completion of the primary series increased to 15% for CoronaVac and 2% for BNT162b2 during the study period, corresponding to 797,956 and 79,650 individuals receiving the second dose, respectively. A total of 963 children were hospitalized with COVID-19-associated severe acute respiratory syndrome.
解释与创新
COVID-19 vaccination, predominantly based on the CoronaVac vaccine, was associated with a significant reduction in severe outcomes among children aged 3-4 years, providing robust evidence to support pediatric immunization strategies and public health efforts.
科研与公卫意义
Vaccine effectiveness against hospitalization was 79.0% (95% CI, 69.9%-85.4%) after partial vaccination and 49.2% (95% CI, 32.5%-61.9%) after completion of the primary series (p < 0.01). Effectiveness against invasive mechanical ventilation was 77.6% and 79.1%, respectively; no deaths occurred among fully vaccinated children.
局限与证据强度
由于 BNT162b2 接受者比例较低,因此 疫苗 之间的直接比较不可行。由于观察设计和潜在的混杂因素,证据强度中等,尽管基于人群的大量样本提高了可靠性。

Effectiveness of BNT162B2 and CoronaVac vaccines in reducing COVID-19 severity among children aged 3-4 years in Brazil.

Authors: Marina Coelho de Padua, Marcos Otávio Brum Antunes, Renato T. Stein, Leonardo A. Pinto, Marcus H. Jones, Frederico Friedrich, Scotta MC, Marcelo Comerlato Scottá
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: National population-based databases from large countries can provide real-world estimates of COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness. This analysis examined the effectiveness of BNT162b2 and CoronaVac against severe COVID-19-related outcomes in children aged 3 to 4 years during the Omicron phase of the pandemic in Brazil. METHODS: This nationwide population-based cohort study assessed incidence rate ratios for hospitalization due to COVID-19-associated severe acute respiratory syndrome, invasive ventilatory support, and death among children aged 3 to 4 years, according to vaccination status (unvaccinated, 1 dose, or 2 doses), during 2022-2023. The study period spanned epidemiological weeks 31-52 of 2022 and weeks 1-13 of 2023. Data on laboratory-confirmed COVID-19-related hospitalizations, clinical outcomes, and vaccination status were obtained from OpenDATASUS, and vaccination coverage was estimated using data from the Brazilian Ministry of Health COVID-19 vaccination dashboard. RESULTS: Among children aged 3 to 4 years completion of the primary series increased to 15% for CoronaVac and 2% for BNT162b2 during the study period, corresponding to 797,956 and 79,650 individuals receiving the second dose, respectively. Epidemiology and mortality among hospital-acquired cases of COVID-19 in the pre-Omicron and Omicron periods: A data-linkage study in Victoria, Australia.

Epidemiology and mortality among hospital-acquired cases of COVID-19 in the pre-Omicron and Omicron periods: A data-linkage study in Victoria, Australia.
作者： Lyn-Li Lim, Stephanie K Tanamas, Suresh Karunappan, Leon J Worth, Ann Bull, Elizabeth J Robinson, N Deborah Friedman
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study addressed the public-health problem of hospital-acquired COVID-19 by asking why in-hospital infections carry higher risks than community-acquired cases and whether this changed between pre-Omicron and Omicron periods.
设计与对象
A retrospective cohort study was conducted in Victoria, Australia, linking state surveillance systems to identify COVID-19 hospitalisations from January 2021 to June 2024, classifying episodes as hospital-acquired if the first positive test occurred at least eight days after admission.
核心方法
使用关联的 监测 数据,对 Omicron 之前和 Omicron 期间医院获得性和非医院获得性病例的年龄、性别、重症监护病房入院情况和 30 天 死亡率 进行比较。
主要结果
在 6,485 例医院获得性发作和 62,670 例非医院获得性发作中,中位年龄分别为 77.8 岁和 Omicron 之前的 77.6 岁,Omicron 期间分别为 52.6 岁和 73.1 岁;Omicron 下每月医院获得性病例上升至 21%,医院获得性病例的病死率较高,Omicron 前的 发病率 比率为 4.49,Omicron 期间为 1.62。
解释与创新
The authors concluded that hospital-acquired COVID-19 remains clinically significant, disproportionately affecting older patients and carrying higher mortality than non-hospital-acquired infection, highlighting the need for strengthened infection prevention.
科研与公卫意义
The findings inform hospital infection prevention strategies by quantifying the excess mortality risk of hospital-acquired COVID-19 and showing its persistence into the Omicron era.
局限与证据强度
该研究的回顾性设计和对 监测 数据的依赖限制了因果推断和普遍性,因此对其结论有一定的信心。

Epidemiology and mortality among hospital-acquired cases of COVID-19 in the pre-Omicron and Omicron periods: A data-linkage study in Victoria, Australia.

Authors: Lyn-Li Lim, Stephanie K Tanamas, Suresh Karunappan, Leon J Worth, Ann Bull, Elizabeth J Robinson, N Deborah Friedman
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVE: The aim of this study was to compare the epidemiology and mortality of hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections among hospitalised patients. METHODS: We conducted a retrospective cohort study of COVID‐19 hospitalisations in Victoria, Australia (January 2021-June 2024), using linked state surveillance systems. Episodes were classified as hospital acquired if the first positive test occurred ≥8 days post admission. Age, sex, intensive care unit admission and 30‐day mortality were compared between hospital-acquired and non‐hospital-acquired cases across pre‐Omicron and Omicron periods. RESULTS: Of 6,485 hospital-acquired and 62,670 non-hospital-acquired episodes, median age for hospital-acquired versus non-hospital-acquired cases was 77.8 versus 77.6 years before Omicron and 52.6 versus 73.1 years during Omicron. Monthly proportion of hospital-acquired cases increased during Omicron (range: 1% to 21%). Case fatality rates were higher in hospital-acquired than in non-hospital-acquired cases, with a larger difference before the Omicron (incidence rate ratio: 4.49, 95% confidence interval: 2.02, 9.99) than during the Omicron period (incidence rate ratio: 1.62, 95% confidence interval: 1.44, 1.82). Intensive care unit admission was associated with 30-day mortality for hospital-acquired (adjusted odds ratio: 4.4, 95% confidence interval: 2.3-8.4) and non‐hospital-acquired cases (adjusted odds ratio: 4.0, 95% confidence interval: 3.5-4.7). CONCLUSIONS: Hospital-acquired COVID-19 infections remain significant, associated with higher mortality rates than non-hospital-acquired infection and disproportionately affecting older patients. IMPLICATIONS FOR PUBLIC HEALTH: Our findings underscore the need for robust hospital COVID-19 infection prevention strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed the public-health problem of hospital-acquired COVID-19 by asking why in-hospital infections carry higher risks than community-acquired cases and whether this changed between pre-Omicron and Omicron periods.
Design and population
A retrospective cohort study was conducted in Victoria, Australia, linking state surveillance systems to identify COVID-19 hospitalisations from January 2021 to June 2024, classifying episodes as hospital-acquired if the first positive test occurred at least eight days after admission.
Core methods
Age, sex, intensive care unit admission and 30-day mortality were compared between hospital-acquired and non-hospital-acquired cases across pre-Omicron and Omicron periods using linked surveillance data.
Main results
Among 6,485 hospital-acquired and 62,670 non-hospital-acquired episodes, median age was 77.8 versus 77.6 years before Omicron and 52.6 versus 73.1 years during Omicron; monthly hospital-acquired cases rose to 21% under Omicron, and case fatality rates were higher for hospital-acquired cases with incidence rate ratios of 4.49 pre-Omicron and 1.62 during Omicron.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that hospital-acquired COVID-19 remains clinically significant, disproportionately affecting older patients and carrying higher mortality than non-hospital-acquired infection, highlighting the need for strengthened infection prevention.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform hospital infection prevention strategies by quantifying the excess mortality risk of hospital-acquired COVID-19 and showing its persistence into the Omicron era.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study’s retrospective design and reliance on surveillance data limit causal inference and generalisability, warranting moderate confidence in its conclusions.

针对神秘 RBD 表位的 ACE2 融合纳米抗体可广泛中和 SARS 样病毒。

ACE2-fused nanobody targeting a cryptic RBD epitope broadly neutralizes SARS-like viruses.
作者： Weihong Zeng, Guanying Zhang, Huan Ma, Lijun Xiong, Mingshi Xu, Shanshan Wang, Xiaoxue Huang, Shi Chen, Xiangyang Chi, Sandra Chiu
摘要中文翻译摘要 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 (SARS2) 和 SARS-CoV (SARS1) 病毒由于其高 突变 率和跨物种 传播 的潜力而构成重大威胁,这使得许多抗体无效。为了应对未来可能出现的新型SARS样病毒,必须开发具有广谱中和能力的纳米抗体。在这里,我们在羊驼中采用了交替免疫策略,使用 SARS1 和 SARS2 RBD 来产生交叉反应纳米抗体。鉴定出四种交叉结合纳米抗体(aSR29、aSR196、aSR347 和 aSR348),对 SARS1/SARS2 RBD 和 Omicron 亚变体表现出高亲和力。结构分析显示,aSR29 靶向 RBD 内部碱基的一个隐秘、高度保守的表位,该区域通过空间位阻与传统抗体隔离,有效中和 SARS1、SARS2 和多种关注变体 (VOC),包括 XBB.1.5 和 BQ.1.1。基于这种独特的结合模式,我们设计了一种双特异性融合蛋白,aSR29-ACE2-Fc。该构建体将高亲和力 RBD 结合与病毒附着的空间阻断协同结合,使针对 SARS2 的中和效力提高了 33 倍,并增强了抵抗免疫逃逸的能力。我们的研究结果验证了引发广谱纳米抗体的有效策略,并证明了 VHH-ACE2 融合蛋白针对当前和未来的 SARS 样病毒的治疗潜力。重要性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的持续 进化 已使大多数现有抗体失效,凸显了针对当前和未来 SARS 类病毒的广谱对策的迫切需要。在这里,我们开发了一种交替免疫策略来生成针对病毒 刺突蛋白 上隐藏的高度保守区域的纳米抗体。aSR29 就是此类纳米抗体之一,可有效中和多种变体,包括 XBB.1.5 和 BQ.1.1。通过将 aSR29 与病毒受体 ACE2 融合,我们创建了一种双特异性分子,可以通过两种独立的机制阻止病毒进入,从而使中和效力提高 33 倍。这项工作为大流行防范提供了一种实用且可扩展的策略,证明工程纳米抗体-ACE2融合可以作为针对新出现的类SARS病毒的有效广谱疗法。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决开发针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 和相关病毒的广谱中和剂的挑战,因为它们的高 突变 率和跨物种 传播 潜力,这损害了许多抗体的有效性。
设计与对象
该研究涉及使用 SARS1 和 SARS2 RBD 来产生交叉反应纳米抗体的羊驼交替免疫策略。鉴定出四种纳米抗体对 SARS1/SARS2 RBD 和 Omicron 亚变体表现出高亲和力。
核心方法
研究人员在羊驼身上采用了交替免疫策略,以产生针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 和 SARS-CoV-1 RBD 的纳米抗体。然后,他们进行了结构分析,以确定纳米抗体靶向的表位,并设计了一种将纳米抗体与 ACE2 结合的双特异性融合蛋白。
主要结果
结构分析表明,aSR29 靶向 RBD 内部碱基的一个隐秘、高度保守的表位,该表位通过空间位阻与常规抗体隔离。这种纳米抗体可有效中和 SARS1、SARS2 和多种相关变体 (VOC),包括 XBB.1.5 和 BQ.1.1。
解释与创新
作者将他们的发现解释为展示了一种新策略,可以引发针对病毒 刺突蛋白 隐藏区域的广谱纳米抗体,为对抗当前和未来的 SARS 类病毒提供了潜在的解决方案。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,工程纳米抗体-ACE2融合体可以作为针对新兴SARS样病毒的有效广谱疗法,为大流行防范提供实用且可扩展的策略。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅基于抽象证据,这可能会限制分析的深度。然而,报告的结果以结构分析为基础,并展示了明确的、针对特定目标的行动机制。

ACE2-fused nanobody targeting a cryptic RBD epitope broadly neutralizes SARS-like viruses.

Authors: Weihong Zeng, Guanying Zhang, Huan Ma, Lijun Xiong, Mingshi Xu, Shanshan Wang, Xiaoxue Huang, Shi Chen, Xiangyang Chi, Sandra Chiu
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT The SARS-CoV-2 (SARS2) and SARS-CoV (SARS1) viruses pose significant threats due to their high mutation rates and potential for cross-species transmission, which has rendered many antibodies ineffective. To counter potential future emergences of novel SARS-like viruses, it is imperative to develop nanobodies with broad-spectrum neutralizing capacity. Here, we employed an alternating immunization strategy in alpacas using SARS1 and SARS2 RBDs to generate cross-reactive nanobodies. Four crossing-binding nanobodies (aSR29, aSR196, aSR347, and aSR348) were identified, exhibiting high affinities for SARS1/SARS2 RBDs and Omicron subvariants. Structural analysis revealed that aSR29 targets a cryptic, highly conserved epitope at the inner base of the RBD, a region shielded from conventional antibodies by steric hindrance, efficiently neutralizing SARS1, SARS2, and multiple Variants of Concern (VOCs), including XBB.1.5 and BQ.1.1. Building on this unique binding mode, we engineered a bispecific fusion protein, aSR29-ACE2-Fc. This construct synergistically combines high-affinity RBD binding with the steric blockade of viral attachment, resulting in a 33-fold increase in neutralization potency against SARS2 and enhanced resilience against immune escape. Our findings validate an efficient strategy for eliciting broad-spectrum nanobodies and demonstrate the therapeutic potential of VHH-ACE2 fusion proteins against current and future SARS-like viruses. IMPORTANCE The continued evolution of SARS-CoV-2 has rendered most existing antibodies ineffective, highlighting the urgent need for broad-spectrum countermeasures against current and future SARS-like viruses. Here, we developed an alternating immunization strategy to generate nanobodies that target a hidden, highly conserved region on the viral spike protein. One such nanobody, aSR29, potently neutralizes diverse variants, including XBB.1.5 and BQ.1.1. By fusing aSR29 with the viral receptor ACE2, we created a bispecific molecule that blocks viral entry through two independent mechanisms, achieving a 33-fold increase in neutralization potency. This work provides a practical and scalable strategy for pandemic preparedness, demonstrating that engineered nanobody–ACE2 fusions can serve as effective broad-spectrum therapeutics against emerging SARS-like viruses.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the challenge of developing broad-spectrum neutralizing agents against SARS-CoV-2 and related viruses due to their high mutation rates and cross-species transmission potential, which has compromised the effectiveness of many antibodies.
Design and population
The study involved an alternating immunization strategy in alpacas using SARS1 and SARS2 RBDs to generate cross-reactive nanobodies. Four nanobodies were identified that showed high affinities for SARS1/SARS2 RBDs and Omicron subvariants.
Core methods
The researchers employed an alternating immunization strategy in alpacas to generate nanobodies targeting the RBD of SARS-CoV-2 and SARS-CoV-1. They then performed structural analysis to identify the epitope targeted by the nanobody and engineered a bispecific fusion protein combining the nanobody with ACE2.
Main results
Structural analysis revealed that aSR29 targets a cryptic, highly conserved epitope at the inner base of the RBD, which is shielded from conventional antibodies by steric hindrance. This nanobody efficiently neutralizes SARS1, SARS2, and multiple Variants of Concern (VOCs), including XBB.1.5 and BQ.1.1.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted their findings as demonstrating a novel strategy for eliciting broad-spectrum nanobodies that target a hidden region of the viral spike protein, offering a potential solution for combating current and future SARS-like viruses.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that engineered nanobody–ACE2 fusions could serve as effective broad-spectrum therapeutics against emerging SARS-like viruses, providing a practical and scalable strategy for pandemic preparedness.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on abstract-only evidence, which may limit the depth of analysis. However, the reported results are grounded in structural analysis and demonstrate a clear, target-specific mechanism of action.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 核衣壳的深度突变扫描揭示了区域稳定性模式。

Deep mutational scanning of SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid reveals regional stability patterns.
作者： Alasdair D Keith, Meredith M Keen, Filipp Frank, Eric A Ortlund
摘要中文翻译严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 核衣壳蛋白 (NP) 内的点 突变 不仅有可能影响检测,还可能影响病毒生命周期,从而影响致病性。利用深度突变扫描 (DMS),我们确定所有可能点 突变 的 7876 个点 (98.9%) 对 NP 表面表达的影响作为稳定性的代理,我们将其称为基于表面表达的折叠适应度 (SEBFF)。我们发现 NP 对 SEBFF 变化的敏感性因区域而异,突变 对结构化 RNA 结合域 (RBD) 和二聚化域 (DD) 更有可能导致 SEBFF 减少。我们表明,连接区中的 R203K 突变 增加了 NP 的折叠适合度,这表明热稳定性可能是推动 α、γ 和 omicron 谱系中出现的 R203K/G204R 突变 组采用的因素之一。我们的 DMS 结果似乎与文献中可获得的 NP 变体的有限重组蛋白和生物物理数据一致。我们测试了三种计算方法,以确定是否有任何方法可以准确预测 DMS 数据;每个变体的预测很差,但每个位点的预测都很好,机器学习方法优于基于物理的策略,并且 ThermoMPNN-D 与 RBD 和 ProteinMPNN 与 DD 配合使用时表现最有效。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 核衣壳蛋白 (NP) 内的点 突变 不仅有可能影响检测,还可能影响病毒生命周期,从而影响致病性。
设计与对象
利用深度突变扫描 (DMS),我们确定所有可能点 突变 的 7876 个点 (98.9%) 对 NP 表面表达的影响作为稳定性的代理,我们将其称为基于表面表达的折叠适应度 (SEBFF)。
核心方法
我们测试了三种计算方法,以确定是否有任何方法可以准确预测 DMS 数据;每个变体的预测很差,但每个位点的预测都很好,机器学习方法优于基于物理的策略,并且 ThermoMPNN-D 与 RBD 和 ProteinMPNN 与 DD 配合使用时表现最有效。
主要结果
我们发现 NP 对 SEBFF 变化的敏感性因区域而异,突变 对结构化 RNA 结合域 (RBD) 和二聚化域 (DD) 更有可能导致 SEBFF 减少。我们表明,连接区中的 R203K 突变 增加了 NP 的折叠适合度,这表明热稳定性可能是推动 α、γ 和 omicron 谱系中出现的 R203K/G204R 突变 组采用的因素之一。
解释与创新
我们的 DMS 结果似乎与文献中可获得的 NP 变体的有限重组蛋白和生物物理数据一致。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现提供了对 突变 在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 核衣壳蛋白中的功能影响的见解,这可能有助于 监测 策略和对病毒 进化 的理解。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外深度突变扫描和计算预测,没有在病毒复制系统中进行直接验证。由于突变覆盖全面,但缺乏体内证实,证据强度中等。

Deep mutational scanning of SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid reveals regional stability patterns.

Authors: Alasdair D Keith, Meredith M Keen, Filipp Frank, Eric A Ortlund
Original abstract (English)
Point mutations within the SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein (NP) have the potential to impact not only detection but the viral life cycle, and therefore pathogenicity. Leveraging deep mutational scanning (DMS) we determine the effect of 7876 (98.9%) of all possible point mutations on NP surface expression as a proxy for stability, which we term surface-expression-based folding fitness (SEBFF). We show NP susceptibility to SEBFF changes varies with region, with mutations to the structured RNA-binding domain (RBD) and dimerization domain (DD) being more likely to cause decreases in SEBFF. We show that the R203K mutation in the linker region increases the folding fitness of NP, suggesting thermostability may have been one factor driving the adoption of the R203K/G204R set of mutations arising in the alpha, gamma and omicron lineages. Our DMS results appear consistent with the limited recombinant protein and biophysical data for NP variants available from the literature. We tested three computational methods to determine whether any could accurately predict the DMS data; per-variant predictions were poor, but per-site predictions were good, with machine learning methods outperforming the physics-based strategy, and ThermoMPNN-D performing most effectively with the RBD and ProteinMPNN with the DD.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Point mutations within the SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein (NP) have the potential to impact not only detection but the viral life cycle, and therefore pathogenicity.
Design and population
Leveraging deep mutational scanning (DMS) we determine the effect of 7876 (98.9%) of all possible point mutations on NP surface expression as a proxy for stability, which we term surface-expression-based folding fitness (SEBFF).
Core methods
We tested three computational methods to determine whether any could accurately predict the DMS data; per-variant predictions were poor, but per-site predictions were good, with machine learning methods outperforming the physics-based strategy, and ThermoMPNN-D performing most effectively with the RBD and ProteinMPNN with the DD.
Main results

We show that the R203K mutation in the linker region increases the folding fitness of NP, suggesting thermostability may have been one factor driving the adoption of the R203K/G204R set of mutations arising in the alpha, gamma and omicron lineages.
Interpretation and novelty
Our DMS results appear consistent with the limited recombinant protein and biophysical data for NP variants available from the literature.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide insights into the functional impact of mutations in the SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein, which may inform surveillance strategies and understanding of viral evolution.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro deep mutational scanning and computational predictions, with no direct validation in viral replication systems. The evidence strength is moderate due to the comprehensive mutational coverage but lack of in vivo confirmation.

The Platform trial In COVID-19 priming and BOOsting (PICOBOO): The immunogenicity, reactogenicity and safety of seven licensed COVID-19 vaccines delivered as fifth dose or subsequent boosters in immunocompetent adults.

The Platform trial In COVID-19 priming and BOOsting (PICOBOO): The immunogenicity, reactogenicity and safety of seven licensed COVID-19 vaccines delivered as fifth dose or subsequent boosters in immunocompetent adults.
作者： C M, M. Dymock, K.L. Flanagan, M. Plebanski, H. Marshall, M.J. Estcourt, M.C. Tjiam, C.C. Blyth, K. Subbarao, F.L. Mordant
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
BACKGROUND PICOBOO is a randomised, adaptive trial evaluating the immunogenicity and reactogenicity of licensed COVID-19 booster vaccines in immunocompetent individuals >12 years old.
设计与对象
Participants were recruited from January to November 2023 (T1) and from January to September 2024 (T2). Participants recruited during T1 were randomised to receive one of five COVID-19 booster vaccines. Participants recruited during T2 were randomised to receive one of two monovalent XBB.1.5 booster vaccines.
核心方法
方法 三个月前未接受过加强剂 疫苗 的人符合资格。对所有参与者的抗尖峰免疫球蛋白 G (IgG) 反应进行了评估。在 T1 期间招募的参与者中评估了抗尖峰总 Ig。在预定义的子集中评估中和反应。
主要结果
FINDINGS There were 276 and 260 participants recruited in T1 and T2, respectively. All vaccines boosted humoral immune responses. Immunological responses measures at D28, 180 and 365 found peak total immunoglobulin (Ig), IgG and virus neutralisation (ND50) responses at D28. Responses returned to near-baseline levels by D365. Participants in the 50- < 70y-BNT162b2 stratum had higher total Ig, IgG and ND50 compared to those in the strata primed with AZD1222. The proportion who reported at least one severe event following Nvx, Pf BA.1, Mod BA.1, Pf BA.4/5, Mod BA.4/5, Pf XBB.1.5 and Mod XBB.1.5 were 1.1%, 2.9%, 6.7%, 0%, 5.9%, 2.9% and 5.4%, respectively.
解释与创新
解释 所有 疫苗 均增强体液免疫并具有可接受的反应原性特征。在用 BNT162b2 免疫的人中观察到更高的结合和中和抗体反应。
科研与公卫意义
该研究结果通过证明多个获得许可的 疫苗 的免疫原性和安全性,为加强 疫苗 的选择提供信息,特别强调了 BNT162b2 引发的个体的优异反应。
局限与证据强度
由于其随机设计和多次 疫苗 比较,该研究的证据强度中等,但其局限性包括缺乏 D365 以后的长期随访以及对特定年龄层的潜在普遍性限制。

The Platform trial In COVID-19 priming and BOOsting (PICOBOO): The immunogenicity, reactogenicity and safety of seven licensed COVID-19 vaccines delivered as fifth dose or subsequent boosters in immunocompetent adults.

Authors: C M, M. Dymock, K.L. Flanagan, M. Plebanski, H. Marshall, M.J. Estcourt, M.C. Tjiam, C.C. Blyth, K. Subbarao, F.L. Mordant
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND PICOBOO is a randomised, adaptive trial evaluating the immunogenicity and reactogenicity of licensed COVID-19 booster vaccines in immunocompetent individuals >12 years old. Here, we present data for seven different vaccines among three pre-specified participant strata defined by age and primary vaccine schedule: those primed with AZD1222 and aged 50 - < 70 or 70+ years old and those primed with BNT162b2 aged 50- < 70 years old until day (D) 365. METHODS People who had not received a booster vaccine in the three months prior were eligible. Participants were recruited from January to November 2023 (T1) and from January to September 2024 (T2). Participants recruited during T1 were randomised to receive one of five COVID-19 booster vaccines. Participants recruited during T2 were randomised to receive one of two monovalent XBB.1.5 booster vaccines. Anti-spike Immunoglobulin G (IgG) responses were evaluated in all participants. Anti-spike total Ig was evaluated in participants recruited during T1. Neutralisation responses were evaluated in a pre-defined subset. FINDINGS There were 276 and 260 participants recruited in T1 and T2, respectively. All vaccines boosted humoral immune responses. Immunological responses measures at D28, 180 and 365 found peak total immunoglobulin (Ig), IgG and virus neutralisation (ND50) responses at D28. Responses returned to near-baseline levels by D365. Participants in the 50- < 70y-BNT162b2 stratum had higher total Ig, IgG and ND50 compared to those in the strata primed with AZD1222. The proportion who reported at least one severe event following Nvx, Pf BA.1, Mod BA.1, Pf BA.4/5, Mod BA.4/5, Pf XBB.1.5 and Mod XBB.1.5 were 1.1%, 2.9%, 6.7%, 0%, 5.9%, 2.9% and 5.4%, respectively. INTERPRETATION All vaccines boosted humoral immunity and had acceptable reactogenicity profiles. Higher binding and neutralising antibody responses were observed in people primed with BNT162b2.

Question and background
BACKGROUND PICOBOO is a randomised, adaptive trial evaluating the immunogenicity and reactogenicity of licensed COVID-19 booster vaccines in immunocompetent individuals >12 years old.
Design and population
Participants were recruited from January to November 2023 (T1) and from January to September 2024 (T2). Participants recruited during T1 were randomised to receive one of five COVID-19 booster vaccines. Participants recruited during T2 were randomised to receive one of two monovalent XBB.1.5 booster vaccines.
Core methods
METHODS People who had not received a booster vaccine in the three months prior were eligible. Anti-spike Immunoglobulin G (IgG) responses were evaluated in all participants. Anti-spike total Ig was evaluated in participants recruited during T1. Neutralisation responses were evaluated in a pre-defined subset.
Main results
FINDINGS There were 276 and 260 participants recruited in T1 and T2, respectively. All vaccines boosted humoral immune responses. Immunological responses measures at D28, 180 and 365 found peak total immunoglobulin (Ig), IgG and virus neutralisation (ND50) responses at D28. Responses returned to near-baseline levels by D365. Participants in the 50- < 70y-BNT162b2 stratum had higher total Ig, IgG and ND50 compared to those in the strata primed with AZD1222. Interpretation and novelty
INTERPRETATION All vaccines boosted humoral immunity and had acceptable reactogenicity profiles. Higher binding and neutralising antibody responses were observed in people primed with BNT162b2.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform booster vaccine selection by demonstrating immunogenicity and safety across multiple licensed vaccines, particularly highlighting superior responses in BNT162b2-primed individuals.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study's evidence strength is moderate due to its randomized design and multiple vaccine comparisons, though limitations include the absence of long-term follow-up beyond D365 and potential generalizability constraints to specific age strata.

Investigation of PDCD1 Gene Polymorphisms and Haplotypes in COVID-19 Severity and Outcome in a Brazilian Population.

Investigation of PDCD1 Gene Polymorphisms and Haplotypes in COVID-19 Severity and Outcome in a Brazilian Population.
作者： Sarah Lott Moretto, Cassela PLCS, Guilherme Lerner Trigo, Marcell Alysson Batisti Lozovoy, Zuleica Naomi Tano, Andrea Name Colado Simão, Karen Brajão de Oliveira, Pedro Luis Candido de Souza Cassela
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
COVID-19 severity and survival are influenced by the host immune response to SARS-CoV-2, and since genetic variability can modulate these responses, we investigated whether PDCD1 polymorphisms are associated with COVID-19 severity and mortality in a Brazilian cohort.
设计与对象
A total of 366 COVID-19 patients (165 mild, 72 moderate, and 129 severe cases) were genotyped for four PDCD1 SNPs, and their haplotype structures were estimated.
核心方法
Multinomial logistic regression identified associations between PDCD1 polymorphisms and COVID-19 severity, and haplotype analysis was performed to evaluate differences in frequencies among the groups.
主要结果
轻、中、重度病例中rs11568821等位基因频率和rs2227982基因型及等位基因频率存在显着差异;然而,这些关联都不是独立的,也没有发现单倍型与疾病严重程度或结果之间存在关联。
解释与创新
The authors suggest that these polymorphisms, although involved in COVID-19 immunopathogenesis, are not suitable biomarkers for predicting COVID-19 severity or clinical outcomes, and this is the first study to evaluate SNP rs2227982 in COVID-19 patients and the first to evaluate the four SNPs in a Brazilian population.
科研与公卫意义
The findings inform that PDCD1 polymorphisms are not reliable biomarkers for COVID-19 severity prediction, which may guide future research toward other genetic or immunological factors for risk stratification.
局限与证据强度
尽管样本量和全面的遗传分析为研究结果提供了合理的可信度,但由于缺乏独立关联以及该研究仅限于单个 队列研究,证据强度中等。

Investigation of PDCD1 Gene Polymorphisms and Haplotypes in COVID-19 Severity and Outcome in a Brazilian Population.

Authors: Sarah Lott Moretto, Cassela PLCS, Guilherme Lerner Trigo, Marcell Alysson Batisti Lozovoy, Zuleica Naomi Tano, Andrea Name Colado Simão, Karen Brajão de Oliveira, Pedro Luis Candido de Souza Cassela
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT COVID‐19 severity and survival are influenced by the host immune response to SARS‐CoV‐2. Programmed cell death 1 (PD‐1), a key immune checkpoint, regulates T‐cell activation and antiviral immune balance. Since genetic variability can modulate these responses, we investigated whether the PDCD1 polymorphisms rs11568821 C > T, rs2227982 G > A, rs2227981 G > A and rs10204525 C > T are associated with COVID‐19 severity and mortality in a Brazilian cohort. A total of 366 COVID‐19 patients (165 mild, 72 moderate, and 129 severe cases) were genotyped for the four PDCD1 SNPs, and their haplotype structures were estimated. Significant differences were observed in the allele frequencies of rs11568821, and in genotypes and allele frequencies of the exonic rs2227982, among mild, moderate, and severe cases. Multinomial logistic regression identified associations between rs2227982 (dominant and overdominant models) and moderate COVID‐19, and between the rs2227981 AA genotype (genotypic and recessive models) and severe COVID‐19. The rs10204525 polymorphism (CT genotype under genotypic dominant and overdominant models) also presented an association with severe COVID‐19. However, none of these associations remained independent. Haplotype analysis identified five major haplotypes with significantly different frequencies among the groups (p = 0.02); however, no association was found between the haplotypes and disease severity or outcome. This is the first study to evaluate SNP rs2227982 in COVID‐19 patients, and the first to evaluate the four aforementioned SNPs in a Brazilian population. Overall, our findings suggest that these polymorphisms, although involved in COVID‐19 immunopathogenesis, are not suitable biomarkers for predicting COVID‐19 severity or clinical outcomes.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
COVID-19 severity and survival are influenced by the host immune response to SARS-CoV-2, and since genetic variability can modulate these responses, we investigated whether PDCD1 polymorphisms are associated with COVID-19 severity and mortality in a Brazilian cohort.
Design and population
A total of 366 COVID-19 patients (165 mild, 72 moderate, and 129 severe cases) were genotyped for four PDCD1 SNPs, and their haplotype structures were estimated.
Core methods
Multinomial logistic regression identified associations between PDCD1 polymorphisms and COVID-19 severity, and haplotype analysis was performed to evaluate differences in frequencies among the groups.
Main results
Significant differences were observed in allele frequencies of rs11568821 and genotypes and allele frequencies of rs2227982 among mild, moderate, and severe cases; however, none of these associations remained independent, and no association was found between haplotypes and disease severity or outcome.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors suggest that these polymorphisms, although involved in COVID-19 immunopathogenesis, are not suitable biomarkers for predicting COVID-19 severity or clinical outcomes, and this is the first study to evaluate SNP rs2227982 in COVID-19 patients and the first to evaluate the four SNPs in a Brazilian population.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform that PDCD1 polymorphisms are not reliable biomarkers for COVID-19 severity prediction, which may guide future research toward other genetic or immunological factors for risk stratification.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the lack of independent associations and the study's limitation to a single cohort, though the sample size and comprehensive genetic analysis provide reasonable confidence in the findings.

"COVID Is Hard. Cancer Is Harder.": A Qualitative Study of Young Adults Diagnosed with Cancer During the COVID-19 Global Pandemic.

"COVID Is Hard. Cancer Is Harder.": A Qualitative Study of Young Adults Diagnosed with Cancer During the COVID-19 Global Pandemic.
作者： Allicock MA, Shay LA, Godinich BM, Andrea C. Betts, Caitlin C. Murphy, Chelsey Gomez, C. Kirk
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to explore the unique challenges faced by adolescents and young adults with cancer (AYAs) during the COVID-19 pandemic, as the pandemic compounded difficulties in accessing care, support, and managing stressors.
设计与对象
AYA(年龄 18-39 岁)在全国范围内招募,完成了一项在线调查,并参加了 2022 年 3 月至 2022 年 10 月期间的六个在线焦点小组之一。
核心方法
使用演绎和归纳编码对成绩单进行分析,以确定与 AYAs 在大流行期间应对癌症 诊断 的经历相关的主题。
主要结果
Ten AYAs who received a cancer diagnosis between 2020 and 2021 participated in focus groups; all were female and non-Hispanic White. Common themes included delays in diagnosis, restricted treatment options, compounded fear of cancer and COVID-19, psychological distress, and unexpected advantages from the pandemic.
解释与创新
作者解释说,隔离、限制家庭参与的医疗保健协议以及大流行的情绪影响给新诊断的 AYA 带来了额外的挑战,强调了在危机期间提供量身定制的支持的必要性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果对大流行病以外的 AYA 医疗保健具有影响,特别是对于那些需要物理隔离来管理健康风险的人,强调需要采取适应性强的护理策略。
局限与证据强度
尽管定性方法提供了丰富的见解,但由于样本量小(n = 10)和缺乏多样性(所有女性和非西班牙裔白人),证据强度中等,限制了普遍性。

"COVID Is Hard. Cancer Is Harder.": A Qualitative Study of Young Adults Diagnosed with Cancer During the COVID-19 Global Pandemic.

Authors: Allicock MA, Shay LA, Godinich BM, Andrea C. Betts, Caitlin C. Murphy, Chelsey Gomez, C. Kirk
Original abstract (English)
Purpose The COVID-19 pandemic compounded unique challenges for adolescents and young adults with cancer (AYAs). Receiving a cancer diagnosis during this period may create further challenges to accessing care, support, and managing stressors. We explored AYAs' experiences of coping with a cancer diagnosis in the context of the pandemic. Methods AYAs (ages 18-39 years) were recruited nationally, completed a brief online survey, and participated in one of six online focus groups from March 2022 to October 2022. We analyzed transcripts using both deductive and inductive coding to identify themes. Results Ten AYAs received a cancer diagnosis between 2020 and 2021 and participated in focus groups. Participants were all female and non-Hispanic White. Common themes were delays in diagnosis and restricted treatment options, compounded fear of cancer diagnosis and risk of COVID-19, and psychological distress and management of cancer and the pandemic. AYAs also discussed unexpected advantages arising from the pandemic. Conclusion Isolation, health care protocols that limited family involvement, and the emotional and mental health impacts of the pandemic have created additional challenges for newly diagnosed AYAs to navigate. Our findings have implications for AYAs' health care beyond a pandemic, especially for those who may require physical isolation to manage health risks.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore the unique challenges faced by adolescents and young adults with cancer (AYAs) during the COVID-19 pandemic, as the pandemic compounded difficulties in accessing care, support, and managing stressors.
Design and population
AYAs (ages 18-39 years) were recruited nationally, completed an online survey, and participated in one of six online focus groups from March 2022 to October 2022.
Core methods
Transcripts were analyzed using both deductive and inductive coding to identify themes related to AYAs' experiences of coping with a cancer diagnosis during the pandemic.
Main results
Ten AYAs who received a cancer diagnosis between 2020 and 2021 participated in focus groups; all were female and non-Hispanic White. Common themes included delays in diagnosis, restricted treatment options, compounded fear of cancer and COVID-19, psychological distress, and unexpected advantages from the pandemic.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that isolation, healthcare protocols limiting family involvement, and the emotional impacts of the pandemic created additional challenges for newly diagnosed AYAs, highlighting the need for tailored support during crises.
Scientific and public-health significance
Findings have implications for AYAs' healthcare beyond pandemics, especially for those requiring physical isolation to manage health risks, emphasizing the need for adaptable care strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the small sample size (n=10) and lack of diversity (all female and non-Hispanic White), limiting generalizability, though the qualitative approach provided rich insights.

预防受控人类 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Delta 变异突破性感染的生物标志物

Biomarkers of protection against controlled human SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant breakthrough infection
作者： Anika Singanayagam, Helen R. Wagstaffe, Lydia J Slater, Polly Fox-Sheehan, Meng-San Wu, Mawer, A., Scott, H., Orlagh Daly, Jen Mae Low, Raquel Lopez Ramon
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
尽管已有免疫力,但需要更好地了解变体如何导致突破性感染,以推进下一代 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗 的开发,包括那些可能提供跨变体保护或阻断 传播 的药物。
设计与对象
18-30岁健康接种疫苗的成年人在逐步剂量递增控制的人类感染模型(CHIM)中鼻内接种,剂量为1x102 TCID50至1x106 TCID50的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Delta变体;在 1x106 TCID50 组中,选择血清中和抗体滴度 (NT50) ≤1:80 的参与者。
核心方法
参与者在剂量递增的 CHIM 中鼻内接种 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Delta 变体,接种后隔离长达 14 天,门诊随访 12 个月;主要目标是引发安全、耐受性良好的 Delta 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 突破性感染,感染率超过 50%。
主要结果
血清选择组中 33% (6/18) 出现轻度至中度症状的持续感染;血清中和抗体、抗 N IgG、粘膜抗 S IgA 和 N 特异性 T 细胞最有力地预测了对病毒学定义的感染的保护,较高的中和和抗体水平与较低的病毒载量相关。
解释与创新
具有混合免疫能力的人对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 突破性感染的易感性与先前存在的抗体水平较低密切相关,但与保护相关的免疫标记物的多样性意味着多管齐下的免疫可能会带来额外的好处。
科研与公卫意义
这项研究建立了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 CHIM 行为的框架,利用血清选择来提高攻击率,并建立了定义保护的定量相关性的过程,这对于下一代 疫苗 的开发是必要的。
局限与证据强度
该研究是在一小群年轻且已接种疫苗的人群中进行的,其普遍性有限;然而,受控人类感染模型为预防 Delta 变异突破性感染的相关性提供了高可信度证据。

Biomarkers of protection against controlled human SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant breakthrough infection

Authors: Anika Singanayagam, Helen R. Wagstaffe, Lydia J Slater, Polly Fox-Sheehan, Meng-San Wu, Mawer, A., Scott, H., Orlagh Daly, Jen Mae Low, Raquel Lopez Ramon
Original abstract (English)
BackgroundImproved understanding of how variants cause breakthrough infection, despite pre-existing immunity, is needed to advance development of next-generation SARS-CoV-2 vaccines, including those that may provide cross-variant protection or block transmission. SARS-CoV-2 controlled human infection models (CHIMs) may therefore identify correlates of protection and accelerate the development of new interventions. MethodsHealthy vaccinated adults aged 18-30 years were inoculated intranasally in a stepwise dose- escalation CHIM with doses from 1x102 TCID50 to 1x106 TCID50 of SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant. Within the 1x106 TCID50 group, participants were selected for serum neutralising antibody titres (NT50) [≤]1:80. Post-inoculation, participants were quarantined for up to 14 days. Outpatient follow-up continued for 12 months. The primary aim was to elicit safe, well-tolerated Delta SARS-CoV-2 breakthrough infection at a rate of over 50%. FindingsForty-six participants were inoculated; 22 during dose-escalation with no resultant infections, and 24 at the highest dose, of whom 18 were screened for low serum neutralising antibodies. Sustained infection with mild-to-moderate symptoms occurred in 33% (6/18) of the sero- selected group, with highly variable viral loads, viral emissions and symptoms. Serum neutralising antibody, anti-N IgG and to a lesser extent, mucosal anti-S IgA and N-specific T cells most strongly predicted protection from virologically-defined infection. Higher neutralisation, serum and nasal anti-N IgG level, and N-specific T cell responses correlated with lower viral load, while baseline nasal anti-S IgA was associated with lower symptom scores. Transient infection was additionally observed in 6 participants and was associated with higher baseline N-specific T cell responses than those that developed sustained infection. InterpretationSusceptibility to SARS-CoV-2 breakthrough infection in those with hybrid immunity is strongly associated with low levels of pre-existing antibody, but the diversity of immune markers associated with protection implies that additional benefits may be conferred by multi-pronged immunity. FundingWellcome Trust Research in contextO_ST_ABSEvidence before this studyC_ST_ABSTo identify other published SARS-CoV-2 controlled human infection models (CHIM), a search on PubMed was carried out on 4th March 2026. The search terms used were (("controlled human infection") OR ("human challenge")) and ((SARS-CoV-2) OR (COVID-19)) and ("clinical trial"). Two clinical studies were identified. The first study involved healthy adult 18-29 year olds, seronegative to SARS-CoV-2 inoculated with 1x101 TCID50 pre-Alpha (wild-type) SARS-CoV-2. Eighteen of 34 (53%) participants became infected. The model was safe and well-tolerated and there were no study-related serious adverse events. Mild to moderate symptoms were reported by 16 of 18 (89%) infected individuals while 2 had virtually no symptoms. 14 of 18 (78%) of participants reported smell disturbance measured by the University of Pennsylvania Smell Identification Test (UPSIT). Viral detection by qPCR became quantifiable in throat swabs from 40 hours ([~]1.67 days) post- inoculation and nose swabs at 58 hours ([~]2.4 days). Viral load peaked in the throat at 112 hours ([~]4.2 days) post-inoculation and later at 148 hours ([~]6.2 days) post-inoculation in the nose. The second study involved healthy adult 18-30 year olds, seropositive to SARS-CoV-2 inoculated with escalating doses (1x101-1x105 TCID50) of the same pre-Alpha variant. Thirty- six participants were inoculated and no sustained infection meeting the protocol-defined definition of infection was induced. Five (14%) of 36 volunteers were considered to have transient (brief) infections, based on the kinetic of their PCR-positive swabs. In the first study, functionally-complete protection was associated with early increases in innate and adaptive cell abundance in the nose post-inoculation, higher pre-existing chemokine levels (particularly CCL13) in the nasal lining fluid and cross-reactive T cell responses. Transient infections (PCR positivity outside of residual inoculum not meeting the protocol- defined criteria for infection) were present in both studies. In the second study, transient infection was associated with significantly lower baseline mucosal and systemic SARS-CoV- 2 antibody levels and significantly lower peripheral IFN-{gamma} producing CD8+ T-cell against a SARS-CoV-2 peptide pool than uninfected participants. Added value of this studyWith near-universal seropositivity to SARS-CoV-2, understanding the factors that influence how variants cause breakthrough infection is critical. The optimisation of a model that can induce safe and tolerable infection in a large proportion of participants is necessary for SARS- CoV-2 CHIMs to be used as a tool for next-generation vaccine development. Our study is the first SARS-CoV-2 CHIM of seropositive individuals to induce sustained, protocol defined infection, albeit with an infection rate of 33%. Despite the low number of infected individuals, we identified several potential correlates of protection against breakthrough Delta SARS-CoV-2 infection beyond serum neutralising antibodies. Implications of all the available evidenceThis study establishes the framework for SARS-CoV-2 CHIM conduct, using sero-selection to increase attack rate in the same way it is used for influenza human challenge studies, and a process for defining quantitative correlates of protection. Further work will optimise model parameters with Omicron subvariants to result in infection rates of [≥]50% to support testing of novel interventions.

Question and background
Improved understanding of how variants cause breakthrough infection, despite pre-existing immunity, is needed to advance development of next-generation SARS-CoV-2 vaccines, including those that may provide cross-variant protection or block transmission.
Design and population
Healthy vaccinated adults aged 18-30 years were inoculated intranasally in a stepwise dose-escalation controlled human infection model (CHIM) with doses from 1x102 TCID50 to 1x106 TCID50 of SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant; within the 1x106 TCID50 group, participants were selected for serum neutralising antibody titres (NT50) ≤1:80.
Core methods
Participants were inoculated intranasally with SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant in a dose-escalation CHIM, with post-inoculation quarantine for up to 14 days and outpatient follow-up for 12 months; the primary aim was to elicit safe, well-tolerated Delta SARS-CoV-2 breakthrough infection at a rate of over 50%.
Main results
Sustained infection with mild-to-moderate symptoms occurred in 33% (6/18) of the sero-selected group; serum neutralising antibody, anti-N IgG, mucosal anti-S IgA, and N-specific T cells most strongly predicted protection from virologically-defined infection, with higher neutralisation and antibody levels correlating with lower viral load.
Interpretation and novelty
Susceptibility to SARS-CoV-2 breakthrough infection in those with hybrid immunity is strongly associated with low levels of pre-existing antibody, but the diversity of immune markers associated with protection implies that additional benefits may be conferred by multi-pronged immunity.
Scientific and public-health significance
This study establishes the framework for SARS-CoV-2 CHIM conduct, using sero-selection to increase attack rate and a process for defining quantitative correlates of protection, which is necessary for next-generation vaccine development.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was conducted in a small, young, vaccinated population with limited generalisability; however, the controlled human infection model provides high-confidence evidence for correlates of protection against Delta variant breakthrough infection.

Modeling the public health impact of vaccination with Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine, LP.8.1, 2025–2026 formula in the pediatric population aged 5–17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19 in the United States

Modeling the public health impact of vaccination with Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine, LP.8.1, 2025–2026 formula in the pediatric population aged 5–17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19 in the United States
作者： Alon Yehoshua, Manuela Di Fusco, Abby E. Rudolph, Elizabeth Thoburn, Santiago M C Lopez, Josie Dodd, Benjamin Yarnoff
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study evaluated the potential public health impact of receiving Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine, LP.8.1, 2025-2026 formula, in the US pediatric population aged 5-17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19.
设计与对象
A previously published static decision-tree model was adapted to compare health outcomes with and without vaccination. The study focused on the US pediatric population aged 5-17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19.
核心方法
人口统计学、流行病学、疫苗(有效性、疫苗 摄取)和临床模型输入源自 疫苗 数据和相关已发表的研究。
主要结果
In the absence of LP.8.1-adapted BNT162b2 COVID-19 vaccination, COVID-19 burden among those aged 5-11 years and 12-17 years was projected as 1,007,114 and 1,467,724 symptomatic cases, 2,493 and 2,547 hospitalizations, and 109 and 107 COVID-19-related deaths, respectively. Increasing uptake to 50% would increase the health burden averted 6-fold.
解释与创新
Receipt of an LP.8.1-adapted COVID-19 vaccine was projected to reduce the COVID-19 burden in the high-risk pediatric population, even with low vaccine uptake. Increasing vaccine uptake in this population could yield larger public health impact.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,疫苗接种 与 LP.8.1 适应的 BNT162b2 可以显着减轻高危儿科人群的严重后果负担,凸显了增加 疫苗 摄取的重要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于之前发布的静态决策树模型,并假设观察到的 2024-2025 年吸收水平,这可能会限制预测的可信度。

Modeling the public health impact of vaccination with Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine, LP.8.1, 2025–2026 formula in the pediatric population aged 5–17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19 in the United States

Authors: Alon Yehoshua, Manuela Di Fusco, Abby E. Rudolph, Elizabeth Thoburn, Santiago M C Lopez, Josie Dodd, Benjamin Yarnoff
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: This study evaluated the potential public health impact of receiving Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine, LP.8.1, 2025-2026 formula, in the US pediatric population aged 5-17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19. RESEARCH DESIGN AND METHODS: A previously published static decision-tree model was adapted to compare health outcomes with and without vaccination. Demographic, epidemiological, vaccine (effectiveness, vaccine uptake), and clinical model inputs were derived from surveillance data and relevant published studies. RESULTS: In the absence of LP.8.1-adapted BNT162b2 COVID-19 vaccination, COVID-19 burden among those aged 5-11 years and 12-17 years was projected as 1,007,114 and 1,467,724 symptomatic cases, 2,493 and 2,547 hospitalizations, and 109 and 107 COVID-19-related deaths, respectively. Assuming observed 2024-2025 uptake levels of 8.7% and 8.6% among those aged 5-11 and 12-17 years, respectively, LP.8.1-adapted BNT162b2 COVID-19 vaccination was projected to avert 4,387 and 7,146 symptomatic cases, 27 and 31 hospitalizations, and 1 death in each age group, respectively. Increasing uptake to 50% would increase the health burden averted 6-fold. CONCLUSIONS: Receipt of an LP.8.1-adapted COVID-19 vaccine was projected to reduce the COVID-19 burden in the high-risk pediatric population, even with low vaccine uptake. Increasing vaccine uptake in this population could yield larger public health impact.

Question and background
This study evaluated the potential public health impact of receiving Pfizer-BioNTech COVID-19 vaccine, LP.8.1, 2025-2026 formula, in the US pediatric population aged 5-17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19.
Design and population
A previously published static decision-tree model was adapted to compare health outcomes with and without vaccination. The study focused on the US pediatric population aged 5-17 years at high risk of severe outcomes from COVID-19.
Core methods
Demographic, epidemiological, vaccine (effectiveness, vaccine uptake), and clinical model inputs were derived from surveillance data and relevant published studies.
Main results
In the absence of LP.8.1-adapted BNT162b2 COVID-19 vaccination, COVID-19 burden among those aged 5-11 years and 12-17 years was projected as 1,007,114 and 1,467,724 symptomatic cases, 2,493 and 2,547 hospitalizations, and 109 and 107 COVID-19-related deaths, respectively. Increasing uptake to 50% would increase the health burden averted 6-fold.
Interpretation and novelty
Receipt of an LP.8.1-adapted COVID-19 vaccine was projected to reduce the COVID-19 burden in the high-risk pediatric population, even with low vaccine uptake. Increasing vaccine uptake in this population could yield larger public health impact.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that vaccination with LP.8.1-adapted BNT162b2 could significantly reduce the burden of severe outcomes in high-risk pediatric populations, highlighting the importance of increasing vaccine uptake.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on a previously published static decision-tree model and assumes observed 2024-2025 uptake levels, which may limit the confidence in the projections.

SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches
作者： ‏Helal F. Hetta, Rehab Ahmed, Abdul Haseeb, Salwa Bukhari, Zinab Alatawi, Ahmad Jamal Mahrous, Mahmoud E. Elrggal, Mohammad Al Masri, Ahmed A. Kotb

查看研究七要素

本综述基于对 PubMed、Scopus 和 Web of Science 数据库的结构化文献检索,涵盖 2020 年 1 月至 2026 年 1 月期间发表的研究。

SARS-CoV-2 Point-of-Care Testing Modalities: Integrating Molecular, Immunological, Biosensor, and AI Approaches

Authors: ‏Helal F. Hetta, Rehab Ahmed, Abdul Haseeb, Salwa Bukhari, Zinab Alatawi, Ahmad Jamal Mahrous, Mahmoud E. Elrggal, Mohammad Al Masri, Ahmed A. Kotb
Original abstract (English)
The coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) pandemic highlighted the critical need for rapid, accessible, and accurate diagnostic tools to support timely clinical decision-making, outbreak control, and public health surveillance. Point-of-care testing (POCT) has emerged as an essential component of decentralized healthcare by enabling diagnostic testing outside conventional laboratory settings. This review was based on a structured literature search of PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science databases covering studies published between January 2020 and January 2026. The review evaluates current advances in COVID-19 POCT technologies, including molecular assays, antigen-based tests, antibody-based assays, biosensor platforms, and artificial intelligence (AI)-assisted diagnostic approaches. Molecular POCT methods, including rapid reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction (RT-PCR), loop-mediated isothermal amplification (LAMP), and CRISPR-based technologies, provide high analytical sensitivity and specificity and are increasingly suitable for decentralized diagnostic applications. Antigen-based assays offer rapid and cost-effective screening solutions, although diagnostic performance may vary depending on viral load, symptom onset, and circulating variants. Antibody-based POCT remains valuable for seroprevalence studies, retrospective diagnosis, and immune-response monitoring rather than acute infection detection. Emerging biosensor technologies and AI-enabled diagnostic systems demonstrate promising analytical capabilities and operational advantages; however, many remain at the prototype or early-validation stage and require further clinical evaluation before widespread implementation. The findings indicate that no single POCT modality is optimal for all clinical scenarios. Instead, molecular, antigen, antibody, biosensor, and AI-assisted approaches provide complementary strengths that support different diagnostic and public health objectives. Continued advances in assay design, digital connectivity, multiplex testing, and variant-resilient detection strategies are expected to further enhance the role of POCT in COVID-19 management and future infectious disease preparedness.
