氧化还原敏感因子作为硫醇化合物的靶标,阻碍 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制和炎症反应。

Redox-sensitive factors as targets of thiol compounds to hinder SARS-CoV-2 replication and inflammatory response.
作者： Marta De Angelis, Gianni Gori Savellini, Elena Piselli, Gabriele Anichini, Giulia Radocchia, Sofia Masini, Francesca Monittola, Francesca Bartoccini, Michela Bruschi, Mauro Magnani
摘要中文翻译进化 经历了快速遗传,导致具有独特 突变 的新变体的出现,影响全球公共卫生。感染后,病毒会引发强烈的炎症反应,其特征是释放促炎细胞因子,这在肺损伤中发挥着核心作用。它还改变 宿主 抗氧化反应,引起氧化应激,支持病毒复制和细胞因子过量产生。本研究调查了感染 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的 Calu-3 和 A549-ACE2/TMPRSS2 细胞中的关键致病效应子,重点关注复制动力学、细胞氧化还原状态和炎症细胞因子谱。注射后 48 小时观察到谷胱甘肽 (GSH) 和半胱氨酸 (Cys) 发生显着的氧化还原变化,并伴有强烈的促炎细胞因子反应,这可能是通过 JNK/AP-1 信号通路的激活实现的。为了抵消这些影响,测试了两种硫醇分子:I-152,N-乙酰半胱氨酸 (NAC) 和 β-巯基乙胺 (MEA) 的单硫醇缀合物及其二硫醇衍生物 I-152SdAc。硫醇通过增强 Nrf2 介导的基因(例如谷氨酸-Cys 连接酶修饰亚基 (GCLM))的表达来恢复 GSH 平衡,并抵消 AP-1 介导的途径,从而显着减少炎症和病毒复制。抗病毒药物 和硫醇的抗炎活性在 NHBE 细胞中得到证实。这些发现强调氧化还原失衡是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的关键致病事件。值得注意的是,除了Nrf2和AP-1之外,其他氧化还原敏感因子,例如CHAC谷胱甘肽特异性γ-谷氨酰环转移酶1(CHAC1),似乎对发病机制有贡献,并且可能代表氧化还原活性化合物的新的潜在治疗靶点。因此,硫醇衍生的分子通过限制病毒复制和炎症而充当广泛有效的化合物。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在确定硫醇化合物可靶向的氧化还原敏感因子,以抑制 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制并减少炎症反应。
设计与对象
该研究重点关注 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体,并检查它们对 Calu-3 和 A549-ACE2/TMPRSS2 细胞以及 NHBE 细胞的影响,以评估复制动力学、细胞氧化还原状态和炎症细胞因子谱。
核心方法
研究人员测试了两种硫醇分子 I-152 和 I-152SdAc,以抵消 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染引起的氧化还原失衡和炎症反应。
主要结果
感染后 48 小时观察到显着的氧化还原变化,包括谷胱甘肽 (GSH) 和半胱氨酸 (Cys) 的变化,以及强烈的促炎细胞因子反应。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,氧化还原失衡是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 发病机制 中的关键因素,硫醇化合物可以有效恢复 GSH 平衡并抵消 AP-1 介导的途径。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果可能有助于开发针对氧化还原敏感因子的新治疗策略,以减少 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染中的病毒复制和炎症。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外模型,不包括临床数据,这可能会影响研究结果的普遍性。然而,证据确凿,直接支持所提出的机制。

Redox-sensitive factors as targets of thiol compounds to hinder SARS-CoV-2 replication and inflammatory response.

Authors: Marta De Angelis, Gianni Gori Savellini, Elena Piselli, Gabriele Anichini, Giulia Radocchia, Sofia Masini, Francesca Monittola, Francesca Bartoccini, Michela Bruschi, Mauro Magnani
Original abstract (English)
SARS-CoV-2 has undergone rapid genetic evolution, leading to the emergence of new variants with distinct mutations impacting global public health. Upon infection, the virus triggers a robust inflammatory response characterized by the release of pro-inflammatory cytokines, which play a central role in lung injury. It also alters the host antioxidant response, causing oxidative stress that supports viral replication and cytokine overproduction. This study investigated key pathogenic effectors in Calu-3 and A549-ACE2/TMPRSS2 cells infected with SARS-CoV-2 variants, focusing on replication kinetics, cellular redox state, and inflammatory cytokine profile. A dramatic redox alteration in terms of glutathione (GSH) and Cysteine (Cys) was observed at 48 h p.i., along with a strong pro-inflammatory cytokine response , likely via activation of the JNK/AP-1 signaling pathway. To counteract these effects, two thiol molecules were tested: I-152, a monothiol conjugate of N-Acetyl-Cysteine (NAC) and β-mercaptoethylamine (MEA) and its dithiol derivative, I-152SdAc. Thiols restored GSH balance by enhancing the expression of Nrf2-mediated genes, such as glutamate-Cys ligase modifier subunit (GCLM), and counteracted AP-1-mediated pathway, resulting in a significant reduction of inflammation and viral replication. Antiviral and anti-inflammatory activities of thiols were confirmed in NHBE cells. These findings highlight that redox imbalance is a key pathogenetic event in SARS-CoV-2 infection. Notably, besides Nrf2 and AP-1, other redox-sensitive factors, such as the CHAC glutathione-specific gamma-glutamyl-cyclotransferase 1 (CHAC1), seem to contribute to the pathogenesis and may represent a new potential therapeutic target of redox active compounds. Therefore, the thiol-derived molecules act as broadly effective compounds by limiting virus replication and inflammation.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to identify redox-sensitive factors that could be targeted by thiol compounds to inhibit SARS-CoV-2 replication and reduce inflammatory responses.
Design and population
The study focused on SARS-CoV-2 variants and examined their effects on Calu-3 and A549-ACE2/TMPRSS2 cells, as well as NHBE cells, to assess replication kinetics, cellular redox state, and inflammatory cytokine profile.
Core methods
The researchers tested two thiol molecules, I-152 and I-152SdAc, to counteract the redox imbalance and inflammatory response induced by SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Main results
A dramatic redox alteration, including changes in glutathione (GSH) and Cysteine (Cys), was observed at 48 hours post-infection, along with a strong pro-inflammatory cytokine response.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that redox imbalance is a critical factor in SARS-CoV-2 pathogenesis and that thiol compounds can effectively restore GSH balance and counteract the AP-1-mediated pathway.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results may inform the development of new therapeutic strategies targeting redox-sensitive factors to reduce viral replication and inflammation in SARS-CoV-2 infections.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro models and does not include clinical data, which may affect the generalizability of the findings. However, the evidence is strong and directly supports the proposed mechanisms.

Determinants of seasonal influenza and COVID-19 vaccine uptake in high-risk individuals in northern Sweden.

Determinants of seasonal influenza and COVID-19 vaccine uptake in high-risk individuals in northern Sweden.
作者： Lucas Grape, Wilma Persson, Adam Sergejev, Simon Vihtari Hansson, Lina Gyllencreutz, Henrik Togstad, Johan Normark, Urban Johansson Kostenniemi
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to identify the remaining vaccination barriers in high-income countries for seasonal influenza and COVID-19 vaccines in high-risk individuals. The background highlights that these vaccines prevent disease, disability, and death, yet vaccine coverage remains suboptimal.
设计与对象
The study recruited patients seeking emergency department care at a hospital in northern Sweden. It included 600 patients, of whom 450 were eligible for both influenza and COVID-19 vaccines.
核心方法
Logistic regression was used to calculate odds ratios (OR) and adjusted odds ratios (aOR) for determinants associated with influenza and COVID-19 vaccination. Data were collected through semi-structured interviews and medical records, and linked with community-level determinants from national registers.
主要结果
Among eligible individuals, influenza vaccine coverage was 52.0% and COVID-19 vaccine coverage was 54.2%. Co-administration of both vaccines was high, with an odds ratio of 55.33 (95% CI: 30.86–99.22). Individual-level determinants associated with lower vaccine uptake included male biological sex (aOR: 0.57, 95% CI: 0.37–0.88), younger age (aOR: 0.50, 95% CI: 0.41–0.61), smoking (aOR: 0.43, 95% CI: 0.18–1.02), reduced independence (aOR: 0.59, 95% CI: 0.31–1.10), and age-only eligibility (aOR: 0.41, 95% CI: 0.19–0.86).
解释与创新
The authors interpreted that vaccine uptake for influenza and COVID-19 was primarily associated with individual-level determinants, whereas community-level determinants showed little impact. This finding highlights the importance of addressing individual factors in improving vaccination rates.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可以为有针对性的沟通策略和统一的资格标准提供信息,以提高高风险人群的 疫苗接种 率。他们还表明,社区层面的干预措施对 疫苗 的吸收影响可能有限。
局限与证据强度
该研究的局限性在于它只关注瑞典北部的一家医院,这可能会影响普遍性。证据强度中等,因为结果基于单一环境,可能无法代表更广泛的人群。

Determinants of seasonal influenza and COVID-19 vaccine uptake in high-risk individuals in northern Sweden.

Authors: Lucas Grape, Wilma Persson, Adam Sergejev, Simon Vihtari Hansson, Lina Gyllencreutz, Henrik Togstad, Johan Normark, Urban Johansson Kostenniemi
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Seasonal influenza and COVID-19 vaccination in high-risk individuals prevent disease, disability, and death. Nevertheless, many high-income countries continue to experience suboptimal vaccine coverage. We therefore aimed to identify the remaining vaccination barriers in these settings. We recruited patients seeking emergency department care at a hospital in northern Sweden. Using semi-structured interviews and medical records, we collected high-resolution individual-level data, and linked community-level determinants from national registers. Logistic regression was used to calculate odds ratios (OR) and adjusted odds ratios (aOR) for determinants associated with influenza and COVID-19 vaccination. We included 600 patients of whom 450 were eligible for both vaccines. Coverage among eligible individuals was 52.0% for influenza and 54.2% for COVID-19. Co-administration of both vaccines was high (OR 55.33, 95% CI: 30.86–99.22). Individual-level determinants associated with lower vaccine uptake included male biological sex (aOR: 0.57, 95% CI: 0.37–0.88, younger age (aOR: 0.50, 95% CI: 0.41–0.61), smoking (aOR: 0.43, 95% CI: 0.18–1.02), reduced independence (aOR: 0.59, 95% CI: 0.31–1.10), and age-only eligibility (aOR 0.41, 95% CI: 0.19–0.86). In multimorbid individuals, meeting more eligibility criteria showed no significant association with higher uptake (aOR: 1.14, 95% CI: 0.93–1.39). Finally, we noted only limited influence of community-level determinants. In conclusion, vaccine uptake for influenza and COVID-19 was primarily associated with individual-level determinants, whereas community-level showed little impact. Targeted communication, such as risk-stacking to improve risk-awareness, harmonized eligibility criteria, and co-administration, may help overcome remaining vaccination barriers in high-risk individuals.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to identify the remaining vaccination barriers in high-income countries for seasonal influenza and COVID-19 vaccines in high-risk individuals. The background highlights that these vaccines prevent disease, disability, and death, yet vaccine coverage remains suboptimal.
Design and population
The study recruited patients seeking emergency department care at a hospital in northern Sweden. It included 600 patients, of whom 450 were eligible for both influenza and COVID-19 vaccines.
Core methods
Logistic regression was used to calculate odds ratios (OR) and adjusted odds ratios (aOR) for determinants associated with influenza and COVID-19 vaccination. Data were collected through semi-structured interviews and medical records, and linked with community-level determinants from national registers.
Main results
Among eligible individuals, influenza vaccine coverage was 52.0% and COVID-19 vaccine coverage was 54.2%. Co-administration of both vaccines was high, with an odds ratio of 55.33 (95% CI: 30.86–99.22). Individual-level determinants associated with lower vaccine uptake included male biological sex (aOR: 0.57, 95% CI: 0.37–0.88), younger age (aOR: 0.50, 95% CI: 0.41–0.61), smoking (aOR: 0.43, 95% CI: 0.18–1.02), reduced independence (aOR: 0.59, 95% CI: 0.31–1.10), and age-only eligibility (aOR: 0.41, 95% CI: 0.19–0.86).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that vaccine uptake for influenza and COVID-19 was primarily associated with individual-level determinants, whereas community-level determinants showed little impact. This finding highlights the importance of addressing individual factors in improving vaccination rates.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform targeted communication strategies and harmonized eligibility criteria to improve vaccination rates among high-risk individuals. They also suggest that community-level interventions may have limited influence on vaccine uptake.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its focus on a single hospital in northern Sweden, which may affect generalisability. The evidence strength is moderate, as the results are based on a single setting and may not represent broader populations.

低剂量的新型 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 mRNA-VLP 疫苗 可引发与许可的 mRNA 疫苗 等效的细胞介导免疫 2260043

Low dosage of a novel SARS-COV-2 mRNA-VLP vaccine elicits equivalent cell-mediated immunity to a licensed mRNA vaccine 2260043
作者： Michael Powell, Nicholas G. Battaglia, Lee-Jah Chang, Raffaeloo Cimbro, Stacey Cromer Berman, Tope Oyedele, Angela F Pack, Marcelino Padilla, Tiffany L. Roe, Ann Marie Stanley
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
mRNA 编码的病毒样颗粒 (VLP) 是 疫苗 技术的一项新兴进步,能够将病毒抗原自组装成与天然病毒非常相似的结构。这种针对传统 mRNA 疫苗接种 的创新方法可能会增强 疫苗 诱导的适应性 免疫应答 并允许减少剂量。
设计与对象
1期临床研究ARTEMIS-C旨在评估18-64岁和≥65岁成年人服用AZD6563(5μg或10μg)或许可的BNT162b2 XBB.1.5 mRNA 疫苗(30μg)后的细胞免疫原性。
核心方法
To evaluate this technology, we developed AZD6563, an mRNA VLP vaccine targeting the COVID-19 XBB1.5 spike variant. The study assessed spike-specific CD4+ and CD8+ T cell responses and functional analysis of cytokine production profiles.
主要结果
AZD6563 驱动的尖峰特异性 CD4+ 和 CD8+ T 细胞反应与高剂量 BNT162b2 引起的反应相当。功能分析显示各组细胞因子的产生情况相似;然而,10μg剂量的AZD6563在≥65年的队列研究中导致更高的TCR多样性。尖峰特异性 B 细胞的扩增在 AZD6563 10μg 组中最为明显,交叉反应性 XBB.1.5 尖峰特异性 B 细胞也显着增加,这些细胞也识别 Omicron BA.4/5 和祖先 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体。
解释与创新
Collectively, these results demonstrate that AZD6563, at reduced doses, matches the cellular immunogenicity of BNT162b2 while enhancing B cell cross-reactivity and TCR diversity in older adults, supporting its potential as a next-generation COVID-19 vaccine candidate.
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,AZD6563 可以以较低剂量提供更有效的 疫苗接种 策略,同时保持稳健的 免疫应答 和针对多种 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的潜在更广泛的保护,这可以提高 疫苗 的可及性和覆盖范围。
局限与证据强度
The study was a phase 1 clinical trial with a limited sample size, and further research is needed to evaluate long-term immunity and efficacy in larger populations. Despite these limitations, the evidence strength is moderate due to the well-controlled design and direct comparison with a licensed vaccine.

Low dosage of a novel SARS-COV-2 mRNA-VLP vaccine elicits equivalent cell-mediated immunity to a licensed mRNA vaccine 2260043

Authors: Michael Powell, Nicholas G. Battaglia, Lee-Jah Chang, Raffaeloo Cimbro, Stacey Cromer Berman, Tope Oyedele, Angela F Pack, Marcelino Padilla, Tiffany L. Roe, Ann Marie Stanley
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Introduction mRNA-encoded virus-like particles (VLPs) are an emerging advancement in vaccine technology, enabling the self-assembly of viral antigens into structures that closely resemble native viruses. This innovative approach to conventional mRNA vaccination may boost vaccine-induced adaptive immune responses and allow for reduced dosing. To evaluate this technology, we developed AZD6563, an mRNA VLP vaccine targeting the COVID-19 XBB1.5 spike variant. Methods The phase 1 clinical study ARTEMIS-C was conducted to assess cellular immunogenicity in adults aged 18—64 years and ≥65 years following administration of AZD6563 (5 μg or 10 μg) or the licensed BNT162b2 XBB.1.5 mRNA vaccine (30 μg). Results AZD6563 drove spike-specific CD4+ and CD8+ T cell responses comparable to those elicited by higher dose of BNT162b2. Functional analysis of these cells revealed similar cytokine production profiles across groups; however, the 10μg dose of AZD6563 led to higher TCR diversity within the ≥65-year cohort. Notably, expansion of spike-specific B cells was most pronounced in the AZD6563 10μg group, with marked increases in cross-reactive XBB.1.5 spike-specific B cells that also recognized Omicron BA.4/5 and ancestral SARS-CoV-2 variants. Conclusion Collectively, these results demonstrate that AZD6563, at reduced doses, matches the cellular immunogenicity of BNT162b2 while enhancing B cell cross-reactivity and TCR diversity in older adults, supporting its potential as a next-generation COVID-19 vaccine candidate. Funding Source n/a Topic Categories Vaccines and Immunotherapy (VAC)
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
mRNA-encoded virus-like particles (VLPs) are an emerging advancement in vaccine technology, enabling the self-assembly of viral antigens into structures that closely resemble native viruses. This innovative approach to conventional mRNA vaccination may boost vaccine-induced adaptive immune responses and allow for reduced dosing.
Design and population
The phase 1 clinical study ARTEMIS-C was conducted to assess cellular immunogenicity in adults aged 18—64 years and ≥65 years following administration of AZD6563 (5 μg or 10 μg) or the licensed BNT162b2 XBB.1.5 mRNA vaccine (30 μg).
Core methods
To evaluate this technology, we developed AZD6563, an mRNA VLP vaccine targeting the COVID-19 XBB1.5 spike variant. The study assessed spike-specific CD4+ and CD8+ T cell responses and functional analysis of cytokine production profiles.
Main results
AZD6563 drove spike-specific CD4+ and CD8+ T cell responses comparable to those elicited by higher dose of BNT162b2. Functional analysis revealed similar cytokine production profiles across groups; however, the 10μg dose of AZD6563 led to higher TCR diversity within the ≥65-year cohort. Expansion of spike-specific B cells was most pronounced in the AZD6563 10μg group, with marked increases in cross-reactive XBB.1.5 spike-specific B cells that also recognized Omicron BA.4/5 and ancestral SARS-CoV-2 variants.
Interpretation and novelty
Collectively, these results demonstrate that AZD6563, at reduced doses, matches the cellular immunogenicity of BNT162b2 while enhancing B cell cross-reactivity and TCR diversity in older adults, supporting its potential as a next-generation COVID-19 vaccine candidate.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that AZD6563 could offer a more efficient vaccination strategy with lower doses while maintaining robust immune responses and potentially broader protection against multiple SARS-CoV-2 variants, which could improve vaccine accessibility and coverage.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was a phase 1 clinical trial with a limited sample size, and further research is needed to evaluate long-term immunity and efficacy in larger populations. Despite these limitations, the evidence strength is moderate due to the well-controlled design and direct comparison with a licensed vaccine.

血液、唾液和眼泪中抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgA 反应不一致 2261177

Discordant Anti-SARS-COV-2 IgA Responses In Blood, Saliva, and Tears 2261177
作者： Kyle McKenna, Hayden Elliot, Matthew Gambrel, Ella Pemberton, Mya Faulkner
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
To determine whether anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA responses in blood reflected the expression of anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA in mucosal tissues, anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies in blood plasma, tears and saliva were measured before and after SARS-CoV-2 exposure or COVID-19 vaccination.
设计与对象
Blood plasma was available from fourteen individuals before and after SARS-CoV-2 exposure or COVID-19 vaccination, with a larger cohort of 39 participants who were anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgG positive.
核心方法
使用Wuhan或Omicron RBD包被板通过间接ELISA测量血浆、眼泪和唾液中的抗严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgA抗体,添加特定稀释度的样本和用于浓度测定的单克隆抗体标准品。
主要结果
在所有个体中均观察到抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgG 抗体的血清转化,但 14 名个体中只有 6 名 (43%) 表现出抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgA 抗体;在较大的 队列研究 中,血液、唾液和泪液中分别有 16 例 (41%)、9 例 (23%) 和 11 例 (28%) 呈抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 RBD IgA 阳性。
解释与创新
作者解释说,血液、眼泪和唾液中的抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 IgA 反应随血液和粘膜组织中的不一致反应而变化,而双眼泪水中的等效抗体反应表明,产生抗体的 B 细胞在两个泪腺中均等播种。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,血液 IgA 水平可能无法可靠地预测粘膜 IgA 反应,这对 监测、疫苗接种 策略以及了解针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的粘膜免疫具有影响。
局限与证据强度
由于样本量较小且研究仅具有摘要性质,因此证据强度中等,这限制了对普遍性和潜在偏差的详细评估。

Discordant Anti-SARS-COV-2 IgA Responses In Blood, Saliva, and Tears 2261177

Authors: Kyle McKenna, Hayden Elliot, Matthew Gambrel, Ella Pemberton, Mya Faulkner
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Introduction To determine whether anti-SARS CoV-2 IgA responses in blood reflected the expression of anti-SARS CoV-2 IgA in mucosal tissues, anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies in blood plasma, tears and saliva were measured by indirect ELISA before and after SARS-CoV-2 exposure or COVID-19 vaccination. Methods SARS-CoV-2 RBD of Wuhan (R&D Systems; Cat #10534CV) or Omicron (Proteogenix; PX-CoV-P080) were used to coat ELISA plates. Blood, tear or saliva specimens were added at 1:100, 1:40, and 1:5 dilutions respectively. Human anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD (Wuhan) IgA monoclonal antibody (Proteogenix; Cat # PTXCOV-569) was used as a standard to determine the concentration of anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgA antibody . Results Blood plasma was available from fourteen individuals before and after SARS-CoV-2 exposure or COVID-19 vaccination. Seroconversion for anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgG antibodies was observed in all individuals whereas only six of fourteen (43%) demonstrated anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies. In a larger cohort of 39 participants that were anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgG positive, 16 (41%), 9 (23%), and 11(28%) were positive for anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgA in blood, saliva and tears respectively. Forty four percent of individuals positive for anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgA in blood were also IgA positive in saliva or tears. Thirty percent of individuals negative for anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgA in blood were IgA positive in saliva or tears. In a pilot study three individuals with anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies directed against Wuhan and Omicron variants demonstrated anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies in the tears from both eyes. Conclusion Our data indicate that anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA responses in blood, tears, and saliva are variable in SARS-CoV-2 exposed and COVID-19 vaccinated individuals with discordant responses in blood and mucosal tissues. Equivalent antibody responses in tears of both eyes suggests equal seeding of both lacrimal glands by antibody-producing B cells. Funding Source Franciscan Institute of Science and Health Topic Categories Mucosal and Regional Immunology (MUC)
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
To determine whether anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA responses in blood reflected the expression of anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA in mucosal tissues, anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies in blood plasma, tears and saliva were measured before and after SARS-CoV-2 exposure or COVID-19 vaccination.
Design and population
Blood plasma was available from fourteen individuals before and after SARS-CoV-2 exposure or COVID-19 vaccination, with a larger cohort of 39 participants who were anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgG positive.
Core methods
Anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies in blood plasma, tears and saliva were measured by indirect ELISA using Wuhan or Omicron RBD-coated plates, with specimens added at specific dilutions and a monoclonal antibody standard for concentration determination.
Main results
Seroconversion for anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgG antibodies was observed in all individuals, but only six of fourteen (43%) demonstrated anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA antibodies; in the larger cohort, 16 (41%), 9 (23%), and 11 (28%) were positive for anti-SARS-CoV-2 RBD IgA in blood, saliva and tears respectively.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that anti-SARS-CoV-2 IgA responses in blood, tears, and saliva are variable with discordant responses in blood and mucosal tissues, and equivalent antibody responses in tears of both eyes suggest equal seeding of both lacrimal glands by antibody-producing B cells.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform that mucosal IgA responses may not reliably be predicted by blood IgA levels, which has implications for surveillance, vaccination strategies, and understanding mucosal immunity against SARS-CoV-2.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the small sample size and the abstract-only nature of the study, which limits detailed assessment of generalizability and potential biases.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 mRNA 疫苗接种 诱导 HIV 感染者产生中和抗体和 I 型 IFN 变化。

SARS-CoV-2 mRNA Vaccination Induces Neutralizing Antibodies and Type I IFN Changes in People Living With HIV.
作者： Federica Frasca, Luca Maddaloni, Alessandra D'Auria, Matteo Fracella, Ginevra Bugani, Carmen Falvino, Valentina Baccolini, Giuseppe Migliara, Francesca Aloisi, Letizia Santinelli
摘要中文翻译本研究检查了HIV感染者(PLWH)接种BNT162b2 疫苗后抗刺突蛋白(抗-S)抗体(Abs)和I型干扰素(IFN-I)的变化,并分析了人口统计学和免疫学因素的影响。总共对75名HIV感染者和28名健康捐赠者在基线(T0)、第二剂接种时(T1)、第二剂接种后(T2)以及1年后(T3)进行了随访。抗-S抗体通过化学发光和基于水泡性口炎病毒(VSV)的假病毒中和试验进行评估,而IFN-α2、IFN-β和IFN-ω的mRNA水平通过RT-实时PCR测量。HIV感染者显示抗-S免疫球蛋白G(IgG)水平增加,与健康捐赠者相当(p < 0.001),并且抗-S中和抗体水平升高(T2 vs. T0,p < 0.014)。年龄、性别、CD4+ T细胞计数、联合抗逆转录病毒疗法(cART)暴露和T0时的IFN-I水平不影响抗-S IgG的产生。IFN-I基因表达显示时间变化,在T2时降低(p < 0.01),随后在T3时升高(IFN-α2和IFN-ω,p < 0.001)。多变量模型显示,除IFN-β外,IFN-I反应随时间无整体变化,IFN-β在T3时低于T1(p = 0.032)。CD4+ T细胞计数与IFN-I反应呈正相关(p < 0.05)。这些结果表明,mRNA 疫苗接种可以在HIV感染者中诱导有效的抗-S反应并调节IFN-β基因表达,CD4+ T细胞计数是疫苗诱导的IFN变化的关键决定因素。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究检查了 HIV 感染者 (PLWH) 接受 BNT162b2 疫苗 治疗后抗 Spike 抗体和 I 型干扰素的变化,并分析了人口和免疫因素的影响。
设计与对象
总共对75名HIV感染者和28名健康捐赠者在基线(T0)、第二剂接种时(T1)、第二剂接种后(T2)以及1年后(T3)进行了随访。
核心方法
通过化学发光和基于水泡性口炎病毒的假病毒中和测定来评估抗S抗体,同时通过RT-实时PCR测量IFN-α2、IFN-β和IFN-ω mRNA水平。
主要结果
与健康捐赠者相比,PLWH 的抗 S 免疫球蛋白 G 水平有所增加 (p < 0.001),并诱导产生抗 S 中和抗体 (T2 与 T2 相比,p < 0.014)。IFN-I 基因表达显示时间变化,T2 时下降(p < 0.01),随后 T3 时增加(对于 IFN-α2 和 IFN-ω,p < 0.001)。多变量模型显示 IFN-I 反应随时间推移没有总体变化,但 IFN-β 除外,T3 时的反应低于 T1 时的反应 (p = 0.032)。
解释与创新
这些结果表明,mRNA 疫苗 可以引发有效的抗 S 反应并调节 PLWH 中的 IFN-β 基因表达,其中 CD4+ T 细胞计数是 疫苗 诱导的 IFN 变化的关键决定因素。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为 疫苗 的 PLWH 策略提供了信息,证明了有效的抗体反应,并强调了 CD4+ T 细胞计数在调节干扰素反应中的作用,这可能会影响 疫苗 的长期疗效和免疫监测。
局限与证据强度
尽管纵向评估和多变量分析为 PLWH 中的 疫苗 反应提供了强有力的见解,但由于观察设计和有限的样本量,证据强度中等。

SARS-CoV-2 mRNA Vaccination Induces Neutralizing Antibodies and Type I IFN Changes in People Living With HIV.

Authors: Federica Frasca, Luca Maddaloni, Alessandra D'Auria, Matteo Fracella, Ginevra Bugani, Carmen Falvino, Valentina Baccolini, Giuseppe Migliara, Francesca Aloisi, Letizia Santinelli
Original abstract (English)
This study examined changes in anti-Spike (anti-S) antibodies (Abs) and type I interferon (IFN-I) following the BNT162b2 vaccine in people living with HIV (PLWH) and analyzed the impact of demographic and immunological factors. In total, 75 PLWH and 28 healthy donors were followed at baseline (T0), at the second dose (T1), after the second dose (T2), and more than 1 year later (T3). Anti-S Abs were assessed by chemiluminescence and vesicular stomatitis virus (VSV)-based pseudo virus-neutralization assay, while IFN-α2, IFN-β, and IFN-ω mRNA levels were measured by RT-Real Time PCR. PLWH showed an increase in anti-S Immunoglobulin G (IgG) levels comparable to healthy donors (p < 0.001) and an induction of anti-S neutralizing Abs (p < 0.014 for T2 vs. Age, gender, CD4+ T cell count, exposure to combined antiretroviral therapy (cART) and IFN-I levels at T0 did not affect the anti-S IgG production. IFN-I gene expression showed temporal changes, with a decrease at T2 (p < 0.01) and a subsequent increase at T3 (p < 0.001, for IFN-α2 and IFN-ω). A multivariable model revealed no overall change in the IFN-I response over time, except for IFN-β, which was lower at T3 than at T1 (p = 0.032). CD4+ T cell count was positively correlated with the IFN-I response (p < 0.05). These results suggest that mRNA vaccination can elicit an effective anti-S response and modulate the IFN-β gene expression in PLWH, with CD4+ T cell count being a key determinant of vaccine-induced changes in IFN.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study examined changes in anti-Spike antibodies and type I interferon following the BNT162b2 vaccine in people living with HIV (PLWH) and analyzed the impact of demographic and immunological factors.
Design and population
In total, 75 PLWH and 28 healthy donors were followed at baseline (T0), at the second dose (T1), after the second dose (T2), and more than 1 year later (T3).
Core methods
Anti-S antibodies were assessed by chemiluminescence and vesicular stomatitis virus-based pseudo virus-neutralization assay, while IFN-α2, IFN-β, and IFN-ω mRNA levels were measured by RT-Real Time PCR.
Main results
PLWH showed an increase in anti-S Immunoglobulin G levels comparable to healthy donors (p < 0.001) and an induction of anti-S neutralizing antibodies (p < 0.014 for T2 vs.). IFN-I gene expression showed temporal changes, with a decrease at T2 (p < 0.01) and a subsequent increase at T3 (p < 0.001, for IFN-α2 and IFN-ω). A multivariable model revealed no overall change in the IFN-I response over time, except for IFN-β, which was lower at T3 than at T1 (p = 0.032).
Interpretation and novelty
These results suggest that mRNA vaccination can elicit an effective anti-S response and modulate the IFN-β gene expression in PLWH, with CD4+ T cell count being a key determinant of vaccine-induced changes in IFN.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform vaccine strategies for PLWH, demonstrating effective antibody responses and highlighting the role of CD4+ T cell count in modulating interferon responses, which may impact long-term vaccine efficacy and immune monitoring.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the observational design and limited sample size, though the longitudinal assessment and multivariable analysis provide robust insights into vaccine responses in PLWH.

Journal: Suicide and Life-Threatening Behaviour  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-01  |  DOI: 10.1111/sltb.70132  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 56, 4, e70132

Factors Associated With Job Precariousness and Material Deprivation, and Their Association With Mental Health During the COVID-19 Pandemic.

Factors Associated With Job Precariousness and Material Deprivation, and Their Association With Mental Health During the COVID-19 Pandemic.
作者： Sheila Lopez-Romeo, Pere Castellvi, Isabel Feria-Raposo, Andrea Miranda-Mendizabal, Silvia Recoder, Marc Casajuana-Closas, Susana Subira-Alvarez, Carlos G Forero, P. Castellví, S. Subirà-Álvarez
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study addressed how pre-existing mental health conditions influenced socio-economic adversity during the first year of the COVID-19 pandemic and whether resilience moderated mental health changes. It aimed to identify whether anxiety, depression, or suicide-related symptoms predicted job precariousness and material deprivation.
设计与对象
在两个时间点对西班牙成年人的全国代表性纵向 队列研究 进行了评估:2019 年 11 月(大流行前)和 2020 年 11 月至 12 月(大流行期间)。在基线时测量重度抑郁发作、广泛性焦虑症以及自杀想法和行为,并在随访时评估工作不稳定和物质剥夺。
核心方法
使用加权多变量回归模型来测试大流行前的心理健康状况与随后的社会经济逆境之间的关联,以及逆境对心理健康症状变化的影响。在这些模型中,复原力被作为一个交互项进行了检验。
主要结果
大流行前的广泛性焦虑症以及自杀想法和行为与大流行期间工作不稳定的可能性较高有关,而重度抑郁发作在调整后并不显着。工作不稳定与抑郁、焦虑、自杀想法和行为症状的小幅减少有关,而物质匮乏则与抑郁症状的增加有关。较高的复原力减弱了贫困与抑郁之间的关联。
解释与创新
作者得出的结论是,预先存在的焦虑和自杀相关症状可能会导致个体在社会经济冲击期间面临更大的工作不稳定风险。他们强调了复原力在减轻物质匮乏对心理健康造成的不利影响方面的潜在保护作用。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,解决物质匮乏问题和增强复原力可以减少危机期间的下游抑郁负担。他们还暗示,心理健康筛查可能有助于识别在大流行背景下面临社会经济逆境风险的个人。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖于自我报告的心理健康和社会经济逆境测量结果,这可能会引入偏见。纵向设计和全国代表性抽样强化了证据,但对其他情况的普遍适用性仍不确定。

Factors Associated With Job Precariousness and Material Deprivation, and Their Association With Mental Health During the COVID-19 Pandemic.

Authors: Sheila Lopez-Romeo, Pere Castellvi, Isabel Feria-Raposo, Andrea Miranda-Mendizabal, Silvia Recoder, Marc Casajuana-Closas, Susana Subira-Alvarez, Carlos G Forero, P. Castellví, S. Subirà-Álvarez
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Introduction COVID‐19 severely disrupted labor markets and living conditions, intensifying job precariousness and material deprivation. We examined whether pre‐pandemic mental health conditions predicted socio‐economic adversity during the first pandemic year and whether resilience buffered changes in mental health. Methods We analyzed a nationally representative longitudinal cohort of Spanish adults assessed in two assessments: November 2019 (T0, pre‐pandemic) and November–December 2020 (T1). Major depressive episode (MDE), generalized anxiety disorder (GAD), and suicidal thoughts and behaviors (STB) were assessed at T0; job precariousness and material deprivation were measured at T1. Weighted multivariable regression models tested (i) associations between T0 mental health and T1 socio‐economic adversity and (ii) associations between T1 adversity and changes in mental health symptoms, including resilience interactions. Results Pre‐pandemic GAD and STB were associated with higher odds of job precariousness during the pandemic, whereas MDE was not significant after adjustment. Job precariousness was associated with small reductions in depressive, anxiety, and STB symptoms, while material deprivation was associated with increased depressive symptoms; higher resilience attenuated the deprivation–depression association. Conclusions Pre‐existing anxiety and suicide‐related symptoms may identify individuals more likely to report job precariousness during socio‐economic shocks. Preventing material deprivation and strengthening resilience may help reduce downstream depressive burden during crises.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed how pre-existing mental health conditions influenced socio-economic adversity during the first year of the COVID-19 pandemic and whether resilience moderated mental health changes. It aimed to identify whether anxiety, depression, or suicide-related symptoms predicted job precariousness and material deprivation.
Design and population
A nationally representative longitudinal cohort of Spanish adults was assessed at two time points: November 2019 (pre-pandemic) and November–December 2020 (during the pandemic). Major depressive episode, generalized anxiety disorder, and suicidal thoughts and behaviors were measured at baseline, while job precariousness and material deprivation were evaluated at follow-up.
Core methods
Weighted multivariable regression models were used to test associations between pre-pandemic mental health conditions and subsequent socio-economic adversity, as well as the impact of adversity on changes in mental health symptoms. Resilience was examined as an interaction term in these models.
Main results
Pre-pandemic generalized anxiety disorder and suicidal thoughts and behaviors were associated with higher odds of job precariousness during the pandemic, whereas major depressive episode was not significant after adjustment. Job precariousness was linked to small reductions in depressive, anxiety, and suicidal thoughts and behaviors symptoms, while material deprivation was associated with increased depressive symptoms. Higher resilience attenuated the association between deprivation and depression.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that pre-existing anxiety and suicide-related symptoms may identify individuals at greater risk of job precariousness during socio-economic shocks. They highlighted the potential protective role of resilience in mitigating the adverse mental health effects of material deprivation.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that addressing material deprivation and strengthening resilience could reduce downstream depressive burden during crises. They also imply that mental health screening may help identify individuals at risk of socio-economic adversity in pandemic contexts.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relied on self-reported measures of mental health and socio-economic adversity, which may introduce bias. The longitudinal design and nationally representative sampling strengthen the evidence, but generalizability to other contexts remains uncertain.

Anti-PEG Antibodies From mRNA COVID-19 Vaccines Affect In Vitro Measurements of Pegylated Drug Levels.

Anti-PEG Antibodies From mRNA COVID-19 Vaccines Affect In Vitro Measurements of Pegylated Drug Levels.
作者： Elizaveta A Svyatova, Zhuoming Liu, Robyn E Becker, Jungeun M Sung, Adetunji P Fayomi, Jeffry A Florian, James L Weaver, Rodney Rouse, Kristina E Howard
摘要中文翻译最近采用基于 mRNA 的技术进行 疫苗 开发,导致人们广泛接触新的 疫苗 成分,例如聚乙二醇 (PEG),可以针对其制造抗体。本研究评估了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 后人血清中抗 PEG 抗体的存在,以及这些抗体是否会干扰含 PEG 药物的药物水平评估。与对照样品相比,在接受 mRNA-1273 疫苗 的个体中检测到抗 PEG 抗体滴度升高且 患病率 延长。在大约 33% 的评估的 mRNA-1273 疫苗 受体中,疫苗接种 后的抗 PEG 抗体水平比 疫苗接种 前高约 10 倍。抗 PEG 抗体水平升高持续超过 6 个月。接受 BNT162b2 或 Ad26.COV2.S 疫苗 的患者的血清具有与对照样品相似的抗 PEG 抗体水平。无论研究组如何,来自抗 PEG 抗体升高的个体的血清通常显示聚乙二醇化药物聚乙二醇非格司亭的检测量减少。这项研究表明,在聚乙二醇化药品的开发过程中,以及在生物仿制药产品开发过程中建立与聚乙二醇化参考产品的可比性期间,抗 PEG 抗体有可能干扰用于药代动力学药物测量的生物分析测定。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
这项研究需要解决基于 mRNA 的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗 产生的抗 PEG 抗体是否会干扰聚乙二醇化药物水平的体外测量的科学问题。目的是调查这种潜在的干扰。
设计与对象
本研究评估了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 后人血清中抗 PEG 抗体的存在,并将 疫苗 接受者与对照样本进行比较。该研究包括接受 mRNA-1273 疫苗 的个体和接受其他 疫苗(例如 BNT162b2 或 Ad26.COV2.S)的个体。
核心方法
该研究使用生物分析测定了 疫苗接种 前后收集的血清样本中的抗 PEG 抗体水平。它还评估了这些抗体对血清样本中聚乙二醇化药物聚乙二醇非格司亭检测的影响。
主要结果
与对照样本相比,在接受 mRNA-1273 疫苗 的个体中检测到抗 PEG 抗体滴度升高且 患病率 延长。在大约 33% 的评估的 mRNA-1273 疫苗 受体中,疫苗接种 后的抗 PEG 抗体水平比 疫苗接种 前高约 10 倍。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,抗 PEG 抗体会干扰用于药代动力学药物测量的生物分析测定,这对聚乙二醇化药品的开发和可比性具有影响。
科研与公卫意义
结果警告,抗 PEG 抗体可能会影响接种基于 mRNA 的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗 的个体药物水平评估的准确性,这对于药物开发和临床监测非常重要。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外测量,并未考虑临床或现实世界的影响。证据是温和的,因为它基于一项特别关注抗体水平和药物检测的研究。

Anti-PEG Antibodies From mRNA COVID-19 Vaccines Affect In Vitro Measurements of Pegylated Drug Levels.

Authors: Elizaveta A Svyatova, Zhuoming Liu, Robyn E Becker, Jungeun M Sung, Adetunji P Fayomi, Jeffry A Florian, James L Weaver, Rodney Rouse, Kristina E Howard
Original abstract (English)
The recent adoption of mRNA-based technology for vaccine development has led to widespread exposure to new vaccine components, such as polyethylene glycol (PEG), against which antibodies may be made. This study assesses the presence of anti-PEG antibodies in human serum following SARS-CoV-2 vaccination, and if these antibodies could interfere with drug level assessment of PEG containing drugs. Elevated anti-PEG antibody titers with prolonged prevalence were detected in individuals who received the mRNA-1273 vaccine as compared to control samples. Anti-PEG antibody levels were approximately 10-fold higher post-vaccination compared to pre-vaccination in approximately 33% of assessed mRNA-1273 vaccine recipients. Elevated anti-PEG antibody levels persisted for over 6 months. Serum from those who received the BNT162b2 or Ad26.COV2.S vaccines had anti-PEG antibody levels similar to control samples. Serum from individuals with elevated anti-PEG antibodies, regardless of study group, typically showed decreased detection of the pegylated drug, pegfilgrastim. This study demonstrates that anti-PEG antibodies have the potential to interfere with bioanalytical assays used for pharmacokinetic drug measurement during the development of pegylated pharmaceutical products, as well as during the establishment of comparability to pegylated reference products during development of biosimilar drug products.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study was needed to address the scientific problem of whether anti-PEG antibodies generated by mRNA-based SARS-CoV-2 vaccines could interfere with in vitro measurements of pegylated drug levels. The objective was to investigate this potential interference.
Design and population
This study assessed the presence of anti-PEG antibodies in human serum following SARS-CoV-2 vaccination, comparing vaccine recipients to control samples. The study included individuals who received the mRNA-1273 vaccine and those who received other vaccines such as BNT162b2 or Ad26.COV2.S.
Core methods
The study measured anti-PEG antibody levels in serum samples collected pre- and post-vaccination using bioanalytical assays. It also evaluated the effect of these antibodies on the detection of pegfilgrastim, a pegylated drug, in serum samples.
Main results
Elevated anti-PEG antibody titers with prolonged prevalence were detected in individuals who received the mRNA-1273 vaccine compared to control samples. Anti-PEG antibody levels were approximately 10-fold higher post-vaccination compared to pre-vaccination in approximately 33% of assessed mRNA-1273 vaccine recipients.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings demonstrate that anti-PEG antibodies can interfere with bioanalytical assays used for pharmacokinetic drug measurement, which has implications for the development and comparability of pegylated pharmaceutical products.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results caution against the potential for anti-PEG antibodies to affect the accuracy of drug level assessments in individuals vaccinated with mRNA-based SARS-CoV-2 vaccines, which is important for drug development and clinical monitoring.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro measurements and does not account for clinical or real-world implications. The evidence is moderate as it is based on a single study with a specific focus on antibody levels and drug detection.

Impact of Non-Pharmaceutical Interventions Targeted at COVID-19 Pandemic on Influenza Burden-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.

Impact of Non-Pharmaceutical Interventions Targeted at COVID-19 Pandemic on Influenza Burden-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.
作者： Laura-Inés Böhler, Lisa Koeppel, Stefan Fabian Weber, Mary Gaeddert, Katharina Thielemann, Kerstin Glaser, Horeya M Ismail, Ulrich Reinacher, Veronika K. Jaeger, Julia Böhnke
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review examines the impact of non-pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs) implemented during the COVID-19 pandemic on the global burden of seasonal influenza. The study focuses on quantifying changes in influenza incidence between pre-pandemic and intra-pandemic periods across diverse populations and settings.
证据基础与方法
根据 RESPINOW 项目中的 PRISMA 指南进行了系统回顾和荟萃分析。此次检索筛选了 20,676 条记录,最终包括来自 98 个国家的 115 项研究,这些研究报告了大流行之前和期间的绝对流感病例数。数据提取和质量评估使用修改后的 NHLBI 工具进行前后研究,通过报告周期长度和使用 发病率 比率估计的相对变化来标准化流感病例。
共识与结论
The COVID-19 pandemic was associated with an unprecedented global reduction in influenza incidence, with a decline of −92% (95% CI: −94 to −90). Reductions were observed across all transmission zones and WHO regions, with larger decreases in high-income countries (−96%, 95% CI: −98 to −94) compared to low-income settings (−82%, 95% CI: −88 to −73). Age-specific declines were smaller among young children (<6 years: −66%) than adults aged 18–64 years (−80%), and influenza A appeared to decline more strongly than influenza B.
争议与缺口
该审查强调了数据的局限性以及需要强大的流行病学指标,以更好地了解 NPI 对流感的影响。虽然观察到了地理差异(例如,一些国家几乎被淘汰,而韩国为-61%),但造成这些差异的原因仍不清楚。研究还指出,对幼儿的影响不如其他年龄组明显,值得进一步调查。
科研与实践启示
需要加强、综合的全球 监测 系统来为未来的大流行应对措施提供信息,并更好地评估公共卫生干预措施的影响。研究结果强调了制定强有力的流行病学指标来监测大流行期间和之后呼吸道病毒传播的重要性,特别是在数据可能有限的低收入环境中。

Impact of Non-Pharmaceutical Interventions Targeted at COVID-19 Pandemic on Influenza Burden-A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.

Authors: Laura-Inés Böhler, Lisa Koeppel, Stefan Fabian Weber, Mary Gaeddert, Katharina Thielemann, Kerstin Glaser, Horeya M Ismail, Ulrich Reinacher, Veronika K. Jaeger, Julia Böhnke
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Seasonal influenza imposes a substantial global health burden. The COVID‐19 pandemic, accompanied by widespread non‐pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs), profoundly disrupted respiratory virus circulation, offering a unique opportunity to assess their broader epidemiological impact. This study aimed to quantify changes in influenza burden between the pre‐pandemic and intra‐pandemic periods. Within the RESPINOW project, we conducted a systematic review following PRISMA guidelines. Studies reporting absolute influenza case counts before and during the pandemic were included. Data extraction and quality assessment were performed using a modified NHLBI tool for before–after studies. Influenza cases were normalized by reporting period length, and relative changes were estimated using incidence rate ratios. Subgroup analyses explored age, setting, hemisphere, human development index, influenza transmission zones, WHO regions, and viral strains. Of 20,676 screened records, 115 studies from 98 countries were included. Globally, influenza incidence declined by −92% (95% CI: −94 to −90) following the onset of the pandemic. Reductions varied geographically, ranging from near‐elimination in several countries to more modest declines (e.g., South Korea: −61%, 95% CI: −79 to −26). Decreases were observed across all transmission zones and WHO regions, with descriptively larger reductions observed in high‐income countries (−96%, 95% CI: −98 to −94) than in low‐income settings (−82%, 95% CI: −88 to −73). Age‐specific declines appeared smaller among young children (< 6 years: −66%, 95% CI: −79 to −45) compared with adults aged 18–64 years (−80%, 95% CI: −90 to −63). Influenza A appeared to decline more strongly than influenza B. The COVID‐19 pandemic was associated with an unprecedented global reduction in influenza incidence. Data limitations and the need for robust epidemiological indicators highlight the importance of strengthened, integrated global surveillance to inform future pandemic responses.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines the impact of non-pharmaceutical interventions (NPIs) implemented during the COVID-19 pandemic on the global burden of seasonal influenza. The study focuses on quantifying changes in influenza incidence between pre-pandemic and intra-pandemic periods across diverse populations and settings.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and meta-analysis was conducted following PRISMA guidelines within the RESPINOW project. The search screened 20,676 records, ultimately including 115 studies from 98 countries that reported absolute influenza case counts before and during the pandemic. Data extraction and quality assessment used a modified NHLBI tool for before-after studies, with influenza cases normalized by reporting period length and relative changes estimated using incidence rate ratios.
Consensus and conclusions
The COVID-19 pandemic was associated with an unprecedented global reduction in influenza incidence, with a decline of −92% (95% CI: −94 to −90). Reductions were observed across all transmission zones and WHO regions, with larger decreases in high-income countries (−96%, 95% CI: −98 to −94) compared to low-income settings (−82%, 95% CI: −88 to −73). Age-specific declines were smaller among young children (<6 years: −66%) than adults aged 18–64 years (−80%), and influenza A appeared to decline more strongly than influenza B.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights data limitations and the need for robust epidemiological indicators to better understand the impact of NPIs on influenza. While geographic variations were observed (e.g., near-elimination in some countries versus −61% in South Korea), the reasons for these differences remain unclear. The study also noted that the impact on young children was less pronounced than in other age groups, warranting further investigation.
Research and practice implications
Strengthened, integrated global surveillance systems are needed to inform future pandemic responses and better assess the impact of public health interventions. The findings underscore the importance of developing robust epidemiological indicators to monitor respiratory virus circulation during and after pandemics, particularly in low-income settings where data may be limited.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.
作者： Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, A. D’Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina García-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注抗 Spike 单克隆抗体 (mAb) 治疗后持续性病毒血症的免疫功能低下患者中的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,检查恢复期血浆 (CCP) 是否恢复病毒清除。
证据基础与方法
进行了系统回顾和个体参与者数据荟萃分析,检索了2020年1月至2026年1月的电子数据库,纳入47个案例(30个已发表,17个未发表)。
共识与结论
在 70.2% 的单克隆抗体耐药感染的免疫功能低下患者中,CCP 输注与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 清除率相关,输注的总 CCP 体积与清除率呈正相关。
争议与缺口
证据基础仅限于 47 个病例,并且该研究并未解决潜在的混杂因素或病毒清除之外的长期结果。
科研与实践启示
对于单克隆抗体耐药性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的免疫功能低下患者,优先考虑 CCP 作为 治疗 选项,并注意优化输血量。

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Authors: Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, A. D’Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina García-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
Original abstract (English)
Anti-Spike monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) progressively lost efficacy during the COVID-19 pandemic due to the emergence and predominance of resistant SARS-CoV-2 variants. By contrast, high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) collected from vaccinated donors recently recovered from infection provides a polyclonal source of antibodies that remains effective in clearing SARS-CoV-2 in immunosuppressed patients, unable to mount an adequate immune response against the virus. We conducted a systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis to examine the effect of CCP in immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following mAb therapy, and to evaluate possible biological factors associated with a favourable outcome. Electronic databases were searched for studies published from January 2020 to January 2026. All studies including eligible cases were considered in the systematic review. Unpublished cases were also collected from investigators of the selected studies. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 cases from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included. In 33 out of 47 patients (70.2%) with a mAb-resistant infection, SARS-CoV-2 clearance occurred following CCP transfusion, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. In logistic regression, total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance. In conclusion, CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in persistently positive immunocompromised patients following failure of anti-Spike mAb therapy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on SARS-CoV-2 in immunocompromised patients with persistent viraemia following anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, examining whether convalescent plasma (CCP) restores viral clearance.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis was conducted, searching electronic databases from January 2020 to January 2026, with 47 cases (30 published, 17 unpublished) included.
Consensus and conclusions
CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in 70.2% of immunocompromised patients with mAb-resistant infections, with total CCP volume transfused positively associated with clearance.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence base is limited to 47 cases, and the study does not address potential confounding factors or long-term outcomes beyond viral clearance.
Research and practice implications
Prioritizing CCP as a treatment option for immunocompromised patients with mAb-resistant SARS-CoV-2 infections, with attention to optimizing transfusion volume.

Effectiveness, Safety, and Immunogenicity of a Second COVID-19 Vaccine Booster in the Elderly: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis Protocol.

Effectiveness, Safety, and Immunogenicity of a Second COVID-19 Vaccine Booster in the Elderly: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis Protocol.
作者： Marieli Thomazini Piske Garcia, Valeria Valim, Luís Carlos Lopes-Júnior
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This systematic review and meta-analysis protocol focuses on SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) and evaluates the effectiveness, safety, and immunogenicity of a second COVID-19 vaccine booster dose in older adults aged ≥ 60 years worldwide.
证据基础与方法
审查将包括来自 PubMed/MEDLINE、EMBASE、Cochrane Library、Web of Science、Scopus 和 LILACS 的随机对照试验和非随机临床试验 (NRCT),无语言或日期限制。研究选择将遵循 PRISMA-2020 指南,使用 RoB 2 和 ROBINS-I 工具评估偏倚风险,并使用 GRADE 方法评估证据的确定性。
共识与结论
The review aims to provide comprehensive evidence regarding second COVID-19 booster doses in older adults to support evidence-based public health strategies and vaccination policies for high-risk populations.
争议与缺口
Evidence regarding the effectiveness, safety, and immunogenicity of a second COVID-19 vaccine booster dose in older adults remains fragmented, with no specific controversies or gaps mentioned in the protocol.
科研与实践启示
研究结果可能支持针对高危人群(包括老年人)的基于证据的公共卫生策略和 疫苗接种 政策。

Effectiveness, Safety, and Immunogenicity of a Second COVID-19 Vaccine Booster in the Elderly: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis Protocol.

Authors: Marieli Thomazini Piske Garcia, Valeria Valim, Luís Carlos Lopes-Júnior
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background/Aims Older adults remain highly vulnerable to severe COVID‐19 outcomes due to immunosenescence and comorbidities. Although booster vaccination strategies have reduced hospitalizations and mortality, evidence regarding the effectiveness, safety, and immunogenicity of a second COVID‐19 vaccine booster dose in older adults remains fragmented. This systematic review and meta‐analysis protocol aims to critically evaluate the available evidence from randomized and nonrandomized clinical trials (NRCTs) regarding the effectiveness, safety, and immunogenicity of the second COVID‐19 vaccine booster in older adults worldwide. Methods A systematic review of clinical trials will be conducted using PubMed/MEDLINE, EMBASE, Cochrane Library, Web of Science, Scopus, and LILACS, without restrictions on publication date or language. Eligible studies will include randomized controlled trials and NRCTs involving older adults aged ≥ 60 years who received a second COVID‐19 vaccine booster dose. Primary outcomes will include measures related to effectiveness, safety, and/or immunogenicity. Study selection will follow PRISMA‐2020 guidelines. Risk of bias will be assessed using RoB 2 and ROBINS‐I tools, while certainty of evidence will be evaluated using the GRADE approach. Results A narrative synthesis will be performed, and meta‐analysis will be conducted whenever methodologically appropriate. Conclusion This review will provide comprehensive evidence regarding second COVID‐19 booster doses in older adults and may support evidence‐based public health strategies and vaccination policies for high‐risk populations. Trial Registration PROSPERO registration number: CRD42025644411.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review and meta-analysis protocol focuses on SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) and evaluates the effectiveness, safety, and immunogenicity of a second COVID-19 vaccine booster dose in older adults aged ≥ 60 years worldwide.
Evidence base and method
The review will include randomized controlled trials and nonrandomized clinical trials (NRCTs) from PubMed/MEDLINE, EMBASE, Cochrane Library, Web of Science, Scopus, and LILACS without language or date restrictions. Study selection will follow PRISMA-2020 guidelines, with risk of bias assessed using RoB 2 and ROBINS-I tools, and certainty of evidence evaluated using the GRADE approach.
Consensus and conclusions
The review aims to provide comprehensive evidence regarding second COVID-19 booster doses in older adults to support evidence-based public health strategies and vaccination policies for high-risk populations.
Controversies and gaps
Evidence regarding the effectiveness, safety, and immunogenicity of a second COVID-19 vaccine booster dose in older adults remains fragmented, with no specific controversies or gaps mentioned in the protocol.
Research and practice implications
The findings may support evidence-based public health strategies and vaccination policies for high-risk populations, including older adults.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.
作者： Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19, focusing on three interacting determinants: timing, antibody titre, and host characteristics. The central question is whether conflicting outcomes from large randomized trials can be reconciled through these factors.
证据基础与方法
进行了设计分层叙述综合的系统评价(PRISMA 2020;SWiM;OSF:https://osf.io/tjcuq)。检索截至 2026 年 5 月的 PubMed、Web of Science 和 Scopus。使用标准化 91 场提取矩阵纳入了 31 项研究(20,793 名患者;8 项随机对照试验,23 项观察性研究)。
共识与结论
CP 仅在特定条件下有效:早期给药、足够的抗体滴度以及用于尚未产生内源性抗体反应的血清阴性或免疫功能低下的 宿主。在随机对照试验中,6 项中的 4 项发现较早的时机获益,4 项中的 3 项发现较高滴度的 CP,两项大型随机对照试验 (n = 12,522) 显示对血清阴性受者有优先益处。模型估计表明,如果早期部署,CP 在大流行的第一个年就挽救了美国数万人的生命。
争议与缺口
大型随机试验的相互矛盾的结果最初导致建议反对常规使用 CP。对非 IgG 免疫球蛋白的评估存在显着差距,31 项研究中只有 2 项报告了 IgA 或 IgM 数据,尽管有证据表明它们对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 具有有效的中和活性。
科研与实践启示
研究结果将 CP 疗效重新定义为一个条件问题,为未来大流行中精确部署被动免疫疗法提供了模板。需要对 CP 产品中的抗体类别(IgA、IgM)进行系统评估,以优化治疗效果。

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Authors: Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
Original abstract (English)
The efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19 has been contested, with large randomized trials producing conflicting results that led to recommendations against routine use. We performed a systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM; OSF: https://osf.io/tjcuq) to test whether these conflicting outcomes can be reconciled through three interacting determinants-timing, antibody titre, and host characteristics-using a standardized 91-field extraction matrix applied to each study. PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus were searched through May 2026. Thirty-one studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational) were included. The synthesis indicates that CP is effective, but only under a specific convergence of conditions: when administered early in the disease course, with sufficient antibody titre, to seronegative or immunocompromised hosts who have not yet mounted an endogenous antibody response. Among RCTs analysing timing, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier administration; for titre, 3 of 4 RCTs with explicit comparisons found benefit with higher-titre CP; and two large RCTs (combined n = 12,522) demonstrated preferential benefit in seronegative recipients. This interpretation is consistent with modelling estimates that CP, deployed largely to hospitalised patients, nonetheless saved tens of thousands of lives in the United States during the first pandemic year, an effect attributed to the subset treated early enough to modify disease progression. A second, previously unquantified finding concerns antibody class: characterisation of non-IgG immunoglobulins was nearly absent, with only 2 of 31 studies-both from our group-reporting IgA or IgM data, despite evidence that IgA and IgM are among the most potent neutralising classes against SARS-CoV-2. This hypothesis-generating observation identifies a systematic gap in CP product assessment. These findings reframe CP efficacy from a binary question to a conditional one and provide a template for the precision deployment of passive immunotherapy in future pandemics.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19, focusing on three interacting determinants: timing, antibody titre, and host characteristics. The central question is whether conflicting outcomes from large randomized trials can be reconciled through these factors.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM; OSF: https://osf.io/tjcuq) was conducted. PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus were searched through May 2026. Thirty-one studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational) were included, using a standardized 91-field extraction matrix.
Consensus and conclusions
CP is effective only under specific conditions: early administration, sufficient antibody titre, and use in seronegative or immunocompromised hosts who have not mounted an endogenous antibody response. Among RCTs, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier timing, 3 of 4 with higher-titre CP, and two large RCTs (n = 12,522) showed preferential benefit in seronegative recipients. Modelling estimates suggest CP saved tens of thousands of lives in the US during the first pandemic year when deployed early.
Controversies and gaps
Conflicting results from large randomized trials initially led to recommendations against routine CP use. A significant gap exists in the assessment of non-IgG immunoglobulins, with only 2 of 31 studies reporting IgA or IgM data, despite evidence of their potent neutralizing activity against SARS-CoV-2.
Research and practice implications
Findings reframe CP efficacy as a conditional question, providing a template for precision deployment of passive immunotherapy in future pandemics. There is a need for systematic assessment of antibody classes (IgA, IgM) in CP products to optimize therapeutic efficacy.

刚果民主共和国COVID‐19 疫苗的接种意愿与疫苗接种:一项横断面调查

Uptake of COVID‐19 Vaccines and Intention to Vaccinate in the Democratic Republic of the Congo: A Cross‐Sectional Survey
作者： Marc Bosonkie, Landry Egbende, Nuole Chen, Rawlance Ndejjo, Steven N. Kabwama, Berthold Bondo, Yves Kashiya, Marcel Mubelo, Lily L. Tsai, Mala Ali Mapatano
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines SARS-CoV-2 and COVID-19 vaccine uptake and intention to vaccinate in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, focusing on factors influencing vaccination behavior among the population.
证据基础与方法
该审查报告了 2022 年 4 月至 5 月在刚果民主共和国通过手机进行的横断面调查,包括 1075 名参与者,以 疫苗 的接种率和接种意愿作为主要结果变量。
共识与结论
The review concludes that older age, residence outside Kinshasa, perception of the Ministry of Health's competence in COVID-19 response, and reliance on health workers as information sources are associated with higher COVID-19 vaccine uptake.
争议与缺口
The review highlights suboptimal COVID-19 vaccine uptake in Sub-Saharan Africa as a persistent challenge, indicating a need for targeted strategies to address barriers to vaccination.
科研与实践启示
The review suggests that strategies to improve COVID-19 vaccine uptake should prioritize addressing demographic disparities, enhancing trust in health authorities, and leveraging trusted information sources such as health workers and radio.

Uptake of COVID‐19 Vaccines and Intention to Vaccinate in the Democratic Republic of the Congo: A Cross‐Sectional Survey

Authors: Marc Bosonkie, Landry Egbende, Nuole Chen, Rawlance Ndejjo, Steven N. Kabwama, Berthold Bondo, Yves Kashiya, Marcel Mubelo, Lily L. Tsai, Mala Ali Mapatano
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background and Aims COVID‐19 pandemic caused significant mortality and morbidity locally and globally. Vaccines were introduced as the most effective medical countermeasure against severe morbidity, hospitalization, and death. However, vaccine uptake remains suboptimal in many countries in Sub‐Saharan Africa. This study assessed uptake of COVID‐19 vaccines and intention to vaccinate and associated factors in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. Methods We carried out a cross‐sectional survey with data collected from April to May 2022 through mobile phones in the DRC among 1075 persons. Uptake of COVID‐19 vaccines and Intention to vaccinate were the main outcome variables. Results Of the participants, 89/1075 (8.3%) declared having received a full dose of the vaccine, and 38/1075 (3.4%) had received an incomplete dose. Results show that the uptake of the COVID‐19 vaccine was associated with being older (adjusted PR = 1.87; 95% CI: 1.05–3.27), living outside of Kinshasa (adjusted PR = 1.77; 95% CI: 1.24–2.53), perceiving the Ministry of Health as competent with regard to the COVID‐19 response (adjusted PR = 1.57; 95% CI: 1.05–2.35), and identifying health workers as a source of information for COVID‐19 (adjusted PR = 1.62; 95% CI: 2.34). Seven out of 10 respondents declared the intention to be vaccinated. Being female (adjusted PR = 0.83; 95% CI: 0.75–0.92), having a high perception in the competence of the MOH staff on COVID‐19 response (adjusted PR = 1.24; 95% CI: 1.13–1.36), and trusting radio as a source of information for COVID‐19 (adjusted PR = 1.11; 95% CI: 1.02–1.22) were factors associated with the intention for COVID‐19 vaccination. Conclusion Biological factors (age and sex), perceiving the MOH as competent with regard to the COVID‐19 and sources of COVID‐19 information (radio and health workers) must be taken into account for the development of strategies to improve the uptake of these interventions for COVID‐19 prevention and control.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines SARS-CoV-2 and COVID-19 vaccine uptake and intention to vaccinate in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, focusing on factors influencing vaccination behavior among the population.
Evidence base and method
The review reports a cross-sectional survey conducted via mobile phones in the DRC from April to May 2022, including 1075 participants, with vaccine uptake and intention to vaccinate as primary outcome variables.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that older age, residence outside Kinshasa, perception of the Ministry of Health's competence in COVID-19 response, and reliance on health workers as information sources are associated with higher COVID-19 vaccine uptake.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights suboptimal COVID-19 vaccine uptake in Sub-Saharan Africa as a persistent challenge, indicating a need for targeted strategies to address barriers to vaccination.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that strategies to improve COVID-19 vaccine uptake should prioritize addressing demographic disparities, enhancing trust in health authorities, and leveraging trusted information sources such as health workers and radio.

Evaluating the Effectiveness of Antivirals for COVID-19 in the Post-vaccine, Omicron Era: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis.

Evaluating the Effectiveness of Antivirals for COVID-19 in the Post-vaccine, Omicron Era: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis.
作者： Laura J Edwards, Bette Liu, James G Wood, Nicola Stephens, Allen C Cheng
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review evaluates the effectiveness of nirmatrelvir-ritonavir and molnupiravir against hospitalization and death in vaccinated populations during the post-Omicron era of COVID-19.
证据基础与方法
对接种成人疫苗的随机对照试验(自 2020 年 1 月起)和观察性研究(自 2022 年 1 月起)进行系统回顾和荟萃分析,搜索 PubMed、Embase 和 Cochrane 图书馆,并按年龄和研究质量进行随机效应荟萃分析和亚组分析。
共识与结论
在接种疫苗人群中进行的随机对照试验并未显示尼马曲韦-利托那韦或莫努匹拉韦可减少住院或死亡;观察性研究表明尼马瑞韦-利托那韦有益,但莫努匹拉韦没有益处;尼马瑞韦-利托那韦的随机对照试验的统计功效有限。
争议与缺口
molnupiravir 死亡率 的混合结果(OR = 0.61;95% CI .41-.91;HR = 0.65;95% CI .42-1.01);观察性研究显示尼马瑞韦-利托那韦有益处,但莫努匹拉韦没有;随机对照试验缺乏检测临床重要差异的能力。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步研究来阐明 抗病毒药物 在疫苗接种人群中的有效性;观察数据表明尼马瑞韦-利托那韦具有潜在益处,但需要证实。

Evaluating the Effectiveness of Antivirals for COVID-19 in the Post-vaccine, Omicron Era: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis.

Authors: Laura J Edwards, Bette Liu, James G Wood, Nicola Stephens, Allen C Cheng
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: Randomized controlled trials (RCTs) of oral antivirals for COVID-19 conducted early in the pandemic had favorable results in unvaccinated populations. We conducted a systematic review and meta-analysis of the effectiveness of nirmatrelvir-ritonavir and molnupiravir against hospitalization and death in vaccinated populations in the post-Omicron era. METHODS: We systematically searched PubMed, Embase and the Cochrane library for RCTs conducted from January 2020, and observational studies in adults who were vaccinated conducted from January 2022. We performed a random effects meta-analysis using the inverse variance method with subgroup analysis by age (<65 vs ≥65 years) and study quality (excluding vs including studies at serious risk of bias). RESULTS: We identified 43 studies (8 RCTs, 35 observational). Two RCTs were in vaccinated populations in which no association between treatment with nirmatrelvir-ritonavir and a reduction in death, or treatment with molnupiravir and a reduction in hospitalization or death, was observed. Molnupiravir was not associated with a significant reduction in hospitalization (n = 5; OR = 0.83; 95% CI .65-1.07) with mixed results for mortality (n = 9; OR = 0.61; 95% CI .41-.91; HR = 0.65; 95% CI .42-1.01). CONCLUSIONS: In vaccinated populations, RCTs have not demonstrated a reduction in hospitalization or death among populations treated with nirmatrelvir-ritonavir or with molnupiravir. However, RCTs of nirmatrelvir-ritonavir had limited statistical power to detect clinically important differences. Observational studies of nirmatrelvir-ritonavir, but not molnupiravir, consistently suggested a benefit against these outcomes. CLINICAL TRAIL REGISTRATION: PROSPERO (CRD420251266532).
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review evaluates the effectiveness of nirmatrelvir-ritonavir and molnupiravir against hospitalization and death in vaccinated populations during the post-Omicron era of COVID-19.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and meta-analysis of RCTs (from January 2020) and observational studies (from January 2022) in vaccinated adults, searching PubMed, Embase, and Cochrane library, with random-effects meta-analysis and subgroup analysis by age and study quality.
Consensus and conclusions
RCTs in vaccinated populations did not demonstrate reduced hospitalization or death with nirmatrelvir-ritonavir or molnupiravir; observational studies suggested nirmatrelvir-ritonavir benefit but not molnupiravir; RCTs for nirmatrelvir-ritonavir had limited statistical power.
Controversies and gaps
Mixed results for molnupiravir mortality (OR = 0.61; 95% CI .41-.91; HR = 0.65; 95% CI .42-1.01); observational studies showed benefit for nirmatrelvir-ritonavir but not molnupiravir; RCTs lacked power to detect clinically important differences.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to clarify antiviral effectiveness in vaccinated populations; observational data suggest potential benefit for nirmatrelvir-ritonavir but require confirmation.

Sociodemographic and Health-Risk Determinants of COVID-19 Vaccine Booster Preferences and Willingness to Pay in Singapore: Discrete Choice Experiment.

Sociodemographic and Health-Risk Determinants of COVID-19 Vaccine Booster Preferences and Willingness to Pay in Singapore: Discrete Choice Experiment.
作者： Hooi Swang Cheng, Yi Wang, Tan SHX, Ian Yi Han Ang
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This study focuses on COVID-19 vaccine booster preferences and willingness to pay in the general population of Singapore, aiming to identify sociodemographic and health-risk determinants influencing booster acceptance.
证据基础与方法
A discrete choice experiment survey was conducted among 1567 participants from an online panel in Singapore in June 2022, using a 2-stage design and mixed logit model to analyze preferences for booster characteristics and willingness to pay.
共识与结论
Respondents preferred boosters with longer protection duration, especially under higher COVID-19 risks, and were willing to pay S$1.43 per percentage-point increase in effectiveness and S$4.95 per additional month of protection. Sociodemographic factors like younger age, female gender, non-Chinese ethnicity, higher socioeconomic status, and chronic conditions were associated with higher booster acceptance.
争议与缺口
The study did not address potential regional variations within Singapore or explore the impact of prior COVID-19 infection on booster preferences beyond the identified negative association. Long-term behavioral changes and evolving public perceptions were not examined.
科研与实践启示
政策制定者应制定加强战略和沟通,以解决社会人口差异和健康风险态度,重点关注老年人、受教育程度较低的人和既往感染者等接受率较低的群体,以提高接种率并确保可持续的免疫覆盖率。

Sociodemographic and Health-Risk Determinants of COVID-19 Vaccine Booster Preferences and Willingness to Pay in Singapore: Discrete Choice Experiment.

Authors: Hooi Swang Cheng, Yi Wang, Tan SHX, Ian Yi Han Ang
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Waning immunity and recurring COVID-19 waves point to the importance of sustained booster vaccination strategies. With repeated boosters needed to sustain population-level immunity, evaluating public preferences for COVID-19 booster vaccination is important for informing future vaccination strategies, as changing perceptions of urgency, threat, and motivation may alter willingness to receive vaccine boosters compared with initial uptake. Objective This study aimed to explore the public’s stated preferences and implicit willingness to pay for COVID-19 vaccine boosters in Singapore and to identify the sociodemographic factors and health-risk acceptance profiles associated with these outcomes. Methods A discrete choice experiment survey was conducted among the general population in Singapore, recruited from an online panel in June 2022. Discrete choice experiment scenarios presented hypothetical scenarios varying by booster characteristics, including effectiveness, protection duration, and cost, and risks of COVID-19 infection, hospitalization, and death. A 2-stage discrete choice design was used: participants first selected between 2 hypothetical scenarios and then reported real-life booster acceptance of their chosen option. A mixed logit model was used for analyses. We also estimated implicit willingness to pay for vaccine boosters, as well as assessed the associations of sociodemographic factors and health-risk profiles with booster acceptance or hesitancy. Results A total of 1567 participants were included in the analysis. Overall, respondents preferred boosters that offered a longer duration of protection, particularly under higher risks of COVID-19 infection and death. Respondents were willing to pay S$1.43 for every percentage-point increase in booster effectiveness, S$4.95 (S$1=US $0.77 as of July 10, 2026) per additional month of protection, and an additional S$55.23 for a vaccine booster under the hypothetical worst-case scenario of COVID-19. Younger adults, women, individuals of non-Chinese ethnicity, those without children, people with higher socioeconomic status, individuals who had received 2 or fewer COVID-19 vaccine doses, and those with chronic conditions or on chronic disease medication were more likely to receive a booster. On the other hand, older adults, individuals with lower education or socioeconomic status, those with prior COVID-19 infection, and those exhibiting higher health-risk acceptance were less likely to accept booster vaccination. Conclusions Our findings demonstrate how vaccine attributes, sociodemographic factors, and health-risk attitudes influence booster acceptance. These insights can guide policymakers in tailoring strategies and communication efforts that address population needs and behavioral drivers to improve booster uptake, address hesitancy, and support sustainable long-term immunization coverage, contributing to adaptive vaccination planning in the ongoing management of COVID-19.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study focuses on COVID-19 vaccine booster preferences and willingness to pay in the general population of Singapore, aiming to identify sociodemographic and health-risk determinants influencing booster acceptance.
Evidence base and method
A discrete choice experiment survey was conducted among 1567 participants from an online panel in Singapore in June 2022, using a 2-stage design and mixed logit model to analyze preferences for booster characteristics and willingness to pay.
Consensus and conclusions
Respondents preferred boosters with longer protection duration, especially under higher COVID-19 risks, and were willing to pay S$1.43 per percentage-point increase in effectiveness and S$4.95 per additional month of protection. Sociodemographic factors like younger age, female gender, non-Chinese ethnicity, higher socioeconomic status, and chronic conditions were associated with higher booster acceptance.
Controversies and gaps
The study did not address potential regional variations within Singapore or explore the impact of prior COVID-19 infection on booster preferences beyond the identified negative association. Long-term behavioral changes and evolving public perceptions were not examined.
Research and practice implications
Policymakers should tailor booster strategies and communication to address sociodemographic disparities and health-risk attitudes, focusing on groups with lower acceptance rates like older adults, those with lower education, and individuals with prior infection to improve uptake and ensure sustainable immunization coverage.
