Post-COVID-19 rise in central precocious puberty: an integrative Review

Post-COVID-19 rise in central precocious puberty: an integrative Review
作者： Xiaoya Chen, Shuxia Ding
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review examines the association between the COVID-19 pandemic and an increase in central precocious puberty (CPP), particularly among girls, focusing on epidemiological findings, clinical features, and proposed mechanisms.
证据基础与方法
This is a narrative review that synthesizes epidemiological findings, clinical features, and proposed mechanisms related to the rise in CPP during the COVID-19 pandemic.
共识与结论
现有证据支持大流行期间 CPP 增加的可重复流行病学信号,特别是在女孩中,但没有建立单一的因果机制。拟议的机制包括久坐行为、体重增加、屏幕暴露增加、睡眠中断、心理压力、就医行为改变和环境内分泌干扰化学物质 (EDC)。
争议与缺口
虽然高通量毒理学数据表明某些环境化合物可以激活人类 KISS1R 或 GnRHR 相关信号传导,但儿童暴露水平、内部剂量、靶组织浓度和疾病水平因果关系仍未得到证实。目前的假设并不能完全解释大流行时期 CPP 中女性占主导地位和有限的平均 BMI 变化。
科研与实践启示
提出了一个谨慎的综合模型来协调观察到的模式,包括女性占主导地位以及与封锁相关暴露的时间关联。未来的研究应优先考虑 队列研究 研究、暴露评估和机制研究来检验这些假设。

Post-COVID-19 rise in central precocious puberty: an integrative Review

Authors: Xiaoya Chen, Shuxia Ding
Original abstract (English)
Abstract The COVID-19 pandemic was associated with a marked increase in central precocious puberty (CPP), particularly among girls, with pooled estimates showing higher odds during pandemic than pre-pandemic periods. The available evidence supports a reproducible epidemiological signal, but it does not establish a single causal mechanism. This narrative review synthesizes epidemiological findings, clinical features, and proposed mechanisms, including sedentary behavior, weight gain, increased screen exposure, sleep and melatonin rhythm disruption, psychological stress, altered healthcare-seeking behavior, and environmental endocrine-disrupting chemicals (EDCs). Recent high-throughput toxicology data indicate that selected environmental compounds, including musk ambrette and some quaternary ammonium compounds (QACs), can activate human KISS1R- or GnRHR-related signaling in experimental systems. However, pediatric exposure levels, internal dose, target-tissue concentrations, and disease-level causality remain unproven. We therefore frame these chemical findings as a hypothesis-generating exposomic perspective rather than as an established explanation for pandemic-era CPP. A cautious integrative model may help reconcile the strong female predominance, limited mean BMI shift, and temporal association with lockdown-related exposures, while defining testable priorities for future cohort, exposure, and mechanistic studies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines the association between the COVID-19 pandemic and an increase in central precocious puberty (CPP), particularly among girls, focusing on epidemiological findings, clinical features, and proposed mechanisms.
Evidence base and method
This is a narrative review that synthesizes epidemiological findings, clinical features, and proposed mechanisms related to the rise in CPP during the COVID-19 pandemic.
Consensus and conclusions
The available evidence supports a reproducible epidemiological signal of increased CPP during the pandemic, particularly in girls, but does not establish a single causal mechanism. Proposed mechanisms include sedentary behavior, weight gain, increased screen exposure, sleep disruption, psychological stress, altered healthcare-seeking behavior, and environmental endocrine-disrupting chemicals (EDCs).
Controversies and gaps
While high-throughput toxicology data suggest that some environmental compounds can activate human KISS1R- or GnRHR-related signaling, pediatric exposure levels, internal dose, target-tissue concentrations, and disease-level causality remain unproven. The female predominance and limited mean BMI shift in pandemic-era CPP are not fully explained by current hypotheses.
Research and practice implications
A cautious integrative model is proposed to reconcile observed patterns, including the strong female predominance and temporal association with lockdown-related exposures. Future research should prioritize cohort studies, exposure assessments, and mechanistic studies to test these hypotheses.

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.
作者： Carol M Kao, Hannah Bahakel, Taylor A Heald-Sargent, Timothy D Minniear, Victoria A Statler, Sanya J Thomas, Kathryn E Weakley, Qing Cao, Monica I Ardura, Lara Danziger-Isakov
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review focuses on immunocompromised pediatric patients, including hematopoietic cell and solid organ transplant recipients, those undergoing chemotherapy for malignancy, and those receiving biologic response modifiers for autoimmune or inflammatory conditions, who are at risk for severe disease from vaccine-preventable respiratory viral infections such as influenza, SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). The central question is to review available data supporting current guidelines regarding vaccine dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent these infections in immunocompromised children.
证据基础与方法
该综述是对现有数据的叙述性综合,因为没有报告具体的数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究计数。作者的目的是回顾现有指南并强调知识差距,但没有描述系统回顾或荟萃分析方法。
共识与结论
由于严重呼吸道病毒感染,免疫功能低下的儿童面临更高的住院率、重症监护室入院率和 死亡率 率,这反映出免疫系统不成熟、免疫记忆缺失和/或环境暴露增加的影响。针对这些儿童的免疫建议很大程度上是根据在健康儿童和/或免疫功能低下的成人中进行的研究推断的,因为缺乏有关该人群免疫反应和 疫苗 功效的主要数据。
争议与缺口
缺乏关于免疫反应和 疫苗 疗效的主要数据,特别是在免疫功能低下的儿童中,导致建议很大程度上是从其他人群推断出来的。此外,疫苗 犹豫的趋势、社区中持续存在的呼吸道病毒感染 传播 以及免疫功能低下儿童及其家庭接触者中 疫苗接种 率不理想的情况进一步加剧了这一弱势群体的风险。
科研与实践启示
随着患有免疫功能低下的儿童数量的增加,护理免疫功能低下儿童的初级保健从业者和专科医生必须确保为这些患者及其家庭和社区接触者提供适当的免疫接种。该综述还强调了关键的知识差距和当前旨在提高呼吸道病毒 疫苗 免疫原性和优化保护的研究领域。

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.

Authors: Carol M Kao, Hannah Bahakel, Taylor A Heald-Sargent, Timothy D Minniear, Victoria A Statler, Sanya J Thomas, Kathryn E Weakley, Qing Cao, Monica I Ardura, Lara Danziger-Isakov
Original abstract (English)
Immunocompromised pediatric patients, including hematopoietic cell and solid organ transplant recipients, those undergoing chemotherapy for malignancy, and those receiving biologic response modifiers for autoimmune or inflammatory conditions, are at risk for severe disease from vaccine-preventable respiratory viral infections such as influenza, SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). These children face higher rates of hospitalization, intensive care admissions, and mortality, reflecting contributions from an immature immune system, absence of immunologic memory, and/or increased environmental exposures. Immunization recommendations for immunocompromised children are largely extrapolated from studies conducted among healthy children and/or immunocompromised adults due to a paucity of primary data on immunological responses and vaccine efficacy in immunocompromised children. Trends in vaccine hesitancy in the general population, ongoing transmission of respiratory viral infections in the community, and suboptimal vaccination rates among immunocompromised children and their household contacts further compound the risk for this vulnerable population. As the number of children with immunocompromising conditions expands, it is imperative that primary care practitioners and subspecialists who care for immunocompromised children ensure appropriate immunizations are provided to these patients and their household and community contacts. We will review available data supporting the current guidelines regarding vaccine dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent influenza, COVID-19, and RSV infections in immunocompromised children. Furthermore, we highlight key knowledge gaps and areas of current investigation aimed at improving respiratory viral vaccine immunogenicity and optimize protection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on immunocompromised pediatric patients, including hematopoietic cell and solid organ transplant recipients, those undergoing chemotherapy for malignancy, and those receiving biologic response modifiers for autoimmune or inflammatory conditions, who are at risk for severe disease from vaccine-preventable respiratory viral infections such as influenza, SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). The central question is to review available data supporting current guidelines regarding vaccine dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent these infections in immunocompromised children.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of available data, as no specific databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study counts are reported. The authors aim to review existing guidelines and highlight knowledge gaps, but no systematic review or meta-analysis methodology is described.
Consensus and conclusions
Immunocompromised children face higher rates of hospitalization, intensive care admissions, and mortality due to severe respiratory viral infections, reflecting contributions from an immature immune system, absence of immunologic memory, and/or increased environmental exposures. Immunization recommendations for these children are largely extrapolated from studies conducted among healthy children and/or immunocompromised adults due to a paucity of primary data on immunological responses and vaccine efficacy in this population.
Controversies and gaps
There is a lack of primary data on immunological responses and vaccine efficacy specifically in immunocompromised children, leading to recommendations that are largely extrapolated from other populations. Additionally, trends in vaccine hesitancy, ongoing transmission of respiratory viral infections in the community, and suboptimal vaccination rates among immunocompromised children and their household contacts further compound the risk for this vulnerable population.
Research and practice implications
As the number of children with immunocompromising conditions expands, it is imperative that primary care practitioners and subspecialists who care for immunocompromised children ensure appropriate immunizations are provided to these patients and their household and community contacts. The review also highlights key knowledge gaps and areas of current investigation aimed at improving respiratory viral vaccine immunogenicity and optimizing protection.

Economic Preferences Predict COVID-19 Vaccination Intentions and Behavior.

Economic Preferences Predict COVID-19 Vaccination Intentions and Behavior.
作者： S. von Angerer, Helena Antonie Baier, Daniela Glätzle-Rützler, Philipp Lergetporer, Thomas Rittmannsberger, Daniela Glätzle‐Rützler

范围与问题
This study investigates the relationship between economic preferences and COVID-19 vaccination readiness using two representative samples of the German population (N > 5000).
证据基础与方法
我们使用经过验证的调查问题来激发利他主义、耐心、冒险精神和信任。

我们发现 疫苗接种 准备度与耐心和信任之间存在牢固、积极的关系。

Economic Preferences Predict COVID-19 Vaccination Intentions and Behavior.

Authors: S. von Angerer, Helena Antonie Baier, Daniela Glätzle-Rützler, Philipp Lergetporer, Thomas Rittmannsberger, Daniela Glätzle‐Rützler
Original abstract (English)
This study investigates the relationship between economic preferences and COVID-19 vaccination readiness using two representative samples of the German population (N > 5000). We elicited altruism, patience, risk-taking and trust using validated survey questions. We find robust, positive relationships between vaccination readiness and both patience and trust. The positive association between altruism and vaccination readiness vanishes when controlling for the other preference dimensions. No consistent effect emerges for risk-taking. Our results underscore the importance of accounting for the interrelated nature of economic preferences when analyzing their connection with field behavior.
View review five-element analysis

Evidence base and method

Consensus and conclusions
We find robust, positive relationships between vaccination readiness and both patience and trust.
Controversies and gaps
The positive association between altruism and vaccination readiness vanishes when controlling for the other preference dimensions. No consistent effect emerges for risk-taking.
Research and practice implications
Our results underscore the importance of accounting for the interrelated nature of economic preferences when analyzing their connection with field behavior.

Perceptions of Spanish-language COVID-19 video messaging among the Hispanic community: A qualitative study in the United States of America.

Perceptions of Spanish-language COVID-19 video messaging among the Hispanic community: A qualitative study in the United States of America.
作者： Brannon GE, Karishma Chatterjee, Chyng-Yang Jang, Charla Markham Shaw, Vinicio Sinta, Julian Rodriguez
摘要中文翻译有证据表明可信和优质的 新型冠状病毒感染 疫苗 信息与积极的 新型冠状病毒感染 态度和意图相关,因此了解面临健康差异的社区如何获取有关 新型冠状病毒感染 的健康信息至关重要。这项对南部郊区一个大县的 49 名西班牙裔人士进行的访谈研究是一项为期三年的大型多方法研究的一部分。通过自决理论的视角,该项目调查了对 CseOVID-19 疫苗接种 上电视播放的四个西班牙语视频信息的看法,这些信息是根据先前当地焦点小组和社区合作伙伴支持的小组调查的结果得出的。本研究的结果表明,以人为本的增益框架信息得到了积极的接受,并强调需要在西班牙新闻媒体中提供有针对性的西班牙语公共卫生信息,以提高有关 新型冠状病毒感染 的信息质量。对健康保护行为的温和建议可能会增强公共卫生信息的感知共鸣、可信度和信息价值,并对心理反应和随后的反应产生潜在影响。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This study explores perceptions of Spanish-language COVID-19 video messaging among the Hispanic community in the United States, focusing on how communities facing health disparities access health information about COVID-19. The central question is about the effectiveness of targeted public health messaging in improving vaccination attitudes and intentions.
证据基础与方法
The study is an interview-based qualitative analysis of 49 Hispanic individuals in a large southern suburban county. It is part of a larger three-year multi-method study and uses self-determination theory to investigate perceptions of four television-aired Spanish-language video messages on COVID-19 vaccination.
共识与结论
The findings indicate that human-centered, gain-frame messaging was positively received by the Hispanic community. This suggests that targeted Spanish-language public health messaging in Spanish news media could improve the quality of information about COVID-19.
争议与缺口
The study highlights the need for more research on how to foster greater perceived resonance,credibility,and informational value of public health messages.它还表明对心理抗拒和随后对健康信息的反应的潜在影响。
科研与实践启示
The study recommends the development of more gentle suggestions for health protective behaviors in Spanish-language public health messaging. It also emphasizes the importance of understanding how communities facing health disparities access health information about COVID-19.

Perceptions of Spanish-language COVID-19 video messaging among the Hispanic community: A qualitative study in the United States of America.

Authors: Brannon GE, Karishma Chatterjee, Chyng-Yang Jang, Charla Markham Shaw, Vinicio Sinta, Julian Rodriguez
Original abstract (English)
With evidence linking credible and quality COVID-19 vaccine information to positive vaccination attitudes and intentions, it's vital to understand how communities facing health disparities access health information about COVID-19. This interview study of 49 Hispanic individuals in a large southern suburban county is a part of a larger three-year multi-method study. Through the lens of self-determination theory, this project investigates perceptions of four television-aired Spanish-language video messages on CseOVID-19 vaccination developed from findings of prior local focus groups and panel surveys supported by community partners. The present study's findings show human-centered, gain-frame messaging was positively received and highlight the need for targeted Spanish-language public health messaging in Spanish news media to improve quality information about COVID-19. Gentler suggestions for health protective behaviors may foster greater perceived resonance, credibility, and informational value of public health messages, with potential implications for psychological reactance and subsequent responses.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study explores perceptions of Spanish-language COVID-19 video messaging among the Hispanic community in the United States, focusing on how communities facing health disparities access health information about COVID-19. The central question is about the effectiveness of targeted public health messaging in improving vaccination attitudes and intentions.
Evidence base and method
The study is an interview-based qualitative analysis of 49 Hispanic individuals in a large southern suburban county. It is part of a larger three-year multi-method study and uses self-determination theory to investigate perceptions of four television-aired Spanish-language video messages on COVID-19 vaccination.
Consensus and conclusions
The findings indicate that human-centered, gain-frame messaging was positively received by the Hispanic community. This suggests that targeted Spanish-language public health messaging in Spanish news media could improve the quality of information about COVID-19.
Controversies and gaps
The study highlights the need for more research on how to foster greater perceived resonance, credibility, and informational value of public health messages. It also suggests potential implications for psychological reactance and subsequent responses to health messaging.
Research and practice implications
The study recommends the development of more gentle suggestions for health protective behaviors in Spanish-language public health messaging. It also emphasizes the importance of understanding how communities facing health disparities access health information about COVID-19.

Persistent physical and mental health effects of COVID-19 hospitalization

Persistent physical and mental health effects of COVID-19 hospitalization
作者： David L. Goldman, Valerie Fong-Silva, Salih Demirhan, Jassour Alrikaby, Kiriam Escobar Lee, Catherine Sancimino, Sophie Molholm, Michael D. Cabana, Betsy C. Herold
摘要中文翻译简介 新冠肺炎大流行对儿童健康的长期影响仍然是一个正在积极研究的领域。病毒感染、免疫应答 和社会混乱被认为是促成因素。方法 我们对原始/阿尔法变异波(2020年3月1日至2021年3月1日)或Omicron波(2021年12月2日至2022年12月21日)期间入住美国纽约州布朗克斯市一家城市儿童医院的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染儿童和未感染对照儿童进行了一项回顾性调查研究。评估了持续症状、恢复时间、家庭压力和精神结果。使用患者健康问卷 (PHQ-9) 和广泛性焦虑症 (GAD-7) (n = 48) 以及儿科症状检查表 (PSC-17) (n = 64) 完成心理测量评估。结果 共有 176 名儿童和家庭完成了调查。大约 31.9% 在 Original/Alpha 波期间入院的儿童和 8.8% 在 Omicron 波期间入院的儿童报告说,他们的疾病需要几个月或更长时间才能痊愈。住院后最常见的症状是疲劳、呼吸急促和睡眠困难,这些症状与未感染对照组相似。PHQ-9 和 GAD-7 评分没有因感染状态而异。然而,原始/Alpha 波期间的 PHQ-9 分数显着高于 Omicron 波期间(中位数 5 比 1;p = 0.04;r = 0.62),GAD-7 的趋势类似(p = 0.05)。总体而言,43.8% 的儿童在 PHQ-9 中筛查出抑郁症状呈阳性,其中 20.9% 的儿童处于中度至重度范围内。PSC-17 评分因波次或感染状态的不同而没有显着差异。据报道,新冠病毒阳性家庭和未感染家庭都面临着与大流行相关的高水平压力,包括失业、儿童保育中断和粮食不安全。结论 原始/阿尔法波期间住院的儿童比 Omicron 波期间住院的儿童经历了明显更大的长期康复负担,这表明病毒学和免疫学因素与急性后身体结果有关。值得注意的是,新冠病毒阳性儿童和未感染对照组的住院后症状相似,这凸显了同期对照组在急性后遗症研究中的重要性。心理健康结果与大流行阶段的相关性比感染状态的相关性更强,这表明社会破坏而不是直接的病毒影响是关键因素。这些发现强调了在突发公共卫生事件期间对住院儿童进行常规心理健康筛查的必要性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The study investigates persistent physical and mental health effects of COVID-19 hospitalization in children, comparing outcomes during the Original/Alpha and Omicron waves, with a focus on prolonged recovery, symptoms, and psychiatric outcomes.
证据基础与方法
对美国纽约州布朗克斯市一家城市儿童医院收治的 176 名儿童和家庭进行了一项回顾性调查研究,这些儿童和家庭在 Original/Alpha 浪潮(2020 年 3 月至 2021 年 3 月)或 Omicron 浪潮(2021 年 12 月至 2022 年 12 月)期间被收治。心理测量评估包括 PHQ-9、GAD-7 和 PSC-17。
共识与结论
在原始/阿尔法波期间住院的儿童比在 Omicron 波期间经历了明显更大的长期康复负担。新冠病毒阳性儿童和未感染对照组的出院后症状相似,这强调了建立同期对照组的必要性。心理健康结果与大流行阶段的相关性比感染状态的相关性更强。
争议与缺口
该研究强调,与直接的病毒影响相比,心理健康结果与社会破坏的关系更大,但推动原始/阿尔法波长期恢复的具体机制仍不清楚。病毒学和免疫学因素在急性后身体结果中的作用需要进一步研究。
科研与实践启示
Routine mental health screening in hospitalized children during public health emergencies is recommended. Further research is needed to understand the virologic and immunologic factors contributing to prolonged recovery in different COVID-19 waves.

Persistent physical and mental health effects of COVID-19 hospitalization

Authors: David L. Goldman, Valerie Fong-Silva, Salih Demirhan, Jassour Alrikaby, Kiriam Escobar Lee, Catherine Sancimino, Sophie Molholm, Michael D. Cabana, Betsy C. Herold
Original abstract (English)
Introduction The long-term health effects of the COVID pandemic on children remain an area of active investigation. Viral infection, immune responses, and societal disruption have been implicated as contributing factors. Methods We conducted a retrospective survey-based study of children infected with SARS-CoV-2 and uninfected controls admitted to a single urban children's hospital in the Bronx, NY, USA, during the Original/Alpha variant wave (1 March 2020 to 1 March 2021) or Omicron wave (2 December 2021 to 21 December 2022). Persistent symptoms, time to recovery, family stress, and psychiatric outcomes were assessed. A psychometric assessment using Patient Health Questionnaire (PHQ-9) and Generalized Anxiety Disorder (GAD-7) ( n = 48) and Pediatric Symptom Checklist (PSC-17) ( n = 64) was completed. Results A total of 176 children and families completed surveys. Approximately 31.9% of children admitted during the Original/Alpha wave and 8.8% during the Omicron wave reported that their illness took several months or more to resolve. The most common symptoms after hospitalization were fatigue, shortness of breath, and difficulty sleeping, and these were similar to those experienced by uninfected controls. PHQ-9 and GAD-7 scores did not differ by infection status. However, PHQ-9 scores were significantly higher during the Original/Alpha wave than during the Omicron wave (median 5 vs. 1; p = 0.04; r = 0.62), with GAD-7 trending similarly ( p = 0.05). Overall, 43.8% of children screened positive for depressive symptoms on PHQ-9, including 20.9% in the moderate-to-severe range. PSC-17 scores did not differ significantly by wave or infection status. High levels of pandemic-related stress, including job loss, childcare disruption, and food insecurity, were reported across both COVID-positive and uninfected families. Conclusion Children hospitalized during the Original/Alpha wave experienced a significantly greater burden of prolonged recovery than those during the Omicron wave, implicating virologic and immunologic factors in postacute physical outcomes. Notably, posthospitalization symptoms were similar between COVID-positive children and uninfected controls, underscoring the importance of contemporaneous control groups in studies of postacute sequelae. Mental health outcomes were more strongly associated with the pandemic phase than infection status, suggesting societal disruption rather than direct viral effects as a key contributor. These findings highlight the need for routine mental health screening in hospitalized children during public health emergencies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study investigates persistent physical and mental health effects of COVID-19 hospitalization in children, comparing outcomes during the Original/Alpha and Omicron waves, with a focus on prolonged recovery, symptoms, and psychiatric outcomes.
Evidence base and method
A retrospective survey-based study of 176 children and families admitted to a single urban children's hospital in the Bronx, NY, USA, during the Original/Alpha wave (March 2020–March 2021) or Omicron wave (December 2021–December 2022). Psychometric assessments included PHQ-9, GAD-7, and PSC-17.
Consensus and conclusions
Children hospitalized during the Original/Alpha wave experienced a significantly greater burden of prolonged recovery than those during the Omicron wave. Posthospitalization symptoms were similar between COVID-positive children and uninfected controls, emphasizing the need for contemporaneous control groups. Mental health outcomes were more strongly associated with pandemic phase than infection status.
Controversies and gaps
The study highlights that mental health outcomes were more linked to societal disruption than direct viral effects, but the specific mechanisms driving prolonged recovery in the Original/Alpha wave remain unclear. The role of virologic and immunologic factors in postacute physical outcomes requires further investigation.
Research and practice implications
Routine mental health screening in hospitalized children during public health emergencies is recommended. Further research is needed to understand the virologic and immunologic factors contributing to prolonged recovery in different COVID-19 waves.

审稿人 #2(公开审稿):严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5′-UTR 中单个位点的 N6-腺苷甲基化促进翻译

Reviewer #2 (Public review): N6-Adenosine Methylation of a Single Site in SARS-CoV-2 5′-UTR Promotes Translation
作者： Ammar Aly, Gary Scott, Mario Calderon, A. Pejmun Haghighi
摘要中文翻译2019冠状病毒病(COVID19)导致了毁灭性的健康结果,尽管全球疫苗接种努力1,它仍在继续传播。这加上疫苗耐药变异株的快速出现,产生了针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)2-7更保守方面的正交治疗策略的需求。病毒的基因组是一条单链正RNA,分为基因组亚基因组片段。所有16个非结构病毒蛋白均从基因组多顺反子ORF 1a和1b翻译而来,使用单一的5′-UTR前导序列8,9。令我们惊讶的是,全长5′-UTR尽管其预测的结构复杂,却能有效地启动蛋白质翻译。通过结合生化测定和生物信息学分析,我们证明严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5′-UTR中的单个METTL3依赖的m6A甲基化事件调节翻译起始速率。我们证明m6A可能通过 destabilizing 第三茎环(SL3)并增加蛋白质复合物的整体可及性来发挥这种作用。我们的发现通过将m6A修饰与严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的翻译调控联系起来,为严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的生物学提供了基础见解,从而为潜在的新型治疗策略开辟了新途径。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究调查了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5'-UTR 中的 N6-腺苷甲基化 (m6A) 在促进翻译起始中的作用,重点是了解这种修饰如何影响病毒蛋白合成及其作为治疗靶点的潜力。
证据基础与方法
证据来自于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 上进行的生化测定和生物信息学分析。该研究没有报告具体的数据库、检索日期或资格标准,但描述了使用单个 5'-UTR 前导序列翻译基因组多顺反子 ORF 1a 和 1b 中的所有 16 种非结构病毒蛋白。
共识与结论
该研究得出结论,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5'-UTR 中的单个 METTL3 依赖性 m6A 甲基化事件可调节翻译起始速率。尽管结构复杂,但全长 5'-UTR 仍能有效启动蛋白质翻译,这一观察结果支持了这一发现。
争议与缺口
该研究不直接报告争议或证据差距。然而,它指出 5'-UTR 的结构复杂性及其在翻译起始中的作用仍未得到充分研究,这表明需要进一步研究病毒 RNA 中 m6A 修饰的机制。
科研与实践启示
该研究强调了靶向 m6A 修饰作为 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 新型治疗策略的潜力。这表明了解 m6A 在病毒翻译中的作用可能有助于开发新的 治疗 和对抗病毒的干预措施。

Reviewer #2 (Public review): N6-Adenosine Methylation of a Single Site in SARS-CoV-2 5′-UTR Promotes Translation

Authors: Ammar Aly, Gary Scott, Mario Calderon, A. Pejmun Haghighi
Original abstract (English)
The coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID19) led to devastating health outcomes and has continued to spread despite global vaccination efforts1. This, alongside the rapid emergence of vaccine resistant variants, creates a need for orthogonal therapeutic strategies targeting more conserved facets of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus (SARS-CoV-2)2–7. The viral genome is a single positive RNA strand divided into a genomic and a subgenomic segment. All 16 non-structural viral proteins are translated from the genomic polycistronic ORFs 1a, and 1b using a single 5′-UTR leader8,9. To our surprise, the full length 5′-UTR efficiently initiates protein translation despite its predicted structural complexity. Through a combination of biochemical assays and bioinformatic analyses, we demonstrate that a single METTL3-dependent m6A methylation event in SARS-CoV-2 5′-UTR regulates the rate of translation initiation. We demonstrate that m6A likely exerts this effect by destabilizing the third stem loop (SL3) and increasing overall accessibility to protein complexes. Our discovery provides a foundational insight into the biology of SARS-CoV-2 by linking m6A modification to its translational regulation and, thus, opens a new avenue for potential novel therapeutic strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study investigates the role of N6-adenosine methylation (m6A) in the 5′-UTR of SARS-CoV-2 in promoting translation initiation, with a focus on understanding how this modification affects viral protein synthesis and its potential as a therapeutic target.
Evidence base and method
The evidence is derived from biochemical assays and bioinformatic analyses conducted on SARS-CoV-2. The study does not report specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria, but it describes the use of a single 5′-UTR leader for translating all 16 non-structural viral proteins from the genomic polycistronic ORFs 1a and 1b.
Consensus and conclusions
The study concludes that a single METTL3-dependent m6A methylation event in the SARS-CoV-2 5′-UTR regulates the rate of translation initiation. This finding is supported by the observation that the full-length 5′-UTR efficiently initiates protein translation despite its structural complexity.
Controversies and gaps
The study does not report controversies or evidence gaps directly. However, it notes that the 5′-UTR's structural complexity and its role in translation initiation remain understudied, suggesting a need for further investigation into the mechanisms of m6A modification in viral RNA.
Research and practice implications
The study highlights the potential of targeting m6A modification as a novel therapeutic strategy for SARS-CoV-2. It suggests that understanding the role of m6A in viral translation could lead to the development of new treatments and interventions to combat the virus.

Country-level immunization recovery and DTP1 no-dose proxy burden after COVID-19: a descriptive ecological analysis of WUENIC, 2010-2024.

Country-level immunization recovery and DTP1 no-dose proxy burden after COVID-19: a descriptive ecological analysis of WUENIC, 2010-2024.
作者： Jinze Li, Ziqi Wang, Yuning Gong
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review examines country-level immunization recovery and DTP1 no-dose proxy burden after COVID-19, focusing on access, series completion, measles coverage, and recovery from 2019 across 194 countries/reporting entities during 2010-2024.
证据基础与方法
使用精确的 2024 年修订版 WHO/UNICEF WUENIC 记录进行描述性生态分析,包括恢复估计、DTP1 无剂量负担、DTP3-MCV1 重叠和表型分配。重现了基准,敏感性分析包括来源状态、目标人群加权、阈值和 2025 年精确修订方法。
共识与结论
Aggregate immunization coverage in 2024 was near 2019 levels (DTP1: 88.94%, DTP3: 84.56%, MCV1: 84.03%), but country-level fixed-effects estimates showed declines (-1.80 to -2.09 percentage points). The DTP1 no-dose proxy burden was 14.26 million, with the top 20 entities accounting for 74.6%, and 45 entities with low DTP3/Measles coverage representing 72.8% of the burden.
争议与缺口
阈值变化最多重新分配了 13.4% 的实体,2025 年数据修订改变了 194 个常见实体中的 20 个恢复类别和 19 个表型分配。作业是描述性的和特定于修订的,未经验证的风险评分,并且可能无法完全捕捉细微差别的恢复模式。
科研与实践启示
项目经理应联合审查实体层面的访问、完成、麻疹覆盖率、恢复和数据质量信号,而不是依赖单一排名。描述性表型需要上下文解释,不应用作独立的风险指标。

Country-level immunization recovery and DTP1 no-dose proxy burden after COVID-19: a descriptive ecological analysis of WUENIC, 2010-2024.

Authors: Jinze Li, Ziqi Wang, Yuning Gong
Original abstract (English)
Background Global immunization averages can obscure entity-level shortfalls in access, series completion, measles coverage, and recovery from 2019. We assessed these signals. Methods The primary analysis used exact-2024-revision WHO/UNICEF WUENIC records for 194 countries/reporting entities during 2010-2024. We estimated recovery from 2019 and DTP1 no-dose proxy burden, quantified DTP3-MCV1 overlap, and assigned ordered phenotypes. We reproduced benchmarks and conducted source-status, target-population-weighted, threshold, and exact-2025-revision sensitivity analyses. Results Reproduced weighted 2024 estimates were 88.94% for DTP1, 84.56% for DTP3, and 84.03% for MCV1. Unweighted country fixed-effects estimates for 2024 versus 2019 were - 1.80 percentage points (95% CI -2.75 to -0.84) for DTP1, -2.09 (-3.25 to -0.93) for DTP3, and - 1.65 (-2.72 to -0.58) for MCV1; target-population-weighted estimates were closer to zero and imprecise. The DTP1 no-dose proxy count was 14.26 million; the top 20 entities accounted for 74.6%. Forty-five entities with DTP3 below 80% and MCV1 below 90% accounted for 72.8% of the proxy count. Threshold changes reassigned at most 13.4% of entities. With exact-2025-revision records and the endpoint fixed at 2024, aggregate metrics were similar, but 20 recovery-class and 19 phenotype assignments changed among 194 common entities. Conclusion Aggregate coverage near 2019 levels coexisted with entity-level access, completion, measles, recovery, and data-review signals. Program managers should review them jointly, not as one ranking; assignments are descriptive and revision-specific, not validated risk scores.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines country-level immunization recovery and DTP1 no-dose proxy burden after COVID-19, focusing on access, series completion, measles coverage, and recovery from 2019 across 194 countries/reporting entities during 2010-2024.
Evidence base and method
A descriptive ecological analysis using exact-2024-revision WHO/UNICEF WUENIC records, with estimation of recovery, DTP1 no-dose burden, DTP3-MCV1 overlap, and phenotype assignments. Benchmarks were reproduced, and sensitivity analyses included source-status, target-population-weighted, threshold, and exact-2025-revision approaches.
Consensus and conclusions
Aggregate immunization coverage in 2024 was near 2019 levels (DTP1: 88.94%, DTP3: 84.56%, MCV1: 84.03%), but country-level fixed-effects estimates showed declines (-1.80 to -2.09 percentage points). The DTP1 no-dose proxy burden was 14.26 million, with the top 20 entities accounting for 74.6%, and 45 entities with low DTP3/Measles coverage representing 72.8% of the burden.
Controversies and gaps
Threshold changes reassigned at most 13.4% of entities, and 2025 data revisions altered 20 recovery-class and 19 phenotype assignments among 194 common entities. Assignments are descriptive and revision-specific, not validated risk scores, and may not fully capture nuanced recovery patterns.
Research and practice implications
Program managers should jointly review entity-level signals for access, completion, measles coverage, recovery, and data quality rather than relying on single rankings. Descriptive phenotypes require contextual interpretation and should not be used as standalone risk metrics.

eLife评估:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5′-UTR中单个位点的N6-腺苷甲基化促进翻译

eLife Assessment: N6-Adenosine Methylation of a Single Site in SARS-CoV-2 5′-UTR Promotes Translation
作者： Alan G. Hinnebusch
摘要中文翻译尽管全球 疫苗接种 做出了努力,2019 年冠状病毒病 (COVID19) 仍导致了毁灭性的健康后果,并且仍在继续传播。这与 疫苗 耐药变体的迅速出现一起,产生了对针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)更保守方面的正交治疗策略的需求。病毒 基因组 是一条正向 RNA 链,分为基因组片段和亚基因组片段。所有 16 种非结构病毒蛋白均使用单个 5'-UTR 前导序列从基因组多顺反子 ORF 1a 和 1b 翻译而来。令我们惊讶的是,尽管预测的结构复杂,但全长 5'-UTR 仍能有效启动蛋白质翻译。通过生化检测和生物信息学分析的结合,我们证明了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5'-UTR 中单个 METTL3 依赖性 m6A 甲基化事件调节翻译起始速率。我们证明 m6A 可能通过破坏第三茎环 (SL3) 的稳定性并增加蛋白质复合物的整体可及性来发挥这种作用。我们的发现通过将 m6A 修饰与其翻译调控联系起来,为 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的生物学提供了基础性的见解,从而为潜在的新型治疗策略开辟了一条新途径。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究探讨了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5′-UTR 中的 N6-腺苷甲基化 (m6A) 在促进翻译起始中的作用。核心问题是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5'-UTR 中的单个 m6A 甲基化事件是否会影响翻译起始的效率,这对于病毒复制和 发病机制 至关重要。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于生化测定和生物信息学分析的结合。证据来源于对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的研究,重点关注其5'-UTR和m6A甲基化的影响。这些方法包括甲基化事件的实验验证和 5'-UTR 的结构分析,以了解其在翻译起始中的作用。
共识与结论
该研究得出结论,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 5'-UTR 中的单个 METTL3 依赖性 m6A 甲基化事件调节翻译起始速率。这一发现表明 m6A 修饰在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的翻译调控中发挥着重要作用,这可能对理解病毒生物学和制定治疗策略具有重要意义。
争议与缺口
研究指出,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的全长 5'-UTR 有效启动蛋白质翻译,尽管其预测的结构复杂。然而,m6A 甲基化影响翻译起始的确切机制仍不清楚,需要进一步研究在不同背景和人群中验证这些发现。
科研与实践启示
m6A 甲基化在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 翻译起始中的作用的发现为针对这一保守机制的治疗策略开辟了新途径。这可能会导致新型 治疗 的开发,该药物会破坏病毒蛋白的合成,从而有可能为当前的 疫苗 和 抗病毒药物 疗法提供替代方案。

eLife Assessment: N6-Adenosine Methylation of a Single Site in SARS-CoV-2 5′-UTR Promotes Translation

Authors: Alan G. Hinnebusch
Original abstract (English)
The coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID19) led to devastating health outcomes and has continued to spread despite global vaccination efforts. This, alongside the rapid emergence of vaccine resistant variants, creates a need for orthogonal therapeutic strategies targeting more conserved facets of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus (SARS-CoV-2)–. All 16 non-structural viral proteins are translated from the genomic polycistronic ORFs 1a, and 1b using a single 5′-UTR leader,. View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study investigates the role of N6-adenosine methylation (m6A) in the 5′-UTR of SARS-CoV-2 in promoting translation initiation. The central question is whether a single m6A methylation event in the 5′-UTR of SARS-CoV-2 influences the efficiency of translation initiation, which is critical for viral replication and pathogenesis.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a combination of biochemical assays and bioinformatic analyses. The evidence is derived from studies on SARS-CoV-2, focusing on its 5′-UTR and the impact of m6A methylation. The methods include experimental validation of the methylation event and structural analysis of the 5′-UTR to understand its role in translation initiation.

The study concludes that a single METTL3-dependent m6A methylation event in the 5′-UTR of SARS-CoV-2 regulates the rate of translation initiation. However, the exact mechanism by which m6A methylation affects translation initiation remains unclear, and further research is needed to validate these findings in different contexts and populations.
Research and practice implications
The discovery of m6A methylation's role in SARS-CoV-2 translation initiation opens new avenues for therapeutic strategies targeting this conserved mechanism. This could lead to the development of novel treatments that disrupt viral protein synthesis, potentially offering an alternative to current vaccines and antiviral therapies.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 后的血栓和脑血管事件:系统评价和荟萃分析的总体综述

Thrombotic and cerebrovascular events following SARS-CoV-2 vaccination: an umbrella review of systematic reviews and meta-analyses
作者： Nicholas Aderinto, Ahmedyar Hasan, Aderinola Halimat Abioye, Ebube Christopher Mbah, Morounfoluwa Patience Olalusi, Enjema Eposi Motimbe, Oluwadamilola Esther Akinbo
摘要中文翻译严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 后有罕见的血栓和脑血管事件报告,引发了安全性担忧。这篇总体综述综合了 19 项系统综述和荟萃分析的证据,这些综述和荟萃分析检查了不同 疫苗 平台上的血栓形成结果,包括急性缺血性中风和脑静脉窦血栓形成。使用 AMSTAR-2 评估方法学质量,并通过结果和平台综合结果。证据一致表明,疫苗接种 后发生血栓和脑血管事件的情况很少见。mRNA 疫苗(BNT162b2、mRNA-1273)与超出背景人群比率的风险增加无关。腺病毒 媒介 疫苗(ChAdOx1 nCoV-19、Ad26.COV2.S)与 疫苗 诱导的免疫性血栓性血小板减少症的罕见综合征有关,最常见的是年轻人中的脑静脉窦血栓形成。全病毒 疫苗 的证据有限,但并未表明一致的安全问题。在所有平台上,血栓形成风险均远低于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的风险。总体而言,疫苗接种 的好处大于风险,凸显了持续的 监测 和透明沟通的重要性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
这篇总括性综述检查了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 后的血栓和脑血管事件,重点关注不同 疫苗 平台上的急性缺血性中风和脑静脉窦血栓形成。
证据基础与方法
该综述综合了 19 项系统综述和荟萃分析的证据,并使用 AMSTAR-2 评估了方法学质量。调查结果按结果和 疫苗 平台进行分类。
共识与结论
证据一致表明 疫苗接种 后血栓和脑血管事件很少见。mRNA 疫苗(BNT162b2、mRNA-1273)与超出背景率的风险增加无关。腺病毒 媒介 疫苗(ChAdOx1 nCoV-19、Ad26.COV2.S)与罕见的 疫苗 诱导的免疫性血栓性血小板减少症有关,主要表现为年轻人的脑静脉窦血栓形成。全病毒 疫苗 显示的证据有限,没有一致的安全问题。
争议与缺口
该综述确定了全病毒 疫苗 的有限证据,并强调需要持续进行 疫苗 来监测罕见的血栓事件,特别是年轻人群中的腺病毒 媒介 疫苗。
科研与实践启示
疫苗接种 的好处大于风险,强调了持续 监测 和关于罕见不良事件的透明沟通的重要性。

Thrombotic and cerebrovascular events following SARS-CoV-2 vaccination: an umbrella review of systematic reviews and meta-analyses

Authors: Nicholas Aderinto, Ahmedyar Hasan, Aderinola Halimat Abioye, Ebube Christopher Mbah, Morounfoluwa Patience Olalusi, Enjema Eposi Motimbe, Oluwadamilola Esther Akinbo
Original abstract (English)
Rare thrombotic and cerebrovascular events have been reported after SARS-CoV-2 vaccination, raising safety concerns. This umbrella review synthesizes evidence from 19 systematic reviews and meta-analyses examining thrombotic outcomes, including acute ischemic stroke and cerebral venous sinus thrombosis, across different vaccine platforms. Methodological quality was assessed using AMSTAR-2, and findings were synthesized by outcome and platform. Evidence consistently shows that thrombotic and cerebrovascular events following vaccination are rare. mRNA vaccines (BNT162b2, mRNA-1273) were not associated with increased risk beyond background population rates. Adenoviral vector vaccines (ChAdOx1 nCoV-19, Ad26.COV2.S) were linked to a rare syndrome of vaccine-induced immune thrombotic thrombocytopenia, most commonly presenting as cerebral venous sinus thrombosis in younger adults. Evidence for whole-virus vaccines was limited but did not indicate consistent safety concerns. Across all platforms, thrombotic risk was substantially lower than that from SARS-CoV-2 infection. Overall, vaccination benefits outweigh risks, highlighting the importance of ongoing surveillance and transparent communication.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This umbrella review examines thrombotic and cerebrovascular events following SARS-CoV-2 vaccination, focusing on acute ischemic stroke and cerebral venous sinus thrombosis across different vaccine platforms.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes evidence from 19 systematic reviews and meta-analyses, with methodological quality assessed using AMSTAR-2. Findings were categorized by outcome and vaccine platform.
Consensus and conclusions
Evidence consistently shows thrombotic and cerebrovascular events after vaccination are rare. mRNA vaccines (BNT162b2, mRNA-1273) were not associated with increased risk beyond background rates. Adenoviral vector vaccines (ChAdOx1 nCoV-19, Ad26.COV2.S) were linked to rare vaccine-induced immune thrombotic thrombocytopenia, primarily presenting as cerebral venous sinus thrombosis in younger adults. Whole-virus vaccines showed limited evidence without consistent safety concerns.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies limited evidence for whole-virus vaccines and highlights the need for ongoing surveillance to monitor rare thrombotic events, particularly for adenoviral vector vaccines in younger populations.
Research and practice implications
Vaccination benefits outweigh risks, emphasizing the importance of continued surveillance and transparent communication about rare adverse events.

Sharp Increase and Sustained Shift: Evolutionary Patterns of Airway Hyperresponsiveness and Type 2 Inflammatory Markers Following Widespread SARS-CoV-2 Transmission
作者： Cong Liu, Jiayu Zhou, Xining Li, Yingna Li, Chuanchuan Dong, Yanting Dong, Min Huang, Xinrui Tian

Sharp Increase and Sustained Shift: Evolutionary Patterns of Airway Hyperresponsiveness and Type 2 Inflammatory Markers Following Widespread SARS-CoV-2 Transmission

Redesigned Hepatitis C Drug Becomes a Powerful New Treatment for COVID-19 - Department of Energy (.gov)

Redesigned Hepatitis C Drug Becomes a Powerful New Treatment for COVID-19 - Department of Energy (.gov)
新闻简报Scientists at Stanford University have successfully redesigned the hepatitis C drug boceprevir to create a powerful new treatment for COVID-19, effective against drug-resistant variants. The drug targets the main COVID-19 protease (Mpro), an enzyme essential for viral replication that remains consistent across variants. Using X-ray crystal structures, researchers refined the drug atom by atom to improve binding within key enzyme pockets. This breakthrough addresses mutations that render existing treatments like Paxlovid less effective. The research, funded by NIH/NIAID and other institutions, offers a promising solution for combating evolving COVID-19 strains.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 29 日;研究于 2026 年 2 月 19 日宣布。
地点与对象
美国;研究在斯坦福大学进行,得到了包括 NIH/NIAID 在内的多个机构的资助。
事件
Scientists redesigned the hepatitis C drug boceprevir into a potent COVID-19 treatment effective against drug-resistant variants, as announced by the Department of Energy.
规模、影响与风险
The drug targets the main COVID-19 protease (Mpro), remaining intact across variants, and is effective against mutated forms resistant to existing therapies like Paxlovid.
应对、状态与不确定性
研究已经完成,一种重新设计的药物可以与 Mpro 紧密结合;广泛使用可能需要进一步开发和临床试验。

Redesigned Hepatitis C Drug Becomes a Powerful New Treatment for COVID-19 - Department of Energy (.gov)

News Brief
Scientists at Stanford University have successfully redesigned the hepatitis C drug boceprevir to create a powerful new treatment for COVID-19, effective against drug-resistant variants. The drug targets the main COVID-19 protease (Mpro), an enzyme essential for viral replication that remains consistent across variants. Using X-ray crystal structures, researchers refined the drug atom by atom to improve binding within key enzyme pockets. This breakthrough addresses mutations that render existing treatments like Paxlovid less effective. The research, funded by NIH/NIAID and other institutions, offers a promising solution for combating evolving COVID-19 strains.

Careers Internships & Fellowships Apprenticeships & Workforce Development Work at DOE Funding Opportunities Breadcrumb Biological and Environmental Research Redesigned Hepatitis C Drug Becomes a Powerful New Treatment for COVID-19 Redesigned Hepatitis C Drug Becomes a Powerful New Treatment for COVID-19 Scientists transformed an old drug into a potent COVID-19 treatment effective against drug-resistant variants. Biological and Environmental Research February 19, 2026 Estimated Read Time 3 min The final version of the redesigned drug binds tightly to the main COVID-19 protease, an enzyme the virus needs to multiply. The drug’s structure is effective even against mutated forms that resist other treatments. Image courtesy of Stanford University The Science Researchers have redesigned the hepatitis C drug boceprevir to create a more effective treatment for COVID-19. Some virus variants no longer respond well to current therapies, like Paxlovid, due to mutations in the main COVID-19 virus protease (Mpro). This is a key enzyme the virus uses to replicate. Mpro is essential to the virus and remains intact from variant to variant. As such, it is an ideal target for treatments to focus on. Using X-ray crystal structures, scientists refined boceprevir atom by atom. They worked to improve how the drug binds within key pockets of the enzyme. The researchers used the Stanford Synchrotron Radiation Lightsource (a DOE Office of Science User Facility) to analyze the crystal structure when the drug was attached to Mpro. The Impact This research produced a drug that is effective against COVID-19 strains that are resistant to existing treatments. The resulting compound, ML2006a4, binds more tightly and durably than earlier drugs. It also shows superior protection in preclinical animal studies. By redesigning an older hepatitis C drug, scientists made it more effective against mutated forms of the virus’s protease. This could help doctors treat patients who no longer respond to existing therapies. Summary Boceprevir is a protease inhibitor originally developed to treat hepatitis C. Scientists adapted this drug to target the main protease (Mpro) of SARS-CoV-2. This is a critical enzyme the virus uses to process its polyproteins and replicate. Because Mpro is conserved across variants and has no close human equivalent, it remains a prime target for antiviral drug development. However, some SARS-CoV-2 variants contain mutations in Mpro that reduce the effectiveness of existing treatments. Using X-ray crystallography data collected at the Macromolecular Crystallography Beamlines at the Stanford Synchrotron Radiation Lightsource (SSRL), researchers determined high-resolution structures of Mpro bound to various boceprevir analogs. These structural insights guided a stepwise redesign of the molecule to improve its fit within the enzyme’s active site and sub-pockets. The team optimized interactions and reduced internal strains within the drug’s structure to improve binding, potency, and resistance profile. The final compound, ML2006a4, binds covalently to Mpro and retains strong activity even against variants harboring resistance-associated mutations. This structure-guided approach demonstrates how iterative design can yield next-generation antivirals with broad-spectrum activity. It also underscores the vital role of DOE- and NIH-supported synchrotron-based methods in accelerating drug discovery against emerging viral threats. Contact Michael Lin Stanford University mzlin@stanford.edu Funding Funding for this research included: Sarafan ChEM-H and the Innovative Medicines Accelerator, Harrington Scholar-Innovator Award, Emergent Ventures at the Mercatus Center at George Mason University, Stanford-Coulter Translational Research Grant, National Institute of Health(NIH)/National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases (NIAID) Antiviral Drug Discovery (AViDD) Center, the Denver Foundation, the Novo Nordisk Foundation, the Stanford Bio-X Program and a Bio-X Interdisciplinary Graduate Student Fellowship, E&M Foundation Postdoctoral Fellowship, Postdoctoral Fellowship in Translational Medicine from the PhRMA Foundation and an anonymous donation to establish the Stanford BSL3 facility. The research used resources at SSRL, a DOE Office of Science user facility, and of the SSRL Structural Molecular Biology Program supported by the DOE Office of Biological and Environmental Research and by NIH National Institute of General Medical Sciences. Publications Westberg, M., et al. “An orally bioavailable SARS-CoV-2 main protease inhibitor exhibits improved affinity and reduced sensitivity to mutations”, Sci. Transl. Med. 16, eadi0979 (2024). [DOI: 10.1126/scitranslmed.adi0979] Related Links Going atom-by-atom, Stanford researchers craft a new precision drug for fighting COVID-19, Stanford Report

Is there such thing as long term COVID-19? - fr.ekiti-west.mlga.ek.gov.ng

Is there such thing as long term COVID-19? - fr.ekiti-west.mlga.ek.gov.ng
新闻简报Published on July 29, 2026, this article from fr.ekiti-west.mlga.ek.gov.ng examines long-term COVID-19 effects, confirming that even mild infections can lead to persistent symptoms like fatigue, cognitive issues, and multi-organ damage. It addresses viral shedding, noting detectable virus may persist for up to three months without being infectious, and discusses reinfection risks as variants circulate. The article references CDC guidelines, stating most people are no longer contagious after 5 days if fever-free for 3 days, and recommends vaccination even after recovery for added protection. No specific case counts are provided, but it emphasizes that long COVID affects all ages and that PCR tests may remain positive longer than infectiousness.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 29 日。证据中未提及具体事件日期。
地点与对象
United States, with reference to over 249 million Americans who have received at least one COVID-19 vaccine shot.
事件
The article discusses long-term effects of COVID-19 (post-COVID conditions or long COVID), confirming that even mild infections can cause persistent symptoms such as fatigue, shortness of breath, and cognitive issues. It also addresses viral shedding, reinfection risks, and vaccine recommendations.
规模、影响与风险
没有提供具体的病例数或死亡人数。文章指出,长新冠 影响所有年龄段,并可能导致多器官损伤。对于循环变异,再感染可能会变得更加常见,并且病毒脱落可能会在感染后长达三个月内发生,但不一定具有传染性。
应对、状态与不确定性
本文引用了 CDC 关于测试、隔离和 疫苗接种 的指南。它指出,如果 3 天不发烧,大多数人在 5 天后就不再具有传染性。不建议通过血清学检测来诊断再感染。没有提及正在进行的调查状态,来源是一篇信息文章而不是官方通知。

Is there such thing as long term COVID-19? - fr.ekiti-west.mlga.ek.gov.ng

News Brief
Published on July 29, 2026, this article from fr.ekiti-west.mlga.ek.gov.ng examines long-term COVID-19 effects, confirming that even mild infections can lead to persistent symptoms like fatigue, cognitive issues, and multi-organ damage. It addresses viral shedding, noting detectable virus may persist for up to three months without being infectious, and discusses reinfection risks as variants circulate. Original source text (English)
The Daily Insight Home General News Updates Home / news Is there such thing as long term COVID-19? Isabella Little | July 29, 2026 Long-term COVID-19 symptoms can be similar to signs of other disease, so it is important to see your doctor and rule out other problems, such as cardiac issues or lung disease. How long could the COVID-19 virus linger in your body? But for most infected people, virus levels in the body peak between three and six days after the original infection, and the immune system clears the pathogen within 10 days. The virus shed after this period is generally not infectious. What is long COVID-19? Some people who have been infected with the virus that causes COVID-19 can experience long-term effects from their infection, known as post-COVID conditions (PCC) or long COVID. What are some of the potential long-term effects of COVID-19? Known as post-acute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection (PASC), or more commonly as Long COVID, these conditions affect all ages. Long-term effects include fatigue, shortness of breath, difficulty concentrating, sleep disorders, fevers, anxiety and depression. Can you still test positive after recovering from COVID-19? According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, some people who contract COVID-19 can have detectable virus for up to three months, but that doesn't mean they are contagious. When it comes to testing, however, the PCR tests are more likely to continue picking up the virus following infection. Is COVID-19 infectious after 7 days? Most people with COVID-19 are no longer contagious 5 days after they first have symptoms and have been fever-free for at least three days. Should I isolate and get tested if I have recovered from COVID-19 but I have symptoms again? If a previously infected person experiences new symptoms consistent with COVID-19 3 months or more after the date of the previous illness onset (or date of last positive viral diagnostic test [RT-PCR or antigen test] if the person never experienced symptoms), the person should undergo repeat viral diagnostic testing. However, serologic testing should not be used to establish the presence or absence of SARS-COV-2 infection or reinfection. These people who have a positive test result should be considered infectious and remain isolated until they again meet criteria for discontinuation of isolation or of transmission-based precautions. Contact tracing during the person’s second episode of symptoms is warranted. Do people experience long term side effects from the COVID-19 vaccines? Over 249 million Americans have received at least one shot. Doctors and health experts are not concerned about the long-term side effects of the COVID-19 vaccines. And for good reason: In the history of vaccines, none have caused reactions in the long term. The vaccine components do not last long in the body. How many times can I get COVID-19? 'A long-term pattern' According to some infectious disease researchers, Covid-19 reinfections are likely to become more common as time goes on and different variants continue to circulate—with some people potentially seeing third or fourth reinfections within a year. Can you contract COVID-19 through sexual intercourse? Although there is currently no evidence that the COVID-19 virus transmits through semen or vaginal fluids, it has been detected in the semen of people recovering from COVID-19. We would thus recommend avoiding any close contact, especially very intimate contact like unprotected sex, with someone with active COVID-19 to minimize the risk of transmission Can mild cases of COVID-19 cause long COVID-19? Advancing public health But even a mild SARS-CoV-2 infection can cause long COVID. And with the high transmissibility of the Omicron variant—which often results in milder disease outcomes—many patients may be concerned about developing long COVID. Can COVID-19 damage organs? COVID-19 can cause lasting damage to multiple organs, including the lungs, heart, kidneys, liver and brain. SARS CoV-2 first affects the lungs through the nasal passages. When the lungs are severely affected, it can affect the heart. Do COVID-19 long haulers test negative for the virus? The vast majority of long haulers test negative for the virus. There's nothing specific to test for lasting coronavirus symptoms. How does the human body shed COVID-19? Viruses make copies of themselves in human cells and then spread to other people. This is the process of viral shedding. People who are sick with COVID-19 shed the virus from their noses and mouths. While we can't stop viral shedding, we can stop those viruses from infecting other people. How long does COVID-19 rebound last? So far there have been no reports of severe illness in those who have experienced covid rebound, and most people seem to recover and stop testing positive around three days later without needing additional covid-19 treatment. How strong is immunity after a COVID-19 infection? How Strong Is Immunity After a COVID-19 Infection? About 90% of people develop some number of protective antibodies after a COVID-19 infection, according to the CDC. But how high those levels climb appears to be all over the map. Should you get the COVID-19 vaccine if you already had COVID-19 and recovered? If I already had COVID-19 and recovered, do I still need to get a COVID-19 vaccine? You should get a COVID-19 vaccine even if you already had COVID-19. Getting a COVID-19 vaccine after you recover from COVID-19 infection provides added protection to your immune system. Do COVID-19 mRNA vaccines have long-term effects? The Pfizer and Moderna vaccines were created using messenger RNA (or mRNA) technology, which has been used for about 10 years in cancer treatment, with no long-term effects detected. And even before that, scientists had been working with mRNA technology for years. 3. mRNA technology does not alter your DNA. What are some common side effects of the COVID-19 vaccine? According to the CDC, common responses to the vaccine include arm soreness, tiredness, headache, muscle pain, chills and/or fever. What are the possible side effects of the Moderna and Pfizer COVID-19 vaccines? Possible side effects: Pain, redness, or swelling at the site where the shot was administered, and/or tiredness, headache, muscle pain, chills, fever, or nausea throughout the rest of the body. If these side effects occur, they should go away in a few days. A few side effects are serious, but rare. When are COVID-19 patients most infectious? Researchers estimate that people who get infected with the coronavirus can spread it to others 2 to 3 days before symptoms start and are most contagious 1 to 2 days before they feel sick. When do you start being contagious with COVID-19? A person with COVID-19 is considered infectious starting 2 days before they develop symptoms, or 2 days before the date of their positive test if they do not have symptoms. What is the incubation period of the COVID-19 variant? Omicron is now the most dominant strain of coronavirus in the U.S., and its incubation period may be shorter than those of previous variants. Research is just beginning. But some scientists who've studied Omicron and doctors who've treated patients with it suggest the right number might be around 3 days. Can patients who have recovered from COVID-19 continue to have detectable SARS-CoV-2 RNA in upper respiratory specimens? • Patients who have recovered from COVID-19 can continue to have detectable SARS-CoV-2 RNA in upper respiratory specimens for up to 3 months after illness onset in concentrations considerably lower than during illness; however, replication-competent virus has not been reliably recovered and infectiousness is unlikely. What are the most common organs affected by COVID-19? Lungs are the main organs affected by COVID-19; however, the virus can also affect other organs, such as the kidneys, brain, and liver. Lungs are the main organs affected by COVID-19. You Might Also Like Why is it more fun and not funner? Does having big muscles mean you punch harder? Why do the Secret Service wear sunglasses? 新闻简报The WHO COVID-19 dashboard provides a global summary of confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases and deaths, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas. It includes information on case definitions, testing strategies, and reporting practices, which vary by region. The dashboard has been updated since March 2020, with weekly data from July 2023 onwards. Data is subject to continuous verification and may change due to retrospective updates or shifts in reporting standards. WHO acknowledges that case detection, testing, and reporting practices differ globally, which can lead to under or overestimation of true case and death counts. Additionally, some countries have stopped reporting or changed their reporting frequency, which may affect the completeness of the data. The dashboard serves as a tool for monitoring the pandemic's spread and impact, but it is important to note that the data reflects official reports and may not capture all cases or deaths accurately.
查看新闻五要素
时间
从2019年12月31日至2020年3月21日,以及自2020年3月22日起,从2023年7月起每周更新(N14、N15、N21)。
地点与对象
全球,数据收集自国家、地区和地区,包括高收入、低收入、中低收入和中上收入群体(N1、N16)。
事件
WHO monitors and reports on the global epidemiological situation of SARS-CoV-2 through the WHO COVID-19 dashboard, including confirmed cases and deaths (N1, N4, N10).
规模、影响与风险
该仪表板包括向世卫组织报告的确诊病例和死亡病例,数据需要经过验证,并且由于回顾性更新或报告做法的变化而可能发生变化(N1、N8、N18)。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织收集和传播其会员国报告的数据,可能存在重大数据错误和更正,并承认各地区在病例发现、检测策略和报告做法方面存在差异(N13、N9、N18)。
A high priorityPublic-health news

COVID-19 dashboard

News Brief
The WHO COVID-19 dashboard provides a global summary of confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases and deaths, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas. It includes information on case definitions, testing strategies, and reporting practices, which vary by region. The dashboard has been updated since March 2020, with weekly data from July 2023 onwards. Data is subject to continuous verification and may change due to retrospective updates or shifts in reporting standards. WHO acknowledges that case detection, testing, and reporting practices differ globally, which can lead to under or overestimation of true case and death counts. Additionally, some countries have stopped reporting or changed their reporting frequency, which may affect the completeness of the data. The dashboard serves as a tool for monitoring the pandemic's spread and impact, but it is important to note that the data reflects official reports and may not capture all cases or deaths accurately.

epidemiological situation via the WHO COVID-19 dashboard. If data for certain countries is unavailable in this section, it may indicate that they have either ceased reporting COVID-19 surveillance data to WHO or have integrated the COVID-19 surveillance into existing respiratory disease surveillance. In the latter case, SARS-CoV-2 detections from sentinel and systematic virological surveillance sites in those countries may be found in the Circulation section which also includes information on SARS-CoV-2 variant circulation. This global summary of COVID-19 cases includes data on confirmed cases reported to WHO from the comprehensive COVID-19 case monitoring. Coronavirus (COVID-19) cases, %%COUNTRY%% data Source: World Health Organization Loading... Loading... Loading... What is the definition of 'confirmed' and 'reported' in COVID-19 data? All data represents the date of reporting as opposed to date of symptom onset. All data is subject to continuous verification and may change based on retrospective updates to accurately reflect trends, changes in country case definitions and/or reporting practices. Significant data errors detected or reported to WHO may be corrected at more frequent intervals with some countries performing retrospective bulk corrections may lead to the appearance of significant spikes or negative values which are verified and validated by WHO. In the context of WHO's statistical reporting of COVID-19 data, it is important to note that only confirmed cases are included in case and death counts. In guidance updated on 22 July 2023; there are two alternative definitions for 'Confirmed case of SARS-CoV-2 infection' in international surveillance reporting, although some departures may exist due to local adaptations: a) A person with a positive Nucleic Acid Amplification Test (NAAT), regardless of clinical criteria OR epidemiological criteria. b) A person meeting clinical criteria AND/OR epidemiological criteria (suspect case A) with a positive professional-use or self-test SARS-CoV-2 Antigen-RDT. Additionally, WHO only disseminates data as reported by its Member States. From the 31 December 2019 to the 21 March 2020, WHO collected the numbers of confirmed COVID-19 cases and deaths through official communications under the International Health Regulations (IHR, 2005), complemented by monitoring the official ministries of health websites and social media accounts. Since 22 March 2020, global data has been compiled through WHO region-specific dashboards, and/or aggregate count data reported directly to WHO headquarters by Member States. Statistical counts include both domestic and repatriated cases. Case detection, definitions, testing strategies, reporting practice, and lag times (e.g. time to case notification, and time to reporting of deaths) differ between countries, territories and areas. These factors, amongst others, influence the counts presented with variable under or overestimation of true case and death counts, and variable delays to reflecting these statistics at a global level. Please note that statistics do not necessarily reflect the actual number of cases and deaths or the actual number of countries where cases and deaths are occurring, as a number of countries have stopped reporting or changed their frequency of reporting. Trends in COVID-19 cases, %%COUNTRY%% Recent COVID-19 cases reported to WHO (weekly) %%COUNTRY%%, July 2023 - present Source: World Health Organization Data may be incomplete for the latest week. Total COVID-19 cases reported to WHO (weekly) %%COUNTRY%%, January 2020 - present Source: World Health Organization Why is COVID-19 data being presented as weekly statistics? A number of countries have stopped reporting or changed their frequency of reporting COVID-19 case and death counts to WHO. An outcome of these differences in reporting is that WHO may receive daily data from some countries, while other countries may only report data to WHO once every 14 days. In addition, countries differ in how they choose to report statistics; some countries provide their data attributed to specific dates while others who report less frequently may group data from 7 days into a single statistic attributed to a week in their reporting. As of 25 August 2023, WHO declared that it is no longer necessary for Member States to report daily counts of cases and deaths to WHO and requested strengthening of weekly reporting. Reported data is still available attributed to specific dates (daily data) here as a download. This dashboard presents the same statistics as weekly figures in charts in order to mitigate against the visual misinterpretation of data. Should daily data have been presented here, many countries would show zero counts for multiple consecutive days due in part to the differences in how they choose to report. While weekly intervals do not completely mitigate against this, the approach reduces the risk that some dashboard users might infer zero cases or deaths when lack of data is often due to reporting differences. What do negative counts of cases or deaths mean? All data is subject to continuous verification and may change based on retrospective updates to accurately reflect trends, changes in country case definitions and/or reporting practices. Significant data errors detected or reported to WHO may be corrected by Member States at more frequent intervals with some countries performing retrospective bulk corrections in their reporting. Bulk corrections may lead to the appearance of significant spikes or negative values which are verified and validated by WHO. Last 28 days ‘Last 28 days’ refers to the four preceding most recently complete epidemiological weeks. WHO Regions The World Health Organization (WHO) is divided into six regions with regional offices, each responsible for addressing health challenges and promoting public health within its designated geographical area. These regions serve as administrative units that allow the WHO to tailor its efforts to the specific needs, priorities, and health conditions of member countries within each region. World Bank Income Groups The World Bank classifies economies into income groups based on their Gross National Income (GNI) per capita. These annually updated classifications help the World Bank and other organizations analyze and understand global economic trends, allocate resources, and design development policies. Data sources COVID-19 case data From the 31 December 2019 to the 21 March 2020, WHO collected the numbers of confirmed COVID-19 cases and deaths through official communications under the International Health Regulations (IHR, 2005), complemented by monitoring the official ministries of health websites and social media accounts. Since 22 March 2020, global data is compiled through WHO region-specific dashboards, and/or aggregate count data reported to WHO headquarters. WHO COVID-19 Dashboard is updated every Tuesday for the period of two full weeks prior. Counts primarily reflect laboratory-confirmed cases and deaths, based upon WHO case definitions; although some departures may exist due to local adaptations. Counts include both domestic and repatriated cases. Case detection, definitions, testing strategies, reporting practice, and lag times (e.g. time to case notification, and time to reporting of deaths) differ between countries, territories and areas. These factors, amongst others, influence the counts presented with variable under or overestimation of true case and death counts, and variable delays to reflecting these data at a global level. COVID-19 Delta variant | virus variant - Encyclopedia Britannica

COVID-19 Delta variant | virus variant - Encyclopedia Britannica
新闻简报Delta 变种是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的一种高度传播的遗传变种,于 2021 年中期出现并迅速在全球传播,导致严重疾病,特别是在未接种疫苗的个体中。澳大利亚、加拿大和印度等国家报告医疗系统不堪重负,感染病例增加,包括疫苗接种人群中出现突破性病例。各国政府因 疫苗 推出缓慢和应对不充分而面临批评,导致战略转向以 疫苗 为重点的预防工作。该变种的高传播性凸显了大流行应对措施的脆弱性,引发了关于强制措施与自愿措施的争论。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 28 日;事件发生在 2021 年中期。
地点与对象
全球,特别提到澳大利亚、加拿大和印度。
事件
Delta 变种 (严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 B.1.617.2) 是一种高度传播的遗传变种,于 2021 年中期广泛传播,导致严重疾病,特别是在未接种疫苗的个体中。
规模、影响与风险
导致感染增加,包括在接种疫苗的个体中;引起严重疾病,特别是在未接种疫苗的人群中;印度的医疗系统不堪重负。
应对、状态与不确定性
各国政府将战略重点转向 疫苗接种;因推出缓慢和反应不充分而受到批评;AI 生成的摘要并建议进行验证。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

COVID-19 Delta variant | virus variant - Encyclopedia Britannica

News Brief
The Delta variant, a highly transmissible genetic variant of SARS-CoV-2, emerged in mid-2021 and quickly spread globally, causing significant illness, especially among unvaccinated individuals. Countries like Australia, Canada, and India reported overwhelmed healthcare systems and increased infections, including breakthrough cases in vaccinated populations. Governments faced criticism for slow vaccine rollouts and inadequate responses, leading to strategic shifts toward vaccination-focused prevention efforts. The variant's high transmissibility highlighted vulnerabilities in pandemic responses, prompting debates on mandatory versus voluntary measures.

COVID-19 was first detected in 2019 in Wuhan, China. A large proportion of infections in China were undocumented before travel restrictions and other control measures were implemented in late January 2020. As a result, COVID-19 very quickly spread to countries worldwide, giving rise to a multiyear pandemic that resulted in millions of deaths. The pandemic also spurred a revolution in business and education, spawning a reliance on distance learning and work-at-home arrangements and the rise of videoconferencing platforms such as Zoom, which became one of the most downloaded applications worldwide and a household word. Read more about the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic. COVID-19 is caused by a coronavirus known as severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2). The disease is transmitted primarily through contact with infectious material, particularly respiratory droplets that enter the environment when an infected person sneezes or coughs. Individuals nearby may inhale or come into contact with these droplets, resulting in disease transmission. Infection may also occur when a person comes into contact with a contaminated surface and then touches his or her mouth, nose, or eyes. Individuals at greatest risk of COVID-19 infection include older adults and persons with chronic illness, largely because of weakened immune function. Britannica Quiz A Visit with the Word Doctor: Medical Vocabulary Quiz COVID-19 is characterized by a variety of symptoms, including fever, cough, congestion, fatigue, shortness of breath, headache, sore throat, nausea or vomiting, loss of smell or taste, and body aches. COVID-19 may progress to severe respiratory illness, with symptoms of chest pain and extreme difficulty in breathing, requiring hospitalization. Some COVID-19 patients who are hospitalized further develop neurological symptoms, including severe fatigue and altered consciousness. Delirium has been observed in many of these patients as well, possibly as a side effect of medication. Delirium and lingering psychological issues, including depression and anxiety, can prolong and complicate recovery. COVID-19 vaccineDoctor holding a syringe and bottle with COVID-19 vaccine.(more) There is no cure for COVID-19. However, different types of drugs have been used to treat infection and to reduce the severity of the disease. Examples include antiviral drugs, such as remdesivir, molnupiravir, and combined ritonavir and nirmatrelvir; a drug used for pancreatic inflammation called camostat mesilate; and various therapeutic monoclonal antibodies, such as REGEN-COV (casirivimab and imdevimab). COVID-19 vaccines, which became available in late 2020, are highly effective in protecting against severe illness and in limiting the spread of the disease; immunity can be further bolstered through subsequent booster doses of vaccine. Risk of disease transmission can be reduced by measures such as social distancing, disinfection of surfaces, and universal community use of face masks. Self-isolation and self-quarantine are other ways in which the spread of COVID-19 can be stopped. Survivors of severe COVID-19, particularly those who were hospitalized, are likely to suffer long-term effects. Individuals who required mechanical ventilation might never fully recover; ventilator use is associated with severe muscle atrophy and weakness, which significantly impact survival and quality of life. In full: coronavirus disease 2019 (Show more) Key People: Anthony Fauci (Show more) Related Topics: The Top COVID-19 Vaccine Myths Spreading Online Questions About COVID-19 Answered What was the impact of COVID-19? COVID-19 in 20 Questions Is It Safe to Reopen Schools During the COVID-19 Pandemic? (Show more) See all related content How We Develop New VaccinesWorried about diphtheria? No? Thank vaccines.(more) See all videos for this article The ongoing spread of COVID-19 has been fueled by the emergence of variants in evolving lineages of SARS-CoV-2. Such variants generally carry mutations that strengthen characteristics such as the virus’s ability to infect individuals (including individuals who are vaccinated and who previously had COVID-19), to cause severe disease, and to potentially escape certain treatments. There are numerous variants, the most notable of which is Omicron, which was first detected in Botswana in November 2021 and quickly became the primary circulating strain globally. Omicron spawned multiple sublineages of concern, including BA.4 and BA.5, which are highly infectious, and BA.2, which gave rise to XBB.1.5—a highly transmissible variant, noted for its heightened ability to bind to cells and to replicate. COVID-19 - Encyclopedia Britannica

COVID-19 - Encyclopedia Britannica
新闻简报COVID-19, a highly contagious respiratory illness caused by SARS-CoV-2, was first detected in Wuhan, China in 2019 and quickly spread worldwide, resulting in a multiyear pandemic with millions of deaths. The disease primarily spreads through respiratory droplets and affects older adults and those with chronic illnesses more severely. Symptoms range from fever and cough to severe respiratory distress, with some patients experiencing neurological complications and long-term effects. While there is no cure, treatments include antiviral drugs and monoclonal antibodies, and vaccines have been highly effective in reducing severe illness. Prevention measures like social distancing, mask use, and self-isolation have helped control transmission, though the pandemic's impact has extended beyond health to transform business, education, and daily life globally.
查看新闻五要素
时间
Published on July 29, 2026. COVID-19 was first detected in 2019 in Wuhan, China.
地点与对象
首次在中国武汉发现。该疾病影响了全世界的人口,其中老年人和患有慢性病的人面临的风险最大。
事件
COVID-19 is a highly contagious respiratory illness caused by the coronavirus SARS-CoV-2. It is characterized by symptoms such as fever, cough, fatigue, and shortness of breath, and can progress to severe respiratory illness requiring hospitalization.
规模、影响与风险
The COVID-19 pandemic resulted in millions of deaths worldwide. It caused significant health-system effects, including hospitalizations and long-term effects for survivors, particularly those requiring mechanical ventilation. The pandemic also spurred a revolution in business and education.
应对、状态与不确定性
There is no cure for COVID-19, but treatments include antiviral drugs and monoclonal antibodies. COVID-19 vaccines, available since late 2020, are highly effective in protecting against severe illness. Prevention measures include social distancing, face masks, and self-isolation. The ongoing spread has been fueled by emerging variants of SARS-CoV-2.

News Brief
COVID-19, a highly contagious respiratory illness caused by SARS-CoV-2, was first detected in Wuhan, China in 2019 and quickly spread worldwide, resulting in a multiyear pandemic with millions of deaths. The disease primarily spreads through respiratory droplets and affects older adults and those with chronic illnesses more severely. Symptoms range from fever and cough to severe respiratory distress, with some patients experiencing neurological complications and long-term effects.
